# Decisions made during implementation

One line per decision the design did not fix. Format: `- [package] decision — reason`. Newest at the bottom of each milestone section.

## B0 Bootstrap

- [B0] Run mode: the unattended prompt in docs/PROMPT.md is authoritative; the owner pasted the older pausing version on 11 Nov 2026 after asking for an overnight run, so the run does not stop between milestones — the owner's latest instruction was an unattended run.
- [B0] TypeScript 6.0 rather than 7.0 — typescript-eslint supports < 6.1 only.
- [B0] Added `src/util/` as the lowest shared layer (flags, seeded PRNG for tests, debug-hook registration) — every module needs these and none of the planned folders may be imported by all.
- [B0] Debug-hook registration (`registerDebug`) lives in `util/debug.ts`; modules register the part they own, and `debug/` holds the full type and app assembly — avoids every module depending on `debug/`.
- [B0] `Transport` gained `readonly selfId: PeerId` — every implementation needs to name itself; Trystero exposes `selfId` too.
- [B0] e2e runs against a test-mode production build (`vite build --mode test` into `dist-test/`, then `vite preview`); `__SPELLPLANE_DEBUG__` is true in every mode except `production`.
- [B0] Broadcast transport filters addressed (`to`) frames at the receiver, since BroadcastChannel reaches every page; leak tests on this harness check what is delivered to the application.
- [B0] Extra harness flag `?speech=fake` selects `voice/fake.ts`, driven by tests through the debug hook.
- [B0] Extra dev dependencies: `eslint-import-resolver-typescript` (boundaries rule resolves TS paths), `@eslint/js`, `globals`, `svelte-eslint-parser`, `@types/node`, `workbox-window` (peer of vite-plugin-pwa).
- [B0] Fixtures: full M19 + tokens from Scryfall, ~24-card samples of the other nine target sets, MTGJSON booster data and a trimmed uuid map for all ten; recorded by `scripts/record-fixtures.mjs`.
- [B0] Deploy copies `index.html` to `404.html` so deep links survive on GitHub Pages (routing is hash-based anyway).
- [B0] RESOLVED 11 Nov 2026: the owner made the repository public; Pages enabled (build_type=workflow) and live at https://izzardry.github.io/spellplane/. Original note: BLOCKER (owner): izzardry/spellplane is private and the org plan has no Pages for private repos (HTTP 422). The run did not make the repository public, since that exposure cannot be undone; the deploy workflow skips while the repo is private. To go live: make the repo public, then `gh api -X POST repos/izzardry/spellplane/pages -f build_type=workflow` and re-run "Deploy to GitHub Pages". Gates verify the production build locally with `vite preview` instead.

## M0 Solo table

- [M0] Added package M0-P7 Solo session (`src/session/`, `main.ts`, `debug/`), second wave after M0-P1 and M0-P5 — the plan had no owner for the client that glues reducer, secret store, private effects, undo, persistence and app wiring. M0-P5's reload acceptance test moves to M0-P7.
- [M0] New lint element `session` (may import core, net, store, data, draft, games, voice, util; imported by ui and debug).
- [M0] Package agents may make additive-only changes to contract files (new optional fields or new exports) when unavoidable and must report them; they never change or remove existing contract members — keeps parallel packages unblocked.
- [M0] Module test pages: `?harness=<name>` in dev/test builds loads `src/<module>/harness.ts` instead of the app, so scene and HUD can be tested before the session exists.
- [M0-P5] The peer id hashes the SPKI (DER) export of the public key: the design gives no format, and SPKI is the standard Web Crypto export a peer can import. `exportPublicKey` and `peerIdOf` are the single source for M1's hello.
- [M0-P5] A new identity has an empty nickname (the UI asks for one). Its preferred seat colour (0..7) comes from the peer id, so two new browsers rarely default to the same colour.
- [M0-P5] Nicknames are trimmed, inner whitespace collapsed and capped at 32 characters. A seat colour outside the integers 0..7 throws RangeError: themes have eight seat colours.
- [M0-P5] When two tabs open at once, only one identity is created: the key pair is generated outside the transaction and inserted with `add`, and the tab that loses re-reads the winner's record.
- [M0-P5] `saveAction` with secrets = null keeps the stored secret store. Log or table records whose roomId differs from the argument are rejected with TypeError before anything is written.
- [M0-P5] Saving a snapshot never deletes old log entries, because sync may still need the tail. `loadTable` returns the entries after `snapshotSeq`, read in one readonly transaction.
- [M0-P5] `listTables` and `decks.list` return the most recently updated first.
- [M0-P5] The service worker uses vite-plugin-pwa injectManifest with no precache or web app manifest, and is built and registered only in production builds, because the task says production only. Test and e2e builds have no worker.
- [M0-P5] The image cache stores only status-200, non-opaque responses: opaque ones can't be used as CORS/WebGL textures and inflate quota use. It keeps at most 2000 entries and evicts the oldest.
- [M0-P5] Only GET requests to https://cards.scryfall.io go through the worker. The cache name is `spellplane-images[-<profile>]`, taken from the requesting page's URL (the client id).
- [M0-P5] `requestPersistentStorage()` skips the browser prompt when storage is already persistent, and resolves false when the API is missing or fails.
- [M0-P6] Stack lane depth is clamped to the centre band: 200 × 220 on the 1v1 and Two-Headed Giant tables, 200 × 320 elsewhere — the spec's 320-deep lane is deeper than the 220 band and would cover both battlefields, which "The effect stack" forbids; the cascade scrolls or collapses to its count badge once it outgrows the lane.
- [M0-P6] The hand row of backs that others see (420 × 88) sits at the seat, 6 outside the territory's near edge, to the owner's right of the avatar — inside the territory it would cover the free-placement battlefield where lands usually go.
- [M0-P6] Avatar centre 40 outside the territory's near edge (footprint radius 34), so on every current table it lies just off the table edge; the scene's fit should include this seat strip (up to 94 beyond the edge with the hand row).
- [M0-P6] Within-territory margins: 12 from the territory edges to the pile column and the command area; command and emblem area 150 × 112 in the near-left corner (two cards side by side).
- [M0-P6] Default resolve destination uses the instance's current face (faceIndex of the '//'-split type line, front face as fallback); without card data a spell defaults to its controller's battlefield — a wrong guess costs one drag.
- [M0-P6] Three-, four-player and Two-Headed Giant layouts are provisional until M3-P1 (follow the spec table, untuned). The spec's four-player territories (1100 × 440 on an 1800 table) overlap 90 × 90 at each corner although the design calls the corners unused; kept as specified for M3-P1 to settle (920 wide would remove the overlap).
- [M0-P6] Draft pack zone is seat-scoped and owner-visible; Rochester's table-scoped public pack is left to the draft package (M2, TODO in games/mtg/zones.ts).
- [M0-P6] Exile is unordered (ordered=false) — the design gives it no order, unlike the graveyard's ordered fan.
- [M0-P6] Solo goldfish uses the '1v1' format and layout with seat 0 only; soloLayout() is exported — Format has no solo value, and the contract layout(format) has no seat-count parameter
- [M0-P6] Provisional ffa3/ffa4/2hg layouts are marked by an exported isProvisionalLayout()/PROVISIONAL_FORMATS rather than a new TableLayout field — clearly marked without a contract change
- [M0-P6] Inside a territory (owner's orientation): pile column at the right edge (library nearest the owner, card edges 12 apart); command area in the near-left corner — the spec gives only the relative placement
- [M0-P6] All anchors rest at rot = seat angle; stackLane is canonical rot 0, and stackLaneForViewer() rotates it so it cascades toward the viewer
- [M0-P6] Two-Headed Giant provisional seat order: seats 0 and 1 on the near edge (team 1), seats 2 and 3 across; each seat on its team's left in its own orientation; ffa3 seat 1 top-left, seat 2 top-right — clockwise order
- [M0-P6] defaultDestination reads the front face of the type line, before // and the subtype dash. Any permanent type sends the spell to the controller's battlefield, otherwise instant or sorcery sends it to the owner's graveyard. With no instance given, the owner falls back to the controller. With no card data, the spell defaults to the controller's battlefield. A spell item with no card returns null
- [M0-P6] Exile is unordered and drawn as squared; graveyard and library are ordered and squared — the design gives exile no order
- [M0-P6] The card back keeps the stub's texture id 'mtg-back' at 488 x 680. It fills the whole texture first, with square corners, so the scene's card mask does the rounding
- [M0-P2] Rate limit is a sliding window: a request starts only if fewer than 2 started in the last 1000 ms, in order — reads '2 requests/s' as 'never 3 in any second'
- [M0-P2] A 429 blocks all requests for 30 s from when it arrives, then the request is retried, at most 3 attempts — the design gives the back-off but no retry policy
- [M0-P2] Decklist lines without a count are syntax errors, and counts must be 1–999 — the grammar requires <count>
- [M0-P2] Headers accept any case, a trailing colon or a '(n)' count; an MTGA 'About' block is skipped — common export variants
- [M0-P2] '(SET)' with no collector number pins the set only (a {name, set} identifier); set codes are 2–6 alphanumerics so '(Showcase)' stays part of the name
- [M0-P2] A set or collector number Scryfall does not know falls back silently to the name alone — the printing chooser can correct it
- [M0-P2] Near matches come from Scryfall's fuzzy lookup (first 10 unresolved names only, because of the rate limit) plus every card name already known locally, ranked by edit distance (at most max(2, len/3)), top 3
- [M0-P2] ResolvedDeck gained optional commander and companion fields — the grammar has these headers and the cards belong in neither main nor side
- [M0-P2] Cards where every face has its own image_uris (transform, modal_dfc) give one image per face; split, adventure and flip cards give their single image; face 1 of a single-faced card falls back to the front; an unknown card or one with no image gets a placeholder face
- [M0-P2] Single-id lookups made at the same time are combined into one /cards/collection request, so a board of cards does not queue one rate-limited request per card
- [M0-P2] Cards older than 30 days are served from the cache at once and refreshed in the background; refreshStale() refreshes them all for the session to call at start-up; cache errors fall back to fetching
- [M0-P2] A pinned printing (set+number, or name+set) is used only if its name, a face name or a ' // ' part equals the line's name after normalisation. Otherwise the line silently falls back to a name-only lookup, and if that fails too it is reported as unresolved. Reason: MTGA set codes and numbers can differ from Scryfall's, and a wrong card must never enter the deck without notice.
- [M0-P2] A blank line after a Commander or Companion block that has a card returns to main, or goes to side if main already has cards. A blank line straight after the header is ignored. Reason: the simplest reading of 'a blank line after the main deck also starts the sideboard'.
- [M0-P2] The limiter runs on performance.now(), and a 429 retry goes to the front of the queue (it keeps its place), so FIFO order holds for first attempts. Reason: clock jumps must not stall Scryfall access, and a retried request should not wait behind later ones.
- [M0-P2] CONTRACT CHANGE, flagged for the integrator: src/data/types.ts gained two additive optional fields. DeckEntry.text holds the source line as pasted, for unresolved-error text. CardFaceData.oracle_id, layout and cmc hold the per-face fields of reversible_card. Reason: the reviewer's suggested fixes. These follow the earlier additive M0-P2 change to ResolvedDeck. CLAUDE.md says contract files are edited only in a contracts package, so the integrator may want to move these into a contracts package. Both are recorded in docs/DECISIONS.md.
- [M0-P2] pnpm e2e was not run in this round. The changes are in data/ only and are covered by unit tests; pnpm check is green with 100 tests.
- [M0-P2] Id lookups are batched before the first await: one cache read, then /cards/collection in batches of 75 for the rest, per tick. If a request fails, its callers and the rest of that tick's batch are rejected, so no further requests go out right after a 429 give-up or a 5xx.
- [M0-P2] Ids Scryfall does not return are remembered as missing for 10 minutes, for the session only; refreshStale() forgets them — the simplest bound that both protects the 2/s budget and lets a card that appears later be found.
- [M0-P2] Refreshes send one 75-id request at a time and remember each before the next; refreshStale() skips ids a background refresh is already fetching.
- [M0-P2] FaceSource.faceFor rejects when the lookup fails and returns the 'Unknown card' placeholder only when Scryfall definitely has no such card — the design says nothing on this, and a placeholder would look permanent.
- [M0-P2] A 200 response whose body is not JSON becomes ScryfallError('Scryfall: invalid JSON'); a decklist count over 999, including one too large to be exact, is reported as 'at most 999'.
- [M0-P3] Tap state: the state carries the `tapped` flag only (as the M0-P1 reducer does); `pose.rot` stays the untapped rotation. The scene draws a tapped battlefield card turned a further 90° (a card of a seat at θ is drawn at θ + 90), animates the flag change with the 120 ms tap motion, and strips the turn back out of every `move`/`moveTemp` pose it dispatches.
- [M0-P3] Drag on empty table pans (as the navigation table says); Shift+drag on empty table draws the marquee (adds to the selection). Click selects one card, Shift+click toggles, click on empty table clears.
- [M0-P3] Drop resolution details: rules 2 and 3 apply to single cards only (a dragged group or pile only aligns or lands free); rule 2 targets loose cards and the top of squared piles, rule 3 loose cards and the last card of a cascade; "lower third" and "toward the owner" are in the target controller's seat frame; "overlapping by at least half" means half the dropped card's width; rule 4 also aligns the other axis to the neighbour's matching edge or centre when within 12 mm, so rows line up; every result is clamped to the table.
- [M0-P3] A squared pile has no reachable bottom card, so dragging its count badge (top-left corner) moves the whole pile; dragging the top card pulls it out singly.
- [M0-P3] Rule 1 avatar drop means the seat's hand (`toZone … position 'any'`); library drops go on top; the stack lane pushes a spell (`stackPush`). Library cards are not draggable from the scene (drawing is a private effect). Double-click on a zone pile reports `pileOpen(<zone id>)`; SceneEvents has no draw event, so for `library:<seat>` the HUD must treat it as draw (DESIGN: double-click draws from a library) — reported to the integrator as a needed SceneEvents addition (`draw(zone)`), scene/types.ts is a contract file.
- [M0-P3] Mouse wheel vs trackpad: ctrl+wheel (pinch) and line-mode or whole-notch wheel events (deltaX 0, integer |deltaY| ≥ 50) zoom; other wheel events pan.
- [M0-P3] Zone outlines and labels are drawn by the scene from the layout in the theme's border/muted colours (the Theme v1 SceneContext carries no layout); the theme builds the table surface and background only. Paper's surround is #f1f1ee so the white table reads as a sheet.
- [M0-P3] SDF text uses a bundled font (src/scene/fonts/Roboto-Medium.ttf, Apache-2.0, licence alongside) so troika never fetches a font from a CDN; table text is limited to Latin characters (others become '?') so troika's unicode fallback resolver is never called.
- [M0-P3] Texture tiers by on-screen card height in CSS px; one texture array per tier (atlas pages as layers) so all cards are one draw call; the 400 MB cap counts decoded level-0 bytes (frameStats.textureBytes); while a texture is missing the next smaller resident tier is drawn, else a blank card.
- [M0-P3] The scene registers cards/camera/lines/frameStats on the debug hook itself; `cards()` reports displayed (animated) poses and screen rects from the real camera projection, plus extra fields (zone, shows, dimmed, tier, moving…). `camera().position` is [centre x, centre y, height] in table mm, pitch 90.
- [M0-P3] The scene harness uses its own synthetic 1v1 layout, game definition, faces and reducer stub (src/scene/harness-fixture.ts), not mtg/core, so scene tests do not depend on M0-P1/M0-P6 internals.
- [M0-P3] B0 stage.ts and App.svelte are left as they are (the B0 smoke still passes); M0-P4/M0-P7 replace the stage with createTableScene when they wire the app.
- [M0-P3] Double-click on a battlefield pile: a squared pile (only its top card is visible) opens; in a cascade every member is visible, so double-click taps the member and only the bottom card opens the pile.
- [M0-P3] Face textures ask the FaceSource for the tier being loaded (small/normal/large images); placeholder drawings are tier-independent and shared between tiers.
- [M0-P3] A dropped card waiting for its confirmation keeps its predicted pose through unrelated state updates until the confirmation or the 1.5 s timeout.
- [M0-P3] Zoom over the surround near the table edge pivots on the nearest table point when the cursor pivot would push the view centre off the table.
- [M0-P3] Paper's `border` token is #8c8c8c (3.4:1 on white) so zone hairlines meet the 3:1 non-text contrast; there is no separate table-marking token in Theme v1.
- [M0-P3] Connection lines are a minimal placeholder in M0 (solid, one width, no arrowheads or pulse); dash patterns, arrowheads and Paper's thickness pulse belong to M1-P4 (Combat and lines, owns scene/lines.ts).
- [M0-P3] Scene harness gained faceRequests(), hold(on) and dispose() test hooks — needed to test per-tier loading, asynchronous confirmation and disposal
- [M0-P1] Contract (additive): optional `ActionBase.restore` and exported `RestorePatch` in core/actions.ts — a single opposite action cannot bring back lossy effects (turn number, target lines removed with a resolved item, an identity dropped in a hidden zone, a pile re-laid around a new base); `inverse()` attaches the exact prior public records as a diff, the reducer applies it only on undoable types and refuses a patch that would break the invariants.
- [M0-P1] Poses are stored quantised to 0.01 mm / 0.01° with `rot` in [0, 360) — attachments follow their host by translation, and quantising makes a move and its undo land on identical doubles, so undo restores the exact hash.
- [M0-P1] Piles exist while a card references them; a one-card pile is valid (no auto-dissolve) — card actions name one card, so building a pile from two loose cards passes through a one-card pile. Members are laid out from the base: cascade 14 mm along the base card's own "toward the owner" axis, squared on the base pose. When the base leaves, the next card takes the old anchor; joining at index 0 with a pose moves the pile.
- [M0-P1] `move` within a zone: the base of a pile (pile field absent) drags the whole pile; any other member given a new pose pulls out singly; `pile: null` leaves explicitly. Moving to another zone leaves the pile and detaches both ways. A moved card takes the top of its zone's draw order unless `index` is given.
- [M0-P1] Attachments: both cards in one public zone; the k-th attachment is tucked at k × (10 right, 10 up) in the host's frame; attachments follow host translations (not rotations); zone draw order is unchanged (the scene draws attachments behind hosts); attachment lines are derived from `attachedTo`, not stored as Line records.
- [M0-P1] Entering any hidden zone resets a card to a canonical record (no identity, face down, untapped, no counters, note, pile, attachments or home, pose 0/0/0, controller = owner) and removes lines touching it; hidden zones take only their owner's cards and never tokens. Leaving a hidden zone for a public one: face up only with a public identity (or a token label), else face down.
- [M0-P1] `flip` down keeps the already public identity (`hide` drops it); `flip` up needs a public identity, `ref`+`salt`, or a token label. `reveal` on an identified card replaces the identity (verification is M1-P2's).
- [M0-P1] Card and player counters clamp at 0 and drop the key; life is not clamped.
- [M0-P1] Tokens: owner = controller = `controller` or the zone's seat; `copy` creates the token in the original's zone under its controller with identity, face index and label but no counters; `remove` removes tokens and table objects only, never from the stack, and clears marker sources, lines, attachments and pile places pointing at it.
- [M0-P1] Stack: every push goes on top (copy markers too); only the top item resolves or is countered; leaving removes lines touching the item and target lines drawn from its spell card. The reducer has no card data, so clients pass `to` from `GameDefinition.defaultDestination`; without it a resolving spell goes to its controller's battlefield, a countered one to its owner's graveyard. Moving a spell card off the stack removes its item; copies of a departed item lose `copyOf`.
- [M0-P1] `turnPass` raises the turn number, sets the active seat or team and clears every auto-pass, but keeps the phase — the reducer holds no phase list; the HUD dispatches `phaseSet` to the first phase in the same group.
- [M0-P1] The reducer checks structure, not roles (actor owns the hidden zone, spectators cannot move cards) — that is the sequencer's structural check (M1-P1).
- [M0-P1] `seq`: an applied action carrying `seq` stamps `state.seq`; a refused one returns the same object. Public records with no shared effect (ping, chat, caption, lookAt, revealTo, reorder, snapshotHash, sequencerClaim) change nothing but `seq`. `inverse()` returns null for an action that changed nothing.
- [M0-P1] `createTable` starts at stage 'lobby' and adds a public table zone 'stack' if the game definition lacks one; `seatLeave` clears name, peer and ready; `seatTake` refuses a seat held by another peer.
- [M0-P1] Not applied yet, refused with the owning package in the reason: ask/respond/autoPass (M1-P3), shuffleCommit/Nonce/Done and rngCommit/Reveal (M1-P2), draft actions (M2).
- [M0-P1] Contract (additive, amends M0-P1's own unmerged `RestorePatch`): the patch is compare-and-set — `RestoreStep {expect, put}` per field or record, new exports `RestoreStep` and `RestorableCardField` — the earlier whole-record patch let any undo rewrite any part of the table (zone visibility, seats, turn marker) and silently reverted other seats' later changes (DESIGN Undo: "There is no table-wide rewind"). It now reaches only fields `controller identity face faceIndex tapped pose home combat pile attachedTo counters note tokenLabel` of existing cards in public zones, the draw order of public zones, lines, the stack, the turn number and seat `autoPass`; every put value is validated like an action field; a step whose `expect` no longer matches refuses the undo ("undo is stale: …"); invariants are checked afterwards. A restore patch is accepted only on the inverse inside `undo`. An identity put back on a public card is structurally no stronger than `reveal`; checking it against the commitment is M1-P2's, as for reveals.
- [M0-P1] An undo that returns a card to a hidden zone drops its identity there, even if it was revealed in that zone before (DESIGN "Table model": peers drop the identity when a public card returns to a hidden zone); undo restores the previous hash except for that identity, which the owner can reveal again.
- [M0-P1] moveTemp/settle implemented in `core/reducer/temp.ts` (the M0 gate needs "attack and settle"; M1-P4 extends it): moveTemp only on a battlefield, sets `home` once, pulls a pile member out of its pile, sets the pose (attachments follow), sets `combat` when given, taps when `tap` is true and never untaps; settle (all, or `cards`) restores every displaced card to `home`, drops `home`/`combat`, removes attack and block lines (all, or those touching the listed cards), keeps marks and the tapped state. A permanent `move` with a pose, `tidy` or joining a pile forgets the home slot. Undo of settle is `settle {cards: []}` plus the restore patch.
- [M0-P1] A card entering a public zone that is not a battlefield (graveyard, exile, command, stack) loses tapped, counters, note and transformed face, as at a physical table; battlefield-to-battlefield moves keep them. Undo restores them.
- [M0-P1] `move` of a pile's base (pile field absent) from one battlefield to another takes the whole pile, re-laid at the new pose; any other cross-zone move of a base (e.g. to the graveyard) moves the base alone.
- [M0-P1] The reducer computes sine and cosine with its own range reduction and fixed Taylor polynomials in plain IEEE arithmetic, never `Math.sin`/`Math.cos`, whose last bit differs between engines (DESIGN "Data model and protocol": shared state identical in every browser).
- [M0-P1] Invariants also check hidden-zone cards are opaque records (face down, front face, pose 0/0/0, controller = owner, zone owned by the card's owner), card owners and controllers are seats, `combat` only with `home`, no displaced card in a pile, and the turn marker names a seat or team with a positive turn number.
- [M0-P1] The natural inverse of moveTemp is a moveTemp back to the previous pose, not a settle, so the undo never removes lines other seats drew in the meantime.
- [M0-P1] The undo property test asserts exact hashes for more than 90% of undos and identity-only differences for the rest (undo into a hidden zone drops the identity).
- [M0-P1] Integrator: the RestorePatch amendment (compare-and-set `RestoreStep`, `RestorableCardField`) is confirmed — RestorePatch was M0-P1's own unmerged additive contract, so the net change to core/actions.ts against main stays additive; the package's other decision lines above already carry the rest of its reported decisions.
- [M0-P4] A bare key acts on the hovered card, or on the whole selection when the hovered card is selected or nothing is hovered; a multi-card gesture shares one `group` — DESIGN "hover plus key … or the selection" leaves the precedence open.
- [M0-P4] T on a mixed selection taps the untapped cards; a fully tapped selection untaps — per-card toggling would scramble a mixed selection.
- [M0-P4] + / − change +1/+1 counters (− falls back to another kind on the card); named kinds (−1/−1, loyalty, charge) come from the context menu — the canonical keymap gives 1–4 to seats, so number keys cannot pick counter kinds.
- [M0-P4] G, E, H, L, Shift+L send a card to its owner's graveyard, exile, hand or library; C casts from hand, graveyard, exile or the command area, never from the battlefield, the stack or the library.
- [M0-P4] R resolves to the game's default destination (`to` omitted); before everyone passed, a second R within 3 s resolves with `force`. The stack chip asks where a spell goes with inline buttons (default first, then battlefield, graveyard, exile, hand); R accepts the default — DESIGN "Resolve asks where the item goes … One key accepts the default" without a modal chooser.
- [M0-P4] D, Shift+D, S and Ctrl+M act on the own library whatever is hovered.
- [M0-P4] Tab at the last step does not wrap (Ctrl+Enter passes the turn clockwise); the new active player's untap-and-draw offer appears in that player's own HUD when it sees the turn number change to its seat or team, and uses bare U while it shows (U is a reserved key: listed in the cheat sheet, refused by remapping, not remappable because `ActionId` is a contract type); Enter while holding the floor releases the hold; K asks without a label.
- [M0-P4] Ctrl+I rolls a d6 and Ctrl+Shift+I flips a coin, dispatching rngCommit then rngReveal back to back (solo); milestone 1 waits for the peers' commits.
- [M0-P4] V, Shift+V and M call an `onVoice` hook and show a chip; the voice module connects in milestone 3.
- [M0-P4] Tidy keeps the previous row bands (lands behind creatures stay behind), wraps at the territory width minus the pile column, and leaves displaced cards alone.
- [M0-P4] Token copies (X) and created tokens enter untapped 69 mm to the controller's right of the source or hovered card, else in the territory centre.
- [M0-P4] The log hides protocol-only steps (rngCommit, shuffleNonce, shuffleDone, snapshotHash, sequencerClaim); life changes merge when by the same actor on the same seat within 2 s.
- [M0-P4] Scry: L / Shift+L on a looked-at library card dispatches `toZone` into the same library; the session performs the private reorder. Search = look at the whole library, shuffle on close (default on). Other seats' libraries show the count only.
- [M0-P4] Leaving a note field cancels the note; Enter saves, empty text clears.
- [M0-P4] AppProps gained optional `settings` (SettingsStore, persists remaps) and `parseDecklist` (data/'s parser); without the parser the HUD uses a minimal plain-list parser. main.ts (M0-P7) should pass both — additive contract change.
- [M0-P4] App shows the B0 bootstrap stage (ui/Smoke.svelte) until main.ts passes a session, so the B0 smoke test stays green before M0-P7 lands.
- [M0-P4] The HUD reads only the own SecretStore and never shows library faces except from an explicit lookAt result; it sends nothing but session actions, so it adds no leak-test scenario.
- [M0-P4] Double-click on the own library draws one card; on any other library, a graveyard or exile it opens the pile viewer; the avatar zone buttons open every viewer, including the own library.
- [M0-P4] Mill N dispatches one `move` to the own graveyard per card of `libraryOrder.slice(0, N)` with a shared group (the session emits the reveals); reveal top dispatches `revealTo` for the top card to every other seat (targeted-reveal path, the card stays hidden in shared state). Both are in the library viewer (sharing the N field with look at top N) and the table menu.
- [M0-P4] While a confirmation prompt shows, Esc first closes a transient overlay (context menu, reveal-to, token search, inspect, cheat sheet) and otherwise holds; pile viewers and the deck dialog do not take Esc from Hold.
- [M0-P4] A HUD hover (hand, pile viewer, log card name) is dropped as soon as its card is no longer shown in that surface, since a removed element fires no pointerleave.
- [M0-P4] Stored keymap overrides are filtered to well-formed records on load, and a load that resolves after the player already remapped in this session is ignored.
- [M0-P4] A keyboard-focused button, link or checkbox keeps its Enter and Space; mouse clicks don't leave focus on HUD buttons — so Enter still means focus chat.
- [M0-P4] Fakes use invented card data only, and the harness never imports tests/fixtures — no real card data in the bundled harness chunk.
- [M0-P7] The public trace of a solo shuffle is `reorder {zone, count: zone size, orderHash}`, because M0's reducer refuses shuffleCommit/shuffleDone (M1-P2); the private audit record is the design's `{sid, zone, ownerNonce, peerNonces: [], order}`. Seed = SHA-256(ownerNonce + peer nonces in seat order) as base64url; byte-stream block i = SHA-256(seed + decimal i); swap partners by 32-bit rejection sampling (no modulo bias); orderHash = SHA-256(order.join(',') + '|' + ownerNonce).
- [M0-P7] Private effects (import, shuffle, draw, reorder) run synchronously with a small pure SHA-256 in session/sha256.ts (tested byte-equal to Web Crypto), so no action can interleave with a half-done shuffle or import; the TableSession methods still return promises.
- [M0-P7] Deck import: random 80-bit opaque ids (`c` + 16 base-32 chars), commits published sorted (salted hashes, so the order says nothing about ids or deck order), instance ids sorted; the unshuffled library is the decklist order and import does not shuffle; the sideboard goes to SecretStore.pool; a second import or an empty main deck is refused.
- [M0-P7] `dispatch` emits `reveal {instanceId, ref, salt}` only when a dry run shows the action applies and the named own card ends up public (moved out of a hidden zone, cast, or turned face up without a public identity); a refused action consumes no seq, publishes nothing and adds a system warning to the log.
- [M0-P7] `toZone` of an own library card into its own library (scry/surveil top or bottom) becomes a private reorder: `reorder {count: 1, orderHash}` plus a ReorderRecord; within the hand it is ignored. `dispatch` refuses ready, shuffle*, reorder, mulligan and undo (session methods only) and a `draw` that does not name the top card.
- [M0-P7] Undo: a stack of own undoable actions with core's `inverse()` computed when applied; a group is undone as one step, all or nothing after a dry run (a stale step is dropped with a warning); `draw(n > 1)` is one group; shuffle, mulligan and deck import clear the stack (undo never reaches past a shuffle); the hand order is put back. "Undo restores the previous hash" is asserted with `seq` aside (the undo is itself a sequenced action, as in core's `undoKey`). The undo stack is per page and not restored after a reload.
- [M0-P7] A fresh solo table is `createTable` 1v1 (seat 1 named '') saved as a seq-0 snapshot, then `stageSet match` as the first action. Restore replays the log after the stored snapshot, stops at a gap or a refused entry with a warning, and reconciles the secret lists with the restored zones. System (warning) lines are not persisted.
- [M0-P7] Mulligan number = the seat's mulligan actions in the session log + 1; `lookAt` works on the own library or hand only and clamps n to the zone size.
- [M0-P7] The debug hook gains `session` (hash, importDeck, importDecklist, shuffle, draw, mulligan, lookAt, undo, canUndo, log, saved), declared by declaration merging in src/debug/session.ts — no contract file changed.
- [M0-P7] Browser tests inject the recorded Scryfall fixtures as `window.__spellplaneFixtures` (Playwright addInitScript); in dev/test builds main.ts then uses `FixtureCardDataSource` (src/debug/fixture-cards.ts, lazily imported, absent from production) instead of Scryfall. Without IndexedDB the app runs unsaved.
- [M0-P7] Integrator: main.ts mounts App with AppProps plus M0-P4's optional `settings` and `parseDecklist` through a cast, because App on m0 is still the B0 stage without props; drop the cast once M0-P4 is merged. With M0-P4's App the solo route renders the HUD instead of the B0 smoke card, so tests/e2e/smoke.spec.ts (expects one card) must follow M0-P4's Smoke fallback or be updated at that merge.
- [M0-P7] Restore replays the stored log only up to the table record's `lastSeq` (later entries are left over from an abandoned timeline). A restore that ends short (gap, refused entry, or before `lastSeq`) immediately stores the restored state as the record's snapshot (`snapshotSeq = lastSeq = restored seq`), and after any failed write the next successful write moves the snapshot to that action; so the stored log between `snapshotSeq` and `lastSeq` is always the current timeline. No TableStore method deletes log entries, so stale entries stay until overwritten and are never replayed.
- [M0-P7] Undo puts back only the cards the undone step returns to the library or hand, each at its index in the order saved before the action; every other card keeps its current private order, so a later scry or other private reorder (not undoable, does not clear the undo stack) survives the undo. When the undone action took a card from below the top of the library, core's public inverse (`toZone … position: 'top'`) is followed by a private reorder to that place — public `reorder {zone, count: 1, orderHash}` plus a ReorderRecord, in the undo's group — so the M1 audit stays consistent. An undone draw needs no reorder.
- [M0-P7] `SoloSession.playFaceDown(card, to, pose?)` puts an own hand or library card onto a battlefield or into exile face down (other public zones show faces, DESIGN "Table model") with no `reveal` (core's relocate turns a card without a public identity face down); flipping it up later emits the reveal. Integrator: TableSession/MoveAction have no face-down placement, so the HUD cannot reach it yet; proposed contract change: `TableSession.playFaceDown` (or `face?: 'down'` on MoveAction/StackPush) before the M1-G gate.
- [M0-P7] Known limits for the integrator: the session log after a reload holds only entries after the stored snapshot (TableStore.loadTable returns no earlier log), so the action log panel and the mulligan number (counted from the log) restart at the last snapshot; fixing it needs a TableStore method that loads the full log or a per-seat mulligan count in shared state. The undo stack is not restored after a reload.
- [M0-P7] A face-down card moved (`move`) from a public zone into a public zone other than a battlefield or exile (graveyard, stack, command) is turned up there: `reveal` first if it is an own card with no public identity, then the move, then `flip up`, all in one group (the move's own group, or a fresh one) so one undo takes back the move and the turn-up; a revealed identity stays public. A face-down card of another seat whose identity this browser does not hold stays face down (only its owner can reveal it). Core's relocate keeps the face on public-to-public moves, so the session does this, not the reducer.
- [M0-P7] `dispatch` and `undo` check and dry-run unstamped drafts and take a cid only for what is committed or finally refused, so a dry-run reveal or a refused undo uses up no cid and a reveal's cid precedes its action's; a draft refused by dispatch is still returned stamped (TableSession.dispatch returns an Action).
- [M0-P7] `SOLO_ROOM` re-exports store's `SOLO_ROOM_ID` (session may import store), so the two cannot drift apart.
- [M0-P7] Integrator: the package's decision lines above already carry its reported decisions. At the merge main.ts dropped the App cast (M0-P4 is merged); with a session `#/` shows the HUD, so the B0 smoke test now moves a fixture card onto the solo battlefield and finds it through the debug hook, and the `#/` route tests expect the HUD. The proposed face-down placement (`TableSession.playFaceDown` or `face?: 'down'` on MoveAction/StackPush) is open for M1-C.
- [M0-G] The gate fetches a Leonin Warleader with the library viewer's Search (hover, H) before casting, so the cast creature always has a related token (its Cat) whatever the shuffle dealt; closing the search shuffles as designed.
- [M0-G] The London mulligan's "bottom cards by hand" is one Shift+L on a hovered hand card; the gate mills three (library viewer, Mill N) so the graveyard fan has several cards; it draws (D) until a land is in hand.
- [M0-G] Beyond the listed screenshot set the gate also saves `attack` and Z-zoomed `attack-zoomed` and `mid-game-board-zoomed`, so counters, tapped state and displacement can be judged at card scale.
- [M0-G] Resolve (R, the Resolve button and the resolve-to choices) sends the destination from GameDefinition.defaultDestination (so an instant goes to its owner's graveyard, not the battlefield) and, for a battlefield, a pose from `freeSlot` (ui/geometry.ts): rows from the territory's front edge back, columns alternating left and right starting lane half-width + gap + half a card height from the centre line (a tapped attacker 110 forward clears the stack lane), 6 mm clear of every card and home slot, the pile column and the command area; the territory centre when full.
- [M0-G] The ghost outline and displacement marker (DESIGN "Combat and annotation", Temporary move) are drawn in M0 by scene/ghost.ts: a dashed card outline at `home` (CardDressing.ghost, under the cards) and a chevron 2 mm outside the card edge facing home, pointing along the displacement (CardDressing.displacedMarker, over the cards, at least 12 px on screen, 5–14 mm). Debug cards gain `displaced` and `ghost`. M1-P4 keeps the geometry and adds lines and blocks.
- [M0-G] Zone labels are a second SDF text batch drawn under the cards (render order 1, like the hairlines); counters, pile counts and notes stay over them. Counter badges sit centred over the art box (not over the mana cost or power/toughness) and scale up (no re-layout) so the type stays at least 11 px tall, up to 3×.
- [M0-G] The HUD tells the scene which screen edges its layer covers through the scene host's `data-fit-insets="top right bottom left"` (no contract change): toolbar row 40, open log 316, hand fan 22% vh + 8, avatar column 190. The bird's-eye fit, the zoom-out limit and go-to-seat frame the table inside the rest (never below 40% of the viewport per axis); a fitted view refits when the insets or viewport change.
- [M0-G] Log: names are frozen when a line is first written (a per-page memo in the HUD; after a reload restored entries are named from the restored state); draws, moves and taps sharing a group are one line ("draws 7 cards", "moves A, B and C to their graveyard"); a `reveal` immediately followed by the same seat's action on that card gets no line; a `reorder` of more than one card (M0's shuffle trace) reads "shuffles their library", of one card "moves a card within their library"; `stageSet match` reads "starts the game".
- [M0-G] A seat with an empty name, no peer and no cards is an empty chair: the HUD shows no avatar for it (solo's second seat). Its zone markings are still drawn by the scene.
- [M0-G] Open for M1-P2: instance ids stay stable across hand → library → graveyard, so a peer could link hidden history to a later reveal; the owner should re-issue opaque ids when a card returns to a hidden zone (DESIGN "Table model", Hidden cards).
- [M0] The M0 workflow was interrupted by a machine crash after integrating all seven packages; the main session verified check and e2e, committed the workflow script, and re-ran the milestone workflow with no contracts and no packages (gate phase only).

- [M0] After the merge, CI on main failed three e2e tests that passed locally (runner timing: the pan fling needs the release within 80 ms of the last move; on Linux Ctrl+Z in the note field is the browser's text undo). Fixed the tests on main (9a45964); CI green.

## M1 1v1 peer-to-peer

- [M1-C] Added package M1-P8 Networked session (`session/*`, `main.ts`, `debug/*`; after M1-P1, M1-P2, M1-P5) — the plan had no owner for the glue of TableSession, NetEngine, pair channel, sequencer timers and the `#/t/<room>/<key>` route; M1-P1 owns `net/*` except `net/private.ts` (M1-P2) and `net/sync.ts` (M1-P7); M1-P5 also owns `#/diag`.
- [M1-C] The NetEngine owns the replica (confirmed state, optimistic pending queue, out-of-order buffer, retained tail) through core's reducer, so sequencing, rollback, catch-up and hash exchange live in `net/` and the session only reacts to `onState`/`onSequenced`/`onRollback` — the design's convergence and late-joiner tests need state inside the engine.
- [M1-C] Handshake: `hello {peerId, pubKey, nickname, seat, role, lastSeq, nonce}` and a new `welcome {peerId, sig, sequencer, seq}` that carries the signature over the other side's hello nonce (`handshakeText` = `spellplane-v1|<roomId>|<nonce>|<signerPeerId>`, ECDSA P-256/SHA-256, IEEE P1363, base64url); `pubKey` is base64url SPKI, matching store/identity.ts's peer id — a hello cannot sign a nonce it has not seen yet.
- [M1-C] Added wire messages beyond the design table: `claimAck {lastSeq}` (answer to `claim`), `bye {reason, version}` (version mismatch notice and clean leave; frozen layout), `lookReq`/`look` on `prv` (looking at another seat's hidden cards goes through its owner on the pair channel). Presence messages carry a per-sender counter `n` so stale unreliable messages are dropped; presence is throttled to 50 ms.
- [M1-C] `syncRes` carries a snapshot whenever the gap exceeds 500 or `haveSeq` is 0 — a fresh joiner's seq-0 table may differ from the creator's (format, seats), so it never replays from its own genesis.
- [M1-C] New actions `away {seat, away}` (reducer M1-P7) and `commitClose {kind, id}` (reducer M1-P2), both proposed only by the sequencer — away seats are shared state, and "excluded after 3 s" needs one sequenced point that all peers agree on; RngRound/ShuffleRound gained optional `closedSeq`.
- [M1-C] Sequencer timers are pure data: `Deadline {key, fromSeq, afterMs, emit}` from `core/deadlines.ts` (`confirmDeadlines` M1-P3 + `commitWindowDeadlines` M1-P2); the engine arms them only while it is the sequencer, from its own apply time of `fromSeq`, so a new sequencer after a handover restarts the same timers. ConfirmRound gained optional `timerSeq` (the release of the last hold restarts the countdown).
- [M1-C] Moved the pure SHA-256 to `core/sha256.ts`, `commitFor` to `core/commit.ts` and the shuffle derivation (`shuffleSeed`, `ByteStream`, `shuffledOrder`, `orderHash`) to `core/rng.ts`; `session/secrecy.ts` re-exports them and keeps only the `random*` functions. `verifyReveal`, `verifyNonce`, `makeCommitments`, `rngSeed`, `rngOutcome` are implemented (one-liners on the spec); `expectedParticipants`, `commitWindowDeadlines`, `receiveIdentity`, `pruneReceived`, `secretTokens` are stubs for M1-P2.
- [M1-C] TableSession gained `roomId`, `playFaceDown(card, to, pose?)` (the M0-P7 proposal, chosen over `face?: 'down'` on MoveAction because the session, not the reducer, decides what stays secret), `chat`, `takeSeat(seat, colour)`, `roll(purpose, n)`, `presence`, `netStatus`/`subscribeNet` and `whenSaved`; hold, release, OK and ask stay plain `respond`/`ask` drafts through `dispatch`. `seat` stays a SeatId: a networked session resolves once it reclaimed its chair or took the lowest free one (spectators in M3 may need it widened).
- [M1-C] TableStore gained `loadLog(roomId, afterSeq?)` (fixes the M0-P7 known limit) and `saveSecrets(roomId, secrets)`, implemented in store/tables.ts since both are one IndexedDB call.
- [M1-C] Debug hook `net` is `NetDebug` (messages with peer and time, sequencer, self, peers, status, seq, clearMessages, kill, revive, close), a superset of the design's `{messages, sequencer}`; the shared leak scan is `tests/leak/scan.ts` `scan(values, forbidden) -> LeakHit[]` fed by core/secrets.ts `secretTokens`.
- [M1-C] UI contracts: `RoomLink`, `StartPageProps` (`#/lobby` start page), `LobbyProps` (in-table lobby while the stage is 'lobby'), `DiagProps`/`DiagReport` (`#/diag`); AppProps gained optional `link`, `identity`, `decks`, `start`, `diag`. `ui/lobby/room.ts` has `roomLink`/`parseRoomLink` and a `newRoom` stub for M1-P5.
- [M1-P2] A reveal (or a flip carrying `ref`/`salt`) that does not open the owner's commitment is applied and marks the card `suspect` in shared state, so every peer flags it the same way; only the card's owner may reveal it, and tokens have no committed identity — DESIGN "Commitments": flagged, not refused.
- [M1-P2] A mismatched `rngReveal` nonce is refused (nack), unlike a card reveal — accepting it would let the sender pick the result after seeing the others' nonces; a seat that committed and never reveals leaves the round pending (no reveal timeout in the design).
- [M1-P2] "Connected seated" for joint randomness = a seat with a non-empty name, away or not (amended in review round 4, see the `away` line below), evaluated on the current state while a round is open (a seat leaving its chair stops being waited for, a seat sitting down is waited for); the reducer seals a round (`closedSeq` = seq of that step) in the step that completes it, whatever the action (last nonce or commit, a seat leaving), so a seat that comes back or sits down afterwards never joins it and the owner's order or the dice seed is computed from a set that cannot change (`sealCompleteRounds`, run after every applied action; no contract change, `closedSeq` now also marks completion); `commitWindowDeadlines` yields one deadline per round still waiting (`rng:<rid>`, `shuffle:<sid>`, from the round's `startSeq`), none once sealed; a `commitClose` on a sealed round is refused. Rng ids and shuffle ids are separate namespaces: `expectedParticipants(state, id, kind?)` looks up only the named kind, and the reducer refuses a `shuffleCommit`/opening `rngCommit` whose id names a round of the other kind.
- [M1-P2] The owner cannot change its nonce or its round after seeing a peer nonce, which a 1v1 sequencer owner does before it sequences that nonce: a shuffle id is never re-committed, and a zone with an unfinished round (committed, no `shuffleDone`) refuses a new `shuffleCommit` until that round is done; no round is ever dropped or replaced (round-3 review; this replaces 'an open round without peer nonces is dropped by a new one'). A lost peer still unblocks the round when the owner does not sequence (the sequencer's `commitClose`); a sequencer owner's round waits for the peer to return or leave, or for M1-P7's recovery (amended in review round 4: `away` no longer ends the wait); `beginShuffle` persists the owner nonce before proposing, so a reload can finish. The binding of `shuffleDone` to the committed nonce is checked by the audit (the reducer cannot see the nonce). Known limit: an owner that lost its nonce mid-round can only leave the zone through M1-P7's recovery flow.
- [M1-P2] M0-G finding closed: opaque ids are re-issued (`Reissue {ids, commits}`, both sorted, optional on `shuffleDone` and `reorder`) only where needed, so the Ready commitment keeps binding live reveals otherwise (DESIGN "Table model", Hidden cards: "where the rules require it"): `finishShuffle(…, disclosed)` re-issues the zone only when it holds an id in `disclosed`, which the owner's client accumulates with `disclosedBy(secrets, prior, action, next)` over every sequenced action (a card whose identity became public, an own `revealTo`, another seat's `lookAt` of own cards); otherwise it permutes the existing ids (Secret shuffle 4) and sends no `reissue`. After a public card returns to the hand or library the owner sends a re-issuing `reorder` of that zone (`reissueZone`). New ids must be fresh (never a card, never in the seat's commitment); the seat's commitment keeps every id and commit ever published, so an old commit can never vouch for a second card. Commitments made by a re-issue are checked live only for reveals of the new ids, and against the deck commitment only by the post-game audit (the reducer cannot tell a needed re-issue from a needless one without a public disclosure marker, a contract amendment). A zone holding a suspect card is never re-issued (the reducer refuses, `reissueZone` returns null, `finishShuffle` keeps the ids), so the flag cannot be washed off. M1-P8 wires `reissueZone` after returns to the hand (it makes the undo of that return stale) and keeps the `disclosed` set (rebuild it from the log on reload).
- [M1-P2] A re-issuing shuffle labels positions with the fresh ids shuffled by `reissueSeed(seed) = SHA-256(seed + '|reissue')`, independent of the old ids' permutation: peers cannot pair old and new ids, while anyone with the owner nonce replays the full order (PLAN: reproducible from public messages plus the owner's nonce); the order hash is over the new ids. Audit records carry `reissue {before, retired}` (old ids and identities) so the audit checks that every card kept its identity and commitment.
- [M1-P2] Owner-side private effects are pure helpers in core/secrets.ts with randomness passed in as `Entropy {id, salt}`: `commitDeck`, `beginShuffle` (stores the pending nonce in the audit record so a reload can finish), `finishShuffle`, `reissueZone`, `privateReorder`, `revealedCard`, `disclosedBy`, `lookAnswer`, `auditSeat` (replays the public log with the published records, finding each reorder record by zone and order hash so a record of a refused proposal is skipped; draws are checked against the replayed order from the first shuffle on); session/ (M0-P7, M1-P8) should call them instead of its own copies.
- [M1-P2] Received identities are dropped (`pruneReceived`) when the card is gone (re-issued, removed), in a zone nobody sees (library) or public; a card shown in its owner's hand stays known while it is there.
- [M1-P2] net/private.ts routes every identity with `sendPrivate` to the one peer holding each named seat (the pair's WebRTC connection encrypts it; no extra application-layer encryption, which would need a key agreement the design does not have), never broadcasts, and sends only the own seat's cards. The disclosure policy lives in net/private.ts, not in the answerer: a look request is answered only when it matches a sequenced, unused `lookAt` of the asking seat (same zone, count, card; one answer per `lookAt`; a request that overtook its `lookAt` waits for it, at most 32), and the answer is cut to at most `count` own cards of that hidden zone, or exactly the peeked card when it is an own face-down card in a public zone (never a hand or library card for a peek); answerers should use core/secrets.ts `lookAnswer` (top `count` in the owner's order), since the channel cannot see the private order. A targeted reveal is taken only once the sender's public `revealTo` of that card naming this seat (cid `about`) is applied; a `reveal` with purpose 'look' only for a look this browser asked that seat for. Any identity waits until the shared state knows its card, because the pair channel can overtake the sequenced log. A pending look resolves empty when the owner leaves. The channel needs `onState` and `onSequenced` of the NetEngine besides the members it names (`PrivateChannelHost`).
- [M1-P2] The shared leak scanner is tests/leak/leak.ts (`leakScan`, `forbiddenFor`, `scanPairs`) on top of M1-C's `scan`: forbidden tokens (Scryfall ids, salts and nonces of A's undisclosed hidden cards, retired salts) anywhere, and forbidden pairs within two levels of one object (current id next to its Scryfall id unless A disclosed exactly that card and it did not return to a hidden zone since; old id next to its re-issued id); `disclosed` comes from A's side. The scripted game runs on a minimal sequencer over LoopbackNetwork (tests/leak/minitable.ts, not M1-P1's engine) in tests/sim/secrecy/leak.test.ts, because vitest only collects tests under tests/unit and tests/sim.
- [M1-P2] Contract additions: `Reissue` and optional `reissue` on ShuffleDoneAction/ReorderAction (core/actions.ts), `ReissueRecord` and optional `reissue` on ShuffleRecord/ReorderRecord (core/types.ts); the reducer's `Draft` gained `rng`/`shuffles` records; tests/unit/core/reducer.test.ts no longer expects the shuffle and randomness actions to be refused. They first appeared on the package branch; the M1-C amendment for the M1 retry landed them on m1 verbatim, so after merging m1 the branch no longer differs from m1 in any contract file.
- [M1-P2] A shuffle's zone is frozen from its `shuffleCommit` until its `shuffleDone` (`isOpen`, reducer/secret.ts `checkFrozenZones`): the reducer refuses any step, by anyone, that changes the zone's set of ids (draw, toZone, move out, mulligan, re-issue, undo) — DESIGN "Secret shuffle" 3: the owner cannot choose the order, which it could by choosing the ids it shuffles once it sees a peer nonce (as the sequencer, before sequencing it). The audit flags a shuffle whose zone ids at `shuffleDone` differ from those at its commit.
- [M1-P2] `commitClose` is refused from the round's own initiator (rng) or the shuffle's owner — otherwise seat 0, the 1v1 sequencer, could close its own window at once and decide alone with a nonce ground offline. Consequence: the sequencer's own round with a silent peer stays open until that peer contributes or leaves its chair (an `away` mark no longer ends the wait, review round 4); the deadline the sequencer emits for its own round is simply refused.
- [M1-P2] The audit checks every private reorder of the library against the replayed order: a plain `reorder {count}` may only move the top `count` cards it looked at (the others keep their relative order), and a re-issuing reorder must record the replayed order as `reissue.before` (it renames cards in place). Hand reorders are not checked (the hand order is never committed).
- [M1-P2] A look answer is bound to its `lookAt`: when another seat's `lookAt` of an own zone or card is applied to the confirmed state, net/private.ts asks the answerer right then (a synthetic `LookReqMessage` with lookId `seq-<seq>`), cuts the answer by the confirmed state and stores it; the asker's request gets exactly those cards, or a refusal if one has left the zone, the zone was shuffled, reordered or re-issued since, or the owner had pending own actions that may change that zone at the `lookAt` (a `reorder`/`shuffleCommit` of it, the `shuffleDone` of its round, a `draw`/`move`/`toZone` of one of its cards or into it, the owner's `mulligan`/`ready`, any `undo`), or view and confirmed state differ there. A plain `reorder` (scry) changes the owner's store but nothing public, so the pending queue is the only sign (round-3 review). `PrivateChannelHost` picks `pending()` from NetEngine (added by the M1-C amendment for the retry; M1-P1's engine implements it), so `createPrivateChannel(engine)` answers zone looks; a host object that has no `pending` function at run time still gets no zone look answered (fails closed); peeks of a face-down card are unaffected. Answerers must answer from `engine.confirmedState()` (`lookAnswer(secrets, engine.confirmedState(), req)`), the same point `disclosedBy` records; `PrivateChannelHost` gained `confirmedState`. A contract amendment passing the state to the answerer would remove that reliance (open, for the integrator).
- [M1-P2] Waiting look requests, targeted reveals waiting for their `revealTo`, and sequenced `lookAt`s never asked for expire after `EXPIRE_AFTER_ACTIONS` (200) sequenced actions; an expired request is refused so the asker's promise resolves. When the waiting-reveal buffer is full the oldest entry is dropped instead of the new one.
- [M1-P2] A restore patch never puts an identity on a card that is not a token (reducer/restore.ts): a committed card's identity becomes public only through a reveal that opens the commitment, so a forged undo cannot reveal a card unverified (round-3 review; closes the M0-P1 hand-over "checking it against the commitment is M1-P2's"). Instead core/undo.ts `undoPlan(before, action)` returns the inverse plus `reveals: UndoReveal[]` (card, ref, face before): the undo brings such a card back face down without identity (`withoutReveals`), and the owner's session then sends the verified re-reveal (`flip` up with `ref`/`salt` when it was face up, else `reveal`) in the undo's group; solo.ts does this, M1-P8 must too. `inverse` keeps its signature (= `undoPlan(...)?.inverse`). Undoing another seat's card that went to a hidden zone leaves it face down until its owner turns it up.
- [M1-P2] core/secrets.ts `lookAnswer` answers another seat's zone look (top `count` of an own hand or library) only with `{allowZoneLook: true}`; peeks of an own face-down card need no flag. The design has no opponent-initiated look at a hidden zone, only the owner's "Reveal to…" (Hidden information 5), so M1-P8's answerer sets the flag only after the owner agreed; since net/private.ts asks the answerer synchronously when the `lookAt` is sequenced, the agreement has to exist before then (for example the owner's standing permission for that seat and zone, shown in the HUD), else the look is refused and the asker sees an empty answer.
- [M1-P2] A randomness round whose commit phase is complete waits for every committed seat's `rngReveal`, with no timeout (the design has none). SecretStore has no slot for a pending rng nonce (a contract amendment), so M1-P8 keeps the nonce of an own open round in its own local storage until the round is decided, and the HUD shows a round still undecided after the reveals of the others as "roll abandoned by sN" (a new roll is a new round). A last revealer that withholds its reveal and starts a new roll is visible in the log, not prevented.
- [M1-P2] Residual risk (for the M1 gate report): the reducer accepts a re-issue of an own hidden zone at any time with commits the owner chooses, so a reveal of a re-issued id is checked live only against those new commits; only the post-game audit (`auditSeat`) ties them back to the deck commitment. Gating a re-issue on a public need (a card entered the zone from a public zone, or a `revealTo`/another seat's `lookAt` touched it since its ids were issued) needs a public marker in TableState, a contract amendment for the integrator; running `auditSeat` automatically at game end is M1-P8/M5's.
- [M1-P1] One proposer's actions keep their order: the engine sends and resends its pending proposals in the order it applied them, each with `prev` (M1-C amendment: the cid of the previous own proposal still pending, null for the oldest), and the sequencer holds a proposal until its `prev` is sequenced or refused, then goes on with the proposals that waited for it in the order they came. A held proposal waits at most HOLD_PREV_MS = 2 s from when it was first held (a resent copy keeps that time; the proposer resends every 1 s, so a lost `prev` normally arrives within one resend), then is sequenced or refused without it — never lost; at most 2,000 are held, and a claim queues them with the other proposals. The engine resends proposals, hellos, claims and catch-up requests until answered (1 s each; a seq gap waits 500 ms for reordering before asking).
- [M1-P1] The sequencer nacks, besides the structural checks (by = sender's seat; unseated players only `seatTake` of a free seat with their own peer id; spectators only social actions with a `by` that is no seat; `away`/`commitClose`/`sequencerClaim`/`respond timeout` only from itself), every action on a card that lies in another seat's hidden zone (the card, host, source, copied card, settled cards or tidied cards it names: move, moveTemp, toZone, stackPush, attach, copy, remove, pile, flip, tap, settle, … and an undo by its inverse) and the owner-only secrecy steps (`draw`/`reveal`/`flip` with identity/`revealTo` of own cards, `shuffleCommit`/`shuffleDone`/`reorder`/`ready` of own zones). A public card may still go into another seat's hidden zone (returning an opponent's creature to its owner's hand: its identity was public), and `lookAt` of another seat's hidden zone stays allowed (it is the asker's public record; the owner answers through net/private.ts). It also nacks whatever the reducer refuses on the confirmed state — it would be a no-op for everyone, and the proposer learns it through its rollback. A sequenced action the reducer refuses still advances `state.seq` (`applySequenced`), identically everywhere.
- [M1-P1] Election while no connected player holds a seat (a table still in its lobby): the lowest peer id among connected players — "earliest join" cannot be agreed on by peers that see different join times.
- [M1-P1] Peers accept `seq` only from their current sequencer (catch-up from others goes through syncReq/syncRes); on a change of sequencer they drop buffered out-of-order seqs and stored sequencer hashes, and the new sequencer's first `seq` triggers an overlap read (last 64 actions) that detects a fork. A fork is repaired by rewinding to the fork point (the replica keeps a state every 50 actions, 12 deep) and fetching the sequencer's tail, or by its snapshot when too old; own actions of the abandoned part that the adopted history lacks are proposed again.
- [M1-P1] Handover: the claimant waits for `claimAck` from every connected peer, then takes over the history of the peer furthest ahead (overlap check, then tail) before it sequences; a peer acks only the claimant its own rule 1 elects. An active sequencer that meets a peer ahead of it, or sees an away peer come back, claims again first — so a sequencer page that returns continues from what the others acknowledged. The new sequencer logs `sequencerClaim {fromSeq, peer}` when it took over from another browser.
- [M1-P1] Handover time: when peers see the sequencer leave, the next action is sequenced within AWAY_AFTER_MS (measured ≤ 3.6 s at 400 ms delay, 5% drop); a sequencer that just falls silent is replaced within AWAY_AFTER_MS + 5 s (detection, then claim, claimAck and catch-up with resends; measured ≤ 10.1 s).
- [M1-P1] State hashes use the synchronous SHA-256 (core/sha256.ts) of the canonical JSON, so hashing never interleaves with incoming actions; peers compare only against the sequencer's hash, and a mismatch reloads the sequencer's snapshot (logs agree, so nothing is re-proposed). `syncRes` snapshots are the latest periodic snapshot (every 200) plus the retained tail after it; a replica retains 1,000 actions and 20,000 cids.
- [M1-P1] The active sequencer proposes `away {seat, away}` for a seat whose peer it saw this session and lost, and `away false` when it returns (the reducer, core/reducer/table.ts `away`, landed on m1). Those records and `sequencerClaim` describe the sequencer's own view at the time: when its history is abandoned for another (a fork repaired by rewind), they are not proposed again with its other own actions; the sequencer derives them afresh once active.
- [M1-P1] Trystero 0.26 gives one reliable data channel per peer: the four channels are four Trystero actions on it (so `pres` is reliable too, a deviation from the design's unreliable channel); Nostr only — the BitTorrent strategy now ships as the separate package `@trystero-p2p/torrent`, which is not a dependency (adding it needs the integrator's approval and a lockfile change), so the second strategy is a gap for the M1 gate report. Trystero is imported on `join`, so tests and the solo table never load it; a `leave` while it is loading opens no room, and an engine left during its transport's join leaves the transport when the join completes.
- [M1-P1] `sendPrivate` is fire-and-forget on `prv` (no ack or resend in the engine): M1-P2's simulated tests should keep `prv` out of `faultyChannels` or resend themselves. Presence numbers all of a sender's messages with one counter and drops a message older than the last applied of the same type (a typing indicator must not make an older cursor stale).
- [M1-P1] Simulated-network tests replace net/identity.ts with a deterministic fake (Web Crypto settles on Node's thread pool, outside the fake clock); real ECDSA is covered by tests/unit/net/identity and handshake tests and the e2e specs. The net e2e specs bundle tests/e2e/net/page.ts with Vite and serve it through a Playwright route on the app's origin, because `?harness=` entries live in main.ts (M1-P8).
- [M1-P1] onSequenced reports each (seq, cid) once: a snapshot reload (hash mismatch, a fork too old to rewind) replays the retained tail through the replica, and actions already reported at the same seq are not reported again; an action at a seq whose cid changed (a replaced history) is reported.
- [M1-P1] The handover line ("… now sequences the table.") is logged only when the former sequencer really sequenced for this browser (a `seq` came from it) or is gone (away, left); a joiner that verifies a higher seat before the lowest one only settles and logs nothing.
- [M1-P1] Leak scenario (CLAUDE.md shared leak test): a non-owner's move, cast or re-sort of another seat's hidden card is nacked and nothing about it leaves the owner (tests/sim/net/protocol.test.ts, authority). M1-P2's shared leak test (tests/sim/secrecy/leak.test.ts) runs on its own minimal sequencer; the integrator should add this scenario there once both packages are on m1.
- [M1-P1] Since the merge of m1 (7b61414, the identity key pair kept as JWK) the net e2e specs use the persisted IndexedDB identity on Chromium and WebKit alike; the WebKit in-memory key fallback and the WebKit skip of the reopened-identity spec are gone. That spec closes the first page with `bye` and a plain `page.close()` (Playwright's WebKit never closes a page asked to `runBeforeUnload`, decision M1-G fix).
- [M1-P1] A replica is *reconciled* once it caught up from its sequencer, completed a claim that connected peers acknowledged, or verified peers name it as their sequencer. Until then (a fresh seq-0 table, a stale save, or a page that sequenced alone before meeting the room) it never elects itself against the room: it follows the sequencer that the verified peer furthest ahead of it names in `welcome`/`hb` (or that peer itself when it names nobody reachable), catches up, and only then elects. An unreconciled claimant that receives a claim from a peer further ahead (or, at the same seq, a lower seat index, a seated before an unseated claimant, then the lower peer id) yields to it and acks; counter-claims go out at most once per CLAIM_RETRY_MS per peer — DESIGN Resume: later arrivals reconcile to the copy with the highest sequence number; without this a joiner whose own table seats nobody elected itself by peer id and two engines bounced claims for ever (review round 2).
- [M1-P1] Open for M1-P8: at seq 0 with nobody seated, a creator's and a fresh joiner's tables are indistinguishable by seq, so the lower peer id's table can win; the session should take its chair (seq ≥ 1) before others can join, and a fresh joiner should not act before it synced.
- [M1-P1] Handshake channel binding: the welcome signs `handshakeText(roomId, boundNonce(helloNonce, signerTransportId, recipientTransportId), signerPeerId)` with `boundNonce = <nonce>|<signer tid>|<recipient tid>`, and the verifier requires the signer to be the connection it heard the welcome on and the recipient to be itself — a peer in the room could otherwise relay one player's hello and welcome to another and be taken for that player (and receive its `prv` messages). Done inside the nonce argument, so `net/messages.ts` stays unchanged; integrator: the M1-C line on `handshakeText` and the WelcomeMessage doc comment should say the nonce is connection-bound. The binding is as strong as Trystero's peer ids (Trystero exposes no DTLS fingerprint to bind to). A verified connection of the same identity that is still listed by the transport and heard within AWAY_AFTER_MS is no longer replaced by a newer one; the newer connection verifies once the old one is away or gone (a reload whose old page sent `bye` replaces at once).
- [M1-P1] Leak scenario for the shared leak test (integrator, with M1-P2's tests/sim/secrecy/leak.test.ts): the relay attack above — Mallory relays Ben's hello and a welcome Ben signed over Anna's nonce; Anna refuses it (`bye badSignature`), keeps Ben on his own connection, and a `sendPrivate` to Ben's seat never reaches Mallory (tests/unit/net/handshake.test.ts).
- [M1-P1] The sequencer refuses `seatLeave` of a seat other than the sender's own (no host kick in the design; the reducer does not check it). Moving chairs: M1-P5 stamps `seatTake {seat: new, by: new, peer: self}` then `seatLeave {seat: old, by: old}`, so one peer briefly holds two chairs. The "sender's seat" the engine passes to the sequencer is the action's `by` when the sender's peer id holds that chair, else its first chair (`senderSeat`, net/sequencer.ts), and a `seatTake` stamped as the chair it takes is accepted from a seated player as from an unseated one (the engine checks its `peer` is the sender's, the reducer that the chair is free). Moves to a lower or a higher chair, stamped either way, are covered (review round 3).
- [M1-P1] The engine's wire-message log (`messages()`, debug hook and leak tests) records only in dev and test builds (`__SPELLPLANE_DEBUG__`), as a ring of the latest 200,000 records; production builds record nothing.
- [M1-P1] For the M1 gate report (owner checks / design deviations): (1) only the Nostr strategy is wired — trystero 0.26's `trystero/torrent` is a stub pointing to `@trystero-p2p/torrent`, which needs the integrator's approval as a new dependency; (2) `pres` runs on Trystero's single reliable data channel, not an unreliable one.
- [M1-P1] A follower re-verifies its history whenever its current sequencer claims again (not only on a change of sequencer): it drops buffered seqs and stored sequencer hashes, and until the check is done neither applies the sequencer's `seq`s nor catches up from it with a plain tail. The sequencer's first `seq` after the claim (or after the change) triggers an overlap read (the last 64 actions); that seq and any that arrive before the answer are dropped, and the answer, which carries them, runs the fork check first. So a follower's confirmed state equals the sequencer's from the sequencer's first new seq on, also when reordering brings a later seq first, and a sequencer that adopted another history during its claim (a split that healed) leads the follower to rewind and re-propose its own acknowledged actions of the abandoned part before anything is applied on top of it. While its sequencer is reachable, a follower catches up from the sequencer only — a plain tail from a peer ahead of it (another side's history) would land on its copy without a fork check. A claimant that saw a connected peer name another sequencer, or adopted another peer's history during the claim, logs `sequencerClaim` at activation, so every follower gets a `seq` to verify against even when both histories reached the same seq (review round 3; regression tests in tests/sim/net/protocol.test.ts, the reviewer's seed-5 split with each follower's history and hash at the sequencer's first new seq).
- [M1-P1] TrysteroTransport lists a peer only from `onPeerJoin`: a message Trystero delivers after `onPeerLeave` is dropped and does not list the peer again; `leave` forgets a pending join, so a join after it opens a room. The engine remembers its own cids up to 4,000 (4 × the retained tail), oldest forgotten first.
- [M1-P1] A sequencer that was cut off and went on alone (its own actions, `away` records for the seats it lost) never sequences on top of that history once the room is back: when a peer it counted away speaks again, or a peer ahead of it verifies, it claims before anything else reacts (before the `away false` for the returning seat). And a follower never takes a fork from its sequencer while that sequencer is behind it: a `seq` at or below the follower's head that differs from its copy, from a sequencer whose last known head (hello, welcome, heartbeat, claim, or the seq itself) is below the follower's, is dropped and the follower sends its head to the sequencer at once (at most once a second), whose `aheadCheck` makes it claim and take over the room's history; its own actions of the abandoned part are proposed again. Reason: DESIGN "Multiplayer architecture", the lowest seat takes over from the last sequence number everyone acknowledges; after the merge of m1 (where `away` succeeds) the returning sequencer's `away false` reached followers that had already elected it and they adopted its stale history (tests/sim/net/protocol.test.ts, a sequencer that went on alone returns).
- [M1-P1] Chair moves through M1-P5's real stamping are tested by driving src/ui/lobby/local-session.ts `takeSeat` over a listening transport and proposing what it sends through the engine (tests/sim/net/chairs.test.ts), together with the review round-3 chair probes, so the lobby's stamping and the sequencer's authority check cannot drift apart.
- [M1-P6] core/log.ts holds the pure timeline: `parseChatText` (`[[name]]`, optional `[[name|SET]]`; blank, unclosed, over 160 characters, or containing a bracket or line break stays plain text; parts join back to the text as typed), `mergeTimeline` (by seq; a system line after the action of its seq; same cid once, sequenced copy preferred; unsequenced own lines last by `at`), `filterTimeline` (all / chat / actions, where 'actions' keeps system lines), `attributions` and `cardsMentioned`. ui/describe.ts keeps every M0-G wording and grouping rule; describing stays in ui/.
- [M1-P6] `[[name]]` resolves lazily on hover or focus through `CardDataSource.resolveDeck` with a one-line deck. One lookup per name per page. A set hint that matches nothing falls back to the name, and a failed request is not cached. An unknown name shows "No card named …" with near matches. A caption is a chat line on the table tab and gets previews too.
- [M1-P6] Filters are a per-page view (All / Chat / Actions in the log header, not persisted, no shared state). Chat tabs show only when there is more than one: 'team' appears when the own seat has a team, and the spectator tab waits for spectators (M3/M5). Chat lines show on their own tab, action lines on every tab. Team chat is still a sequenced `chat` action that every peer receives, so its secrecy is open for M3. Chat input is capped at 500 characters.
- [M1-P6] Attribution flash: an action touches the cards it changes (move, moveTemp, tap, flip, transform, rotate, counter, note, attach's card, detach, pile, toZone, remove, hide, settle/tidy cards listed, stackPush of a card, lookAt of a card, draw, and undo as its inverse). Pings, lines, copies, ability sources and an attach's target only refer to a card. A card is another player's when another seat controls it (sits in their territory) in the state the action applied to. So using a stolen card you control does not flash, and taking your own card back from their side does. Every browser flashes, for the ping fade time, as a ring in the toucher's seat colour with their name, drawn over the scene's drawn rectangle (`scene.debug.cards()`). Cards the scene does not draw get no ring. `window.__spellplane.chat.flashes()` / `activeFlashes()` expose the flashes (declaration merging in ui/chat/debug.ts, no contract change). History before the HUD mounted never flashes.
- [M1-P6] M0-G log findings: a line names a card this browser could see just before the action (prefilled into the frozen-name memo from the HUD's previous state by ui/chat/watch.ts) or right after it. So the owner's log names the hand card it bottoms after a mulligan, and a public card bounced to a hand keeps its name, while peers read "a card". A card with no known identity now reads "a card" in the log instead of "Hidden card". A `lookAt` of every card of a zone reads "searches their library" (frozen per line, since the zone size changes later).
- [M1-P6] Own chat lines that the session log does not hold yet (a networked session before sequencing) are merged in as pending entries and dropped once the log has their cid. The solo session logs them at once.
- [M1-P6] Integrator: touched non-owned UI files through small hooks. Hud.svelte: LogWatch in the subscription, the `logName` null-for-hidden rule, merging pending chat, LogPanel props, and the Flashes overlay. LogPanel.svelte: FilterBar, ChatLines, the tab-aware send. describe.ts: the named Seg variant, `LogLine.channel`, `entryKeyOf`, the frozen search wording, and chat parsing. No contract file changed. No hidden information is sent or stored, because names come from this browser's own view, so there is no new leak-test scenario.
- [M1-P6] Describing log lines stays in ui/describe.ts (its M0-G rules are kept); core/log.ts holds the pure parse, merge, filter and attribution functions — the plan's 'describe/merge' was split this way because describe.ts already existed and is pure.
- [M1-P6] [[name]] also accepts [[name|SET]]. Blank, unclosed or over-160-character references, and those containing a bracket or line break, stay plain text. Names resolve lazily on hover or focus through CardDataSource.resolveDeck, cached per page; failed requests are not cached.
