There is no card-data API to look up a single name, and a one-line deck reuses the decklist's matching rules.
- [M1-P6] The filter (All/Chat/Actions) is a per-page view and is not persisted. 'Actions' keeps system lines. Chat lines show only on their own tab; action lines show on every tab.
- [M1-P6] Chat tabs appear only when there is more than one: 'team' when the own seat has a team; the spectator tab waits for spectators (M3/M5). Team chat is still a sequenced chat action every peer receives, so its secrecy is open for M3.
- [M1-P6] An action touches the cards it changes (including draw, a peek at a single card, casting a card, and undo as its inverse). Pings, lines, copies, ability sources and an attach target only refer to a card. A card is another player's when another seat controls it in the state before the action, so using a stolen card you control does not flash, but taking your own card back from their side does — the CardInstance controller is the seat whose territory the card sits in.
- [M1-P6] The flash shows on every browser for the ping fade time, as a ring in the toucher's seat colour with their name (so it does not rely on colour alone). It is drawn over the scene's drawn rectangle (scene.debug.cards()); cards the scene does not draw are recorded in the debug hook only. History from before the HUD mounted never flashes. The scene renders no pings yet, so the HUD draws the flash itself.
- [M1-P6] A log line names a card this browser could see just before the action (the watch fills the frozen-name memo from the HUD's previous state) or just after it. Unknown cards read 'a card' instead of 'Hidden card'. This fixes the mulligan-bottom finding generally (a public card bounced to a hand keeps its name too) without ever naming library faces.
- [M1-P6] A lookAt of every card in a zone reads 'searches <zone>', frozen per line through the name memo. LookAtAction has no search flag, and adding one would change a contract file and the session's lookAt.
- [M1-P6] Own chat lines the session log does not hold yet are merged in as pending entries and dropped once the log has their cid. The solo session logs them at once; this is for M1-P8's optimistic networked session.
- [M1-P6] Chat input is capped at 500 characters.
- [M1-P6] No hidden information is sent or stored: names come only from this browser's own view, and chat sends only the typed text. So there is no new leak-test scenario.
- [M1-P4] A block is a left-drag (plain or Alt) of another seat's battlefield card(s) onto an attacking card or one of its blockers, dispatched as moveTemp `combat: 'block'` per blocker plus a block line blocker → attacker in one group (scene/combat.ts); it takes precedence over drop rules 2–4 only there — DESIGN Combat shortcuts 3 names the drag onto an attacker as the block gesture and the pair as one step.
- [M1-P4] The blocker fan is centred on the attacker, 96 mm toward the defender, 69 mm apart, ordered left to right as the attacking player sees it — the attacking player orders damage, and the geometry spec gives 96/69.
- [M1-P4] The damage order is not stored; it is read from the blockers' poses along the fan axis — the order is visible on the table already and a second copy could disagree with it.
- [M1-P4] A blocker dropped onto an attacker, a fan member, or inside its own fan's footprint (its spots grown by half a card and the snap radius) reorders that fan or changes nothing; dropped anywhere else it unblocks (block line removed first in the same group, fan closes up) — dragging a blocker away is the natural undo of a block, and putting a lone blocker back near its spot must not silently unblock it.
- [M1-P4] In two-player games A draws no attack line; right-drag from an attacker to an opposing avatar or permanent draws (or replaces) its attack line, to anything else a target line, and an attacker without one shows a dashed stub only when its controller has several opponents — with one opponent the attack target is implicit, and abilities of attackers still need target lines.
- [M1-P4] Lines by mouse: right-drag from a public card (or its stack item) to a public card or avatar spot draws a target line, Shift+right-drag a mark, and drawing an existing line again removes it — the design gives right-drag for lines and needs a manual removal without another gesture.
- [M1-P4] The context menu opens on release of a right press that did not drag and is ignored for 400 ms after a line — right-drag and right-click share the button.
- [M1-P4] The attachment line is derived, not state: drawn while an attached card's centre is over 30 mm from its host's; an explicit `attach` state line replaces it — the design shows it only while the pair is pulled apart, which is a function of poses.
- [M1-P4] Strokes end 1.5 mm outside the cards or avatar circle they join, with the theme's seat dash (px converted to mm at the current zoom) and arrowhead; labels sit upright at the midpoint and scale like counter badges — lines must not hide the card faces, and dashes pair with seat colours for accessibility.
- [M1-P4] Paper's line creation pulse and glow are not drawn — cosmetic, deferred to the theme polish.
- [M1-P4] A ping is a ring just outside the pinged card (a circle at an avatar spot) in the pinger's seat colour, pulsing out twice over the first second (2 to 6 mm), then resting 2 mm out and fading linearly over pingFadeMs; no pulse with reduced motion — the design asks for a short pulse fading after two seconds.
- [M1-P4] Each ping has its own line layer — a layer's opacity applies to all its lines, so a shared layer would keep an older ping at full strength while a newer one shows.
- [M1-P4] Settle animates with the settle timing (260 ms, 20 ms stagger per card; scene/motion.ts gained the 'settle' MoveKind and a start delay), and an attachment whose host goes home rides in its host's stagger slot — the geometry spec fixes settle timing, and an attachment moving on its own would tear away from its host.
- [M1-P4] Core settle sends hosts home before their attachments — otherwise a displaced attachment of a displaced host is carried off its home by the host's later move.
- [M1-P4] moveTemp of a card carried along by a displaced host records as home its pose minus that nearest displaced ancestor's translation (pose − home) — the host's temporary spot is not the card's slot, and settle moves it by exactly that translation.
- [M1-P4] Fan poses are quantised to 0.01 mm like the reducer's stored poses — so an unchanged blocker is recognised and re-fanning dispatches no no-op moveTemps at seat angles off the right angles.
- [M1-P4] Debug hook: `lines()` entries carry `ends: {from, to}` (null while an end is not drawn); new parts `drawnLines()` and `pings()` are added by declaration merging in scene/lines.ts — additive and type-safe without editing a contract file; the next contracts package may fold them into util/debug.ts.
- [M1-P4] Edits outside the package's files are call sites only: scene/table-scene.ts wires a LineView (scene/lines.ts) and the block drop and settle motion (scene/combat.ts, a new file), and scene/motion.ts gained the 'settle' kind — the package needs them wired, and keeping the logic in its own files limits merge conflicts.
- [M1-P4] A blocker no longer on a battlefield keeps its block line until settle but leaves the fan (blockersOf counts only battlefield blockers) — lines drop only on entering a hidden zone, and re-fanning must not pull a dead creature back onto the table.
- [M1-P4] Blocking needs an opposing seat (not the same seat, not a Two-Headed Giant teammate); a teammate's creature dropped onto your attacker is an ordinary drop — teammates do not block each other.
- [M1-P4] Open follow-ups left to their owners: touch long-press-drag lines (M5-P1 Touch), entering a line label (labels are drawn, no UI sets one; HUD/context menu), attach-by-drop of an Aura or Equipment (card-manipulation drop rules), and A on a selection should skip a card whose host is also selected (ui/commands.ts; today the Aura is also tapped and marked 'attack', though it now settles home correctly) — those files are outside this package.
- [M1-P4] A block is a left-drag (plain or Alt) of another seat's battlefield card(s) onto an attacker or onto one of its blockers. It takes precedence over pile drop rules 2–4 only in that case — the design's gesture, and these are the only drops that are not piles.
- [M1-P4] The blocker fan is centred, 96 mm toward the defender and 69 mm apart. Left to right is as the attacking player sees it, because the attacker orders damage. The order is read from the blockers' poses, not stored — no new state is needed.
- [M1-P4] A blocker enters the order where it is dropped. Dropping it on its own fan reorders it, dropping it on another attacker moves the block there, and dragging it elsewhere removes its block line and closes the fan — this is 'rearranged by dragging'.
- [M1-P4] In two-player games A draws no attack line, because the target is implicit. The dashed stub appears only when the controller has more than one opponent — design: 'In two-player games the target is implicit'.
- [M1-P4] Right-drag from an attacker draws its attack line and replaces any earlier one; from other cards it draws a target line, and Shift+right-drag a mark. Drawing an existing line again removes it — the design gives no other manual removal path.
- [M1-P4] The context menu opens when a right press is released without dragging, and is ignored for 400 ms after a line is drawn — so right-drag works whether the platform fires the menu on press (macOS) or on release.
- [M1-P4] The attachment line is worked out, not stored: it shows while an attached card is more than 30 mm from its host's centre. An explicit attach line in the state replaces it — 'shows an attachment line when pulled apart' with no new state.
- [M1-P4] Line strokes stop 1.5 mm outside the cards they join. Theme dashes given in px are converted to mm at the current zoom. Labels scale up like counter badges at far zoom. Paper's creation pulse and glow are not drawn — readability first; the pulse is cosmetic.
- [M1-P4] A ping is a ring around the card (a circle at a seat's avatar spot) in the pinger's seat colour. It pulses and fades linearly over pingFadeMs (2 s); with reduced motion it fades without pulsing.
- [M1-P4] Settle animates with settleMs and settleStaggerMs. scene/motion.ts gained a 'settle' move kind and a start delay — the design's motion spec.
- [M1-P4] Core settle sends hosts home before their attachments — otherwise a displaced attachment of a displaced host ended off its home slot, depending on id order.
- [M1-P4] Debug hook: lines() entries carry `ends`. New parts drawnLines() and pings() are added by declaration merging in scene/lines.ts — the acceptance test asserts on debug endpoints, and no contract file needed to change.
- [M1-P4] Integrator: the wiring had to go into files outside my list — scene/table-scene.ts (lines, pings, right-drag, block drop, settle motion) and scene/motion.ts. New file: scene/combat.ts. No HUD changes were needed (the A, Shift+A, B and I bindings already existed).
- [M1-P4] Open: dropping an Aura or Equipment onto a card does not attach it (the scene's drop rules have no attach case), and long-press-and-drag lines on touch are not implemented.
- [M1-P4] A blocker no longer on a battlefield keeps its block line until Settle but is not part of the fan (blockersOf counts only battlefield blockers) — design: dead cards go to the graveyard by hand; Settle returns survivors
- [M1-P4] Dragging a blocker anywhere but a fan unblocks it before the drop, in the same group — a drop into a hidden zone drops lines itself, so a later lineRemove would be refused
- [M1-P4] Blocking needs an opposing seat (not the same seat, not a 2HG teammate); a teammate's creature onto your attacker is an ordinary drop — DESIGN Block: 'A defender drags a creature onto an attacker'
- [M1-P4] Settle motion: an attachment of a host going home rides along in the host's stagger slot with the settle timing — keeps Aura/Equipment with its host during settle
- [M1-P4] Right-drag from an attacker draws an attack line only to an opposing seat's avatar or an opposing permanent, a target line otherwise; the preview with nothing under the pointer is an attack line — Connection lines: Target = right-drag from a card; attackers still need ability targets
- [M1-P4] Ping ring pulses twice over the first second (2 to 6 mm), then rests 2 mm out while fading over pingFadeMs — matches the documented behaviour
- [M1-P4] Line/ping drawing lives in a LineView in scene/lines.ts; table-scene.ts keeps call sites only — reduces conflicts with M1-P3/M1-P5 wiring into table-scene.ts
- [M1-P4] Open follow-ups: touch long-press-drag lines (M5-P1), line label entry UI (HUD/context-menu owner), attach-by-drop (card-manipulation drop rules) — out of package scope
- [M1-P4] moveTemp of a card carried along by a displaced host records as home its pose minus that nearest displaced ancestor's (pose - home) — the host's temporary spot is not the card's slot, and settle moves it by exactly that translation
- [M1-P4] Fan poses are quantised to 0.01 mm like the reducer's stored poses — so an unchanged blocker is recognised and re-fanning dispatches no no-op moveTemps at angled seats
- [M1-P4] A blocker dropped inside its own fan's footprint (spots grown by half a card plus the snap radius) with nothing under the pointer reorders the fan or changes nothing; elsewhere it unblocks — putting a lone blocker back near its spot must not silently unblock it
- [M1-P4] Each ping has its own line layer — a layer's opacity applies to all its lines, so a shared layer kept older pings at full strength
- [M1-P3] A confirmation round waits for every occupied seat (a seat with a name) other than the asker; empty chairs (solo's second seat, a seat that left) never stall it, so a solo round has passed at once and Resolve needs no second press there — DESIGN "Every other player sees a prompt".
- [M1-P3] A round stores the table's pass timer at its start (`durationMs`, 0 when off) and `startSeq` = the action's seq (`seq + 1` on an optimistic apply); a host change of the timer applies to later rounds. Seats with auto-pass answer OK when a round opens, and turning auto-pass on passes the open round; Hold clears it — simplest reading of "pass until end of turn".
- [M1-P3] One seat holds at a time (a second Hold is refused while the holder is present). Release, or OK from the holder, passes and restarts the countdown from that action (`timerSeq`). A late Hold after passing is allowed while the item is on the stack (nothing is ever blocked); the prompt only offers it while unanswered.
- [M1-P3] Timeouts: the sequencer deadline (`confirm:<round>:<timerStartSeq>:<waiting seats>`) emits `respond timeout` for every seat still waited for. A hold by a seat that is away (`Seat.away`, M1-P7) or has left no longer stops the clock and that seat times out too, counted from the round's start or last release (it usually fires as soon as the seat is marked away, since away takes 6 s) — "a dropped friend never stalls the table". The reducer accepts a `timeout` from any seat (state does not say who sequences); M1-P1's sequencer must refuse a proposed `respond timeout` from anyone but itself and emit it only from its own deadlines (integrator: give M1-P1 an acceptance test that a peer's proposed `respond timeout` is nacked).
- [M1-P3] Resolving the item of a round that still waits is refused without `force` (the HUD's second R within 3 s); counter, reorder and dragging out are never held back. So every early resolve is in the log as "resolves … before everyone passed", and a hold sequenced before a plain resolve makes that resolve fail (nack) instead of being overrun.
- [M1-P3] A round about a stack item ends when the item leaves the stack, however it leaves (core/reducer/draft.ts `finish`); a new round replaces the open one, so after the top item resolves the item below has no open round and Resolve is lit. The round is invisible to personal undo (core/undo.ts `undoKey` sets `confirm` aside, as answers are not undoable): undoing a resolve or counter brings the item back without the round that ended with it, undoing a push ends the round about its item, and a round that push replaced stays replaced.
- [M1-P3] core/reducer/draft.ts gained `confirm`/`setConfirm` (the Draft had no confirmation round); ui/commands.ts `roundPassed`/`promptShowing` and resolveTop delegate to core/reducer/confirm.ts; scene/gestures.ts gives a drop onto the stack a round id; scene/layout.ts places a spell card in its item's slot; ui/describe.ts attributes a `respond` line to the answering seat (a timeout is sent by the sequencer); Avatar gains an optional `asking` tag that pulses for a manual Ask.
- [M1-P3] Stack lane: markers (ability, trigger, copy) are drawn as the face of their source card (or of what a copy copies) in their cascade slot, unaltered, with a line to the source; number and kind sit left of each title strip, the note right of it, in the controller's seat colour; every item gets a seat-coloured outline that pulses while its round waits (steady with reduced motion); lines anchor on an item's visible title strip; hovering an item highlights its source and target cards. The far-zoom count badge is not built (parked for the integrator; the lane label shows the count). The debug hook gains `stack()` (scene/stack.ts, by declaration merging).
- [M1-P3] Manual Ask: K opens an inline, non-modal Ask field (like the note field); Enter asks with the typed label, or none when empty, Esc cancels without asking (Esc dismisses everywhere else too). The action accepts an 80-character label and the prompt shows it. Test page `?harness=confirm` (src/ui/confirm/harness.ts, registered in main.ts) runs the real HUD and scene over an in-page sequencer with the real reducer and deadline timers; the sim tests (tests/sim/confirm) use a minimal loopback sequencer, not M1-P1's engine, and stand in for M1-P7's `away` handler.
- [M1-P3] Markers are drawn at a uniform 0.6 scale (whole and undistorted), top edge level with their slot so name and art stay in the visible strip, so an ability or copy no longer looks like a cast card; `CardDraw` gained an optional uniform `scale` (scene/cards.ts) and math.ts `pointInCard`/`cardCorners` take any size.
- [M1-P3] A marker is hit-tested on its own; the scene reports the hovered stack item through `hoveredStackItem(scene)` (scene/stack.ts, a WeakMap, so the TableScene contract is unchanged). While a marker is hovered and no card is, N notes it, X copies it, I pings it, and card keys do nothing (never the selection).
- [M1-P3] Dragging a marker: dropped in the lane it takes the slot under the pointer (`stackReorder`); dropped anywhere else it leaves the stack, i.e. is removed — `stackCounter` when on top, else `stackReorder` to the top plus `stackCounter` with one `group` (the contract only counters the top item), so it is one undo step.
- [M1-P3] The prompt hides Hold (and H/Esc send nothing) while another present seat holds the floor; the countdown ticks only while the timer runs; the asker's "All passed" pill shows for 4 s (the round itself stays open until its item leaves or a new round starts). The scene keeps redrawing while an item's round waits (the pulse), as designed.
- [M1-P3] Edits outside the package's files, for the integrator to confirm before merging: core/undo.ts (`undoKey`), core/reducer/draft.ts (`confirm`, also touched by M1-P4's lines), core/index.ts, scene/{table-scene,layout,gestures,cards,math}.ts, ui/{Hud,Avatar}.svelte, ui/commands.ts, ui/describe.ts and main.ts (the `?harness=confirm` entry; main.ts belongs to M1-P8).
- [M1-P3] A round waits for every seated player (a seat with a name) other than the asker — empty chairs never stall a round, so in solo play it has passed at once and Resolve needs no second press
- [M1-P3] A round keeps the pass timer in force when it starts (0 = off) and `startSeq` = the action's seq (seq+1 when applied before sequencing); changing the timer mid-game affects later rounds — simplest reading of the design
- [M1-P3] Auto-pass answers OK when a round opens, and turning it on passes the open round; Hold clears it — 'pass until end of turn'
- [M1-P3] Only one seat holds at a time (a second Hold is refused while the holder is present); Release, or OK from the holder, passes and restarts the countdown from that action via `timerSeq`
- [M1-P3] A hold by a seat that is away or has left no longer stops the clock, and that seat times out, counted from the round's start or last release — a dropped friend never stalls the table
- [M1-P3] Resolving the item of a round that still waits is refused without `force` (the second press), so every early resolve is in the log; counter, reorder and dragging out are never blocked
- [M1-P3] A round about a stack item ends when the item leaves the stack (done in Draft.finish), and a new round replaces the open one; undoing a push restores the earlier state only when no other round was open before it, otherwise that push is not undoable
- [M1-P3] A late Hold after passing is accepted while the item is still on the stack (nothing is ever blocked); the prompt only offers Hold while the seat has not answered
- [M1-P3] Markers are drawn as their source card's face (or, for a copy, the face of what it copies), unaltered, in their cascade slot with a line to the source; number and kind sit beside the title strip and the outline is in the controller's seat colour, so faces are never recoloured
- [M1-P3] Markers are not cards, so X/N/Q do not act on a hovered marker (only on spell cards and through the top-item buttons); the far-zoom count badge is not built because the lane label already shows the count
- [M1-P3] Manual Ask (K) sends no label (the action accepts an 80-character label and the prompt shows it); for a manual round the asker's avatar shows a pulsing 'asks' tag
- [M1-P3] The reducer accepts `respond timeout` from any seat; M1-P1's sequencer should refuse a proposed timeout from anyone but itself
- [M1-P3] Small edits outside the owned files: core/reducer/draft.ts gains confirm/setConfirm; ui/commands.ts roundPassed, promptShowing and resolveTop delegate to core; scene/gestures.ts adds a round id when a card is dropped on the stack; scene/layout.ts places spell cards by stack slot; ui/describe.ts credits a respond line to the answering seat; Avatar.svelte gains an optional `asking` tag; Hud.svelte mounts ConfirmPrompt; main.ts registers ?harness=confirm; tests/unit/core/reducer.test.ts drops ask, respond and autoPass from its not-yet-implemented list
- [M1-P3] The sim tests use a minimal loopback sequencer (not M1-P1's engine) and a stand-in for M1-P7's `away` handler
- [M1-P3] Personal undo ignores the confirmation round: core/undo.ts `undoKey` sets `confirm` aside, because answers are not undoable. Undoing a resolve or counter brings the item back without its round. Undoing a push ends that push's round, and a round the push replaced stays replaced. This edits core/undo.ts (M0-P1's file, not a contract) because RestorePatch, which is a contract, cannot carry a round.
- [M1-P3] The confirmation deadline key includes the waiting seats (`confirm:<round>:<from>:<seats>`), so the engine re-arms with a fresh emit whenever that set changes.
- [M1-P3] Markers are drawn at a uniform 0.6 scale, with their top edge level with their slot; `CardDraw` gained an optional uniform `scale`.
- [M1-P3] The hovered stack item is exposed through `hoveredStackItem(scene)`, a WeakMap registry in scene/stack.ts, so the TableScene contract does not change. Over a marker, N, X and I act on it, and card keys do nothing.
- [M1-P3] A marker dropped outside the lane is removed. A buried marker gets `stackReorder` to the top plus `stackCounter` in one group, because the contract only counters the top item.
- [M1-P3] K opens an inline Ask field: Enter asks, with or without a label, and Esc cancels. The finding suggested 'Esc sends without a label'; I chose Esc cancels, because Esc dismisses everywhere else in the HUD.
- [M1-P3] The prompt hides Hold while another present seat holds the floor. The countdown ticks only while the timer runs. 'All passed' shows for 4 s.
- [M1-P3] Integration: M1-P4 moved line drawing into scene/lines.ts `LineView`, so M1-P3's stack-lane line work moved there as two optional `LineViewHost` hooks — `stackEnd(item)` (a stack item's line end at its visible title strip or marker slot, drawn from the lane's layout) and `extraLines()` (marker-to-source strokes on the same layer); without them LineView behaves as before.
- [M1-P5] Room links: `newRoom` draws three words from a 128-word list (one byte each, no bias) and a number 10–99 by rejection sampling; the key is 16 random bytes base64url. `parseRoomLink` finds `#/t/<room>/<key>` anywhere in pasted text (bare fragment, bare path, trailing punctuation, the design example's `k=` prefix), re-bases it on this page, and refuses a key that is not exactly 22 base64url characters (it is the signalling password). Share links drop this page's query (`?profile=`, `?transport=` are per browser).
- [M1-P5] Seat colours are unique among occupied chairs (cursors, lines and log names must stay apart); two players who pick one colour at once: the higher seat index switches to the lowest free colour. Free chairs never count (their seq-0 colours are defaults). Nicknames: trimmed, whitespace collapsed, control characters dropped, 1–24 characters.
- [M1-P5] Chairs: a player may move to a free chair until Ready (the commitment and library belong to the chair) and not after the game started; there is no un-Ready in M1. The seat shuffle of the design is deferred to M3 (moving other players needs a protocol; with two seats "who goes first" is the firstPlayer roll).
- [M1-P5] The host is the present, non-away seated player with the lowest seat index (the sequencer rule); the host's "Start the game" (`stageSet match`) is enabled once every chair of the format is taken and every seated player is Ready.
- [M1-P5] Connectivity check: a condition counts as a failure after 15 s (`CONNECT_CHECK_MS`): joining that never completes (relays), being alone in the room, a peer stuck in the handshake, or a seated, non-away player whose peer this browser has no connection to while it has others (per pair). Each message names the remedies (other network, phone hotspot, VPN) and says there is no relay server. A version mismatch is shown at once.
- [M1-P5] `#/diag` joins a `diag-<words>-NN` room (never a table room) and exchanges `diagPing`/`diagPong` on `ctl` every 2 s; it reports join time, per-peer connect time and the latest round trip, and fails plainly after 20 s without relays or without a peer. New route `#/diag/<room>/<key>` joins a check another machine started (added to ui/routes.ts as `diagJoin`).
- [M1-P5] Presence: scene/presence.ts draws remote cursors (arrow plus name in seat colour) and dashed outlines of remotely dragged cards on a pointer-transparent canvas over the scene host, projected through `worldToScreen` (works for every rig); drag outlines are lifted 12 mm. The canvas runs a frame loop only while some remote presence is drawable (none on the solo table) and clears once when the last shape goes. A presence silent for 30 s is no longer drawn; the sender re-sends a resting cursor every 10 s (`PRESENCE_RESEND_MS`), so only a vanished peer goes stale. Hud.svelte calls `attachPresence` after creating the scene and `beginExternalDrag` next to each `scene.startDrag` (hand fan, pile viewer).
- [M1-P5] Drag ghosts hang off the pointer as the scene draws the cards: at drag start the sender reads the displayed poses from the scene's `debug.cards()` (part of the TableScene contract; the scene's `dragging` flag, when present, gives its own drag set incl. squared piles), keeps each card's offset from the press point and sends pointer + offset with the displayed rot. TableState poses are not used (a card in a squared pile or the stack lane is drawn at the anchor, not at its stored pose). Cards of public zones are shown; a card the HUD hands out of an 'owner' zone of the own seat (hand, sideboard) is shown by its opaque id, which shared state already lists; library cards ('nobody') never are (their id would leak the order). No identity ever travels on `pres`.
- [M1-P5] App.svelte only branches on the route: `#/lobby` → ui/lobby/StartRoute.svelte, `#/diag` → ui/diag/DiagRoute.svelte, `#/t/<room>/<key>` → ui/lobby/RoomRoute.svelte (TableRoom: lobby while the stage is 'lobby', then the HUD) with AppProps passed through; the page frame style is `:global(.sp-page)`. RoomRoute uses `AppProps.link` when it names this room and key, else derives the link from the fragment; it takes main.ts's session when its `roomId` matches. Until M1-P8 does, dev and test builds with a dev transport (`?transport=local`, or `window.__spellplaneTransport = 'silent' | 'unreachable'` from an init script) join through ui/lobby/local-session.ts, a stand-in TableSession (best-ranked peer — highest seq, then lowest peer id — sequences through core's reducer and broadcasts the state; Ready publishes commitments only; real `pres` messages), lazily imported, absent from production. M1-P8 replaces it by passing its NetSession (and may delete local-session.ts and dev-room.ts). Integrator: please acknowledge the shell (App.svelte, Hud.svelte) and tests/e2e/ui/hud.spec.ts edits outside this package.
- [M1-P5] Ready with a pasted list: a list with no resolved card is refused with a message; unresolved lines need a second press on Ready (the message says they will be left out); the list is saved to the deck library only after Ready went through, under an id hashed from the text (`pasted-<fnv64>`), so a retried Ready never saves it twice.
- [M1-P5] Integrator / M1-P8: (1) the lobby renames by `identity.setNickname` then `session.takeSeat(seat, colour)`, so the real `takeSeat` must read the current nickname from the IdentityStore, not the snapshot in NetSessionOptions; (2) a fresh identity's nickname is '' and the reducer refuses an empty seat name, so the session must fall back to a default ('Player'); (3) `takeSeat` to another chair should take the new chair before leaving the old one (the stand-in does; a browser without a chair would be re-seated in between).
- [M1-P5] Playwright WebKit on macOS blocks the page's main thread when a CryptoKey is put into IndexedDB (WebKit wraps serialized keys with a keychain master key), so every page that reads the identity (`#/lobby`, a table room) hangs there. The lobby e2e tests skip on WebKit only when `process.platform === 'darwin'` (they run on Chromium and on Linux WebKit in CI), and the M0 route test's `#/lobby` case does the same. Open for the integrator: M1-P8/M1-G browser tests on macOS WebKit will hit it too.
- [M1-P5] Connectivity check, being alone: with no other player in the shared seats nobody may have opened the link yet, so after 15 s the lobby shows a neutral `role=status` hint (waiting, with the remedies) and escalates to the `role=alert` failure only after 120 s (`ALONE_ALERT_MS`); when the shared seats list another present player this browser cannot reach, the failure still comes after 15 s.
- [M1-P5] Presence bursts go out in sequence order (the throttle re-inserts each kind, so a drag end queued before a later cursor is not overtaken and dropped as stale); since `pres` is unreliable, the sender says a drag end once more with the next cursor it sends (a move, a leave or the 10 s resting-cursor resend).
- [M1-P5] Moving to another chair keeps the player's seat colour: the chair being left does not count as using it (`pickColour(..., leaving)`); another player's colour still does.
- [M1-P5] `#/diag` drops a peer from the report when the transport says it left (the other machine closed the check or dropped off) and forgets pings unanswered for three ping intervals.
- [M1-P5] For the M1 gate report (docs/gates/M1.md, written at M1-G): (1) design deviation — the seat shuffle ("can shuffle seats randomly", DESIGN "Players, seats and formats") is deferred to M3; (2) owner check — the lobby e2e tests and the `#/lobby` route test skip on macOS WebKit (CryptoKey in IndexedDB hangs) and run there only on Linux WebKit in CI.
- [M1-P5] Room id = three words from a 128-word list plus a number 10-99 drawn by rejection sampling; key = 16 random bytes base64url. parseRoomLink finds #/t/<room>/<key> anywhere in pasted text, accepts the k= prefix, and refuses keys that are not exactly 22 base64url characters. Share links drop this page's query (?profile=, ?transport=) — these flags belong to one browser only.
- [M1-P5] Seat colours are unique among occupied chairs. If two players pick the same colour at once, the higher seat index moves to the lowest free colour. Nickname is 1-24 characters after trimming. — cursors, lines and log names must stay distinguishable.
- [M1-P5] A player can move to a free chair until Ready, not after, and there is no un-Ready. The design's seat shuffle is deferred to M3. — the commitment and library belong to the chair; moving other players needs a protocol.
- [M1-P5] The host is the present, non-away seated player with the lowest seat index. 'Start the game' (stageSet match) is enabled when every chair is taken and everyone is Ready. — same rule as the sequencer; the design says the host advances from the lobby.
- [M1-P5] Connectivity failures show after 15 s: relays unreachable, alone in the room, a handshake that never finishes, or a seated player this browser cannot reach while it can reach others. Each message names the remedies and says there is no relay server; a version mismatch shows at once. — design: check per pair, say it plainly.
- [M1-P5] #/diag uses its own diag-<words>-NN rooms with diagPing/diagPong on ctl every 2 s, and fails plainly after 20 s. Added route #/diag/<room>/<key> (diagJoin) so a second machine can join. — the plan's connectivity check.
- [M1-P5] scene/presence.ts draws remote cursors and drag outlines on a click-through canvas projected with worldToScreen. A peer silent for 30 s is no longer drawn. Your own drags are inferred from the pointer on the scene host (4 px threshold, the selection, public zones only). — TableScene has no drag events and scene/ may not import net/.
- [M1-P5] App.svelte uses props.session for a room only when its roomId matches the link. Otherwise dev and test builds with a dev transport (?transport=local, or window.__spellplaneTransport = 'silent' | 'unreachable' from an init script) use the stand-in session. — the e2e tests use only the UI and the debug hook, so they also hold once M1-P8's real session is wired in.
- [M1-P5] Integrator/M1-P8: takeSeat must read the current nickname from IdentityStore, not the NetSessionOptions snapshot. It must fall back to a non-empty name because a fresh identity's nickname is '' and the reducer refuses it. A chair move should take the new chair before leaving the old one. — needed for the lobby's rename and chair moves to work.
- [M1-P5] Playwright WebKit on macOS freezes the page when a CryptoKey is stored in IndexedDB, so the 8 lobby tests that read the identity skip only on webkit+darwin. — a test-environment limit; M1-P8 and the gate will hit it too.
- [M1-P5] Drag ghosts use the poses the scene draws, read from scene.debug.cards() (part of the TableScene contract) at drag start, and keep each card's offset from the press point. The scene's 'dragging' flag, when present, picks the drag set (this covers squared piles). Reason: TableState poses are wrong for pile and stack cards, and scene/types.ts needs no change.
- [M1-P5] An external drag (hand fan, pile viewer) can show a card from an 'owner' zone of the player's own seat, by its opaque id only (shared state already lists that id). 'nobody' zones (library) are never shown. Reason: the hand order and the library order must not leak; position is the only intent shown.
- [M1-P5] The presence sender re-sends a resting cursor every 10 s; the 30 s staleness cut-off stays for peers that vanish. Reason: the net engine's presence does not yet promise to drop peers that disconnect, and this works with either implementation.
- [M1-P5] The presence canvas loops only while some remote presence can be drawn. Reason: render only when needed; no per-frame layout reads on the solo table.
- [M1-P5] Ready with a pasted list refuses an empty result and needs a second press when lines did not resolve. The list is saved after Ready succeeds, under an id hashed from the text. Reason: no silent failures and no duplicate saved decks.
- [M1-P5] App.svelte only branches on routes, to StartRoute, DiagRoute and RoomRoute. RoomRoute prefers AppProps.link when it matches the room. Reason: keeps lobby logic out of the shared shell and honours the M1-C link field.
- [M1-P5] Alone with no other seated player: a neutral status hint after 15 s and an alert only after 120 s (ALONE_ALERT_MS). If another present seated player is listed but cannot be reached, the alert comes after 15 s. Reason: before anyone joins, there is no pair that could have failed.
- [M1-P5] The presence throttle sends in sequence order, and the sender repeats a drag end once with the next cursor. Reason: pres is unreliable and the receiver drops older n.
- [M1-P5] Moving chairs keeps the seat colour (the chair being left does not count as using it). Reason: the player picked that colour.
- [M1-P5] #/diag drops peers on 'leave' and prunes pings unanswered after 3 intervals. Reason: otherwise the report claims a connection that has ended.
- [M1-P5] The first full pnpm e2e run took 13.5 min and failed 22 Chromium tests, mostly scene and solo specs this package did not touch, most likely because of machine load. The immediate full rerun passed everything (266 passed, 8 skipped, 48.7 s). Reason: reported so the integrator knows. No retries were added.
- [M1] The identity key pair is stored as JWK in the `identity` store and imported with a non-extractable private key when loaded (`StoredIdentity`, additive store contract change by the main session). Storing CryptoKey objects in IndexedDB makes WebKit fetch a "WebCrypto Master Key" from the macOS keychain, which raised repeated keychain dialogs from Playwright's WebKit during the unattended run. The design only asks for a key pair kept in the browser. Records from earlier builds (CryptoKey pair) are still read.
- [M1-G] The gate's two players are two pages of one browser context, kept apart by `?profile=` (own IndexedDB, own key pair). Reason: BroadcastChannel (`?transport=local`) only joins pages of one context, so two contexts never meet.
- [M1-G] The scripted game is one test whose steps are recorded: a failed step is listed, the game goes on where it can, and the test fails at the end with the full list (also written to `artifacts/gate/M1-<project>.json`). Reason: one run shows every gap and takes all the screenshots it can reach; no step is retried.
- [M1-G] Scripted cards (a land, a creature, Essence Scatter, Titanic Growth, Shock) reach the hand through the library viewer's Search and H, as a player tutoring would. Reason: shuffles are truly random, so the script cannot rely on draws.
- [M1-G] Putting a hand card onto the battlefield face down has no UI path; the gate calls `__spellplane.session.playFaceDown` and lists it as a gap. Reason: the design names Reveal to… and F for public cards but no gesture for a face-down play from hand.
- [M1-G] The leak scan forbids the salts of every card left in the owner's hand or library, and their Scryfall ids unless another copy of the same card is public or was revealed to the viewer. Reason: a public copy makes the id public knowledge without leaking the hidden card.
- [M1-G fix] F on an own hand card puts it onto the own battlefield face down at a free slot through `TableSession.playFaceDown` (the card menu offers it as the flip entry); the gate uses that path instead of the debug hook, which also gained `session.playFaceDown` for solo and room tables. Reason: DESIGN "Table model" has face-down cards on the battlefield and F as the face-down key, and the gate found no UI path to start one.
- [M1-G fix] The confirmation prompt and the cheat sheet label Hold with H (H is now the first `confirmHold` binding; Esc still holds). Reason: DESIGN "Turn flow and confirmation" names H while the prompt is up, and labels show the first binding.
- [M1-G fix] A page that opens mid-turn (reload, rejoin) offers untap-and-draw when the log's last `turnPass` handed this seat the turn and the seat has done nothing since, or when this tab had the offer open at the same turn number (sessionStorage, per tab, wrapped in try/catch). Reason: the offer must not be lost by a reload, and it is never done for the player.
- [M1-G fix] The fitted view frames the table plus the hand rows of every other taken seat (`scene/layout.ts` `fitFrame`, `BirdseyeRig.frame`); empty chairs add nothing, so the solo fit is unchanged. Reason: the opponent's row of backs lies outside the table edge and sat under the turn strip; the screen-anchored layer must not cover table content.
- [M1-G fix] Zone labels keep at least 9 px of type on screen (`legibleScale`, up to 3x the 5.5 mm size, whose capital height still fits the 12 mm gap between piles). Reason: at the fitted view they were about 3 px high.
- [M1-G fix] The `away {seat, away}` reducer landed in core ahead of M1-P7: it marks a taken seat away or back, refuses a no-op and an empty chair, and `seatTake` clears it; who may send it stays the sequencer's check. The avatar shows an away seat (`data-away`, an "away" tag, dashed border). Reason: the gate's away-seat step and DESIGN "Resume": "A disconnected player's seat is marked away".
- [M1-G fix] The dev/test stand-in session (`ui/lobby/local-session.ts`, until M1-P8) now follows the TableSession contract for hidden cards: a `reveal` goes first when an own card becomes public, the secret store is reconciled with the zones after every applied action, London mulligan, targeted reveal sent to the chosen seat's peer only, `playFaceDown` checks, the sequencing browser marks a seat away after 6 s without its peer (a proposed `away` is refused), and the table and secret store are saved per room and restored on the next visit (the highest seq wins). Reason: the gate's HUD paths can be exercised before M1-P8; it is still no engine (no gap detection, secret shuffle protocol, commit-and-reveal dice, logged looks or `net` debug part) and is deleted when M1-P8 lands.
- [M1-G fix] Not done in the gate fix round: merging M1-P1/M1-P2 and building M1-P7/M1-P8. Reason: M1-P2 edits contract files (needs an M1-C amendment first), both branches carry unreviewed round-3 fixes, and M1-P7/M1-P8 were never started; the park notes' retry order stands.
- [M1-G fix] A prompt binding whose action can do nothing right now falls through to the table binding (`ui/keymap.ts` PromptKeys, `promptKeys` in commands.ts): while this seat holds the floor, or another seat holds it, H is "to hand" again, not Hold. Reason: a player who held to respond tutors or picks up cards with H; the gate's Essence Scatter tutor during Ben's hold was swallowed by the prompt.
- [M1-G fix] A right-drag that ends on a player's avatar in the HUD draws the line to that seat: the scene checks for a screen-layer element marked `data-line-seat` under the pointer before the table (the HUD avatar carries it). Reason: DESIGN "Combat and annotation" has lines from a card to a player, and players are shown by their HUD avatars; the table's avatar spot is hidden under the screen layer.
- [M1-G fix] The gate and the lobby test close a page with a plain `page.close()`. Reason: Playwright's WebKit never closes a page asked to `runBeforeUnload`, so the page stayed connected and its seat was never away.
- [M1-C] Amendment for the M1 retry: landed M1-P2's `Reissue` and optional `reissue` on `shuffleDone`/`reorder` (core/actions.ts) and `ReissueRecord` with optional `reissue` on `ShuffleRecord`/`ReorderRecord` (core/types.ts), copied verbatim from `m1-p2` so that branch merges without contract edits — reason: the M1-P2 park note; a package may not edit contract files.
- [M1-C] `NetEngine.pending(): readonly Action[]` (net/types.ts), the same queue as `SyncEngine.pending()` — reason: M1-P2's pair channel refuses a look at a zone a pending own action may change and fails closed without it; M1-P1's engine already implements it, and m1 has no NetEngine stub object to update. The answerer still reads `engine.confirmedState()`; no state parameter was added to `answerLooks`.
- [M1-C] Optional `prev?: string | null` on `ProposeMessage`: the cid of the proposer's previous still-pending proposal; the sequencer holds a proposal until `prev` is sequenced or refused, and M1-P1 decides how long it waits for a `prev` that never arrives — reason: per-proposer ordering under loss (M1-P1 park note); optional, so current senders stay valid.
- [M1-C] Doc fix: the handshake nonce a `welcome` signs is connection-bound, `<hello nonce>|<signer transport id>|<recipient transport id>` (M1-P1 `boundNonce`), stated on `HelloMessage`, `WelcomeMessage` and `handshakeText`; the function itself is unchanged — reason: M1-P1's round-2 relay fix.
- [M1-C] Lost-save recovery contract: public actions `recover {hand, round}` (by the recovering seat: discard every card in its hidden zones, drop unfinished shuffles of them, set `Seat.recovering {hand, round, deckSize, startSeq}`, open confirmation round `round`; sent again it re-proposes the count) and `recoverCommit {commits, instanceIds, zone}` (accepted once that round is current and passed; commits exactly `recoveryLibrarySize` fresh ids into the library as `ready` does, appends them to the seat's `Commitment` as a re-issue does, clears `Seat.recovering`); then M1-P2's ordinary secret shuffle and `draw` actions for the agreed hand. Both are family 'zone', not undoable, and refused by the reducer as "not implemented yet (M1-P7)" — reason: simplest flow that reuses the M1-P3 round (silence passes, a hold objects) and M1-P2's commitments and shuffle; no new wire message.
- [M1-C] Recovery arithmetic stubs in `core/recovery.ts` (M1-P7): `recoveryLibrarySize(state, seat)` = `deckSize` − the seat's non-token cards still on the table, and `recoveryRemainder(deck, state, seat)`, the owner's re-import minus public identities; for public face-down cards whose identity the owner lost, the owner drops cards of its choice and the audit cannot check that — reason: deck size is counted from cards, not commitments, because re-issues grow the commitment list; residual risk accepted among friends.
- [M1-C] For M1-P7: the reducer lives in new `core/reducer/recovery.ts` (replacing the two `later('M1-P7')` entries in `core/reducer/index.ts`); add log lines for `recover`/`recoverCommit` in `ui/describe.ts` (it returns null for them now); add the recovery scenario to the shared leak test; the sequencer's existing rule (hidden-zone actions come from the zone's owner, `by` is the sender's seat) covers both actions.
- [M1-C] PLAN: M1-P7 depends on M1-P1, M1-P2, M1-P8 and its files are `store/tables.ts` (not `store/table.ts`), `net/sync.ts`, `core/recovery.ts`, `core/reducer/recovery.ts`, `ui/recovery/*` — reason: M1-P7 park note; every acceptance criterion is networked and table persistence already lives in `store/tables.ts`.
- [M1-P2 retry] The round-3 review probes are permanent regression tests (`tests/unit/secrecy/review-probes.test.ts`): a forged undo restore identity by s1 (every inverse type, either seat, face-down or public card), the shuffle-commit replacement and the id-set change (0-3 hand cards in, a draw, a mulligan, the undo of a draw, a peer's mill) before the first nonce, and the pending scry before a sequenced `lookAt` — reason: docs/gates/M1-parked-m1-p2.md, 'What a retry should do first' 3.
- [M1-P2 retry] An `away` seat is still waited for by every randomness round and shuffle (`expectedParticipants` counts named seats, away or not), and a step by a round's own owner or initiator that stops it waiting for a seat that has not contributed is refused (`checkRoundParticipants`; today only `seatLeave` of the peer's chair under the owner's name) — `away` is sequencer-only and in 1v1 the sequencer is the owner, so marking the connected peer away (before or after the commit) let it decide the order or a die alone with a nonce ground offline (review round 4; probes are regression tests in tests/unit/secrecy/participants.test.ts). Exclusion now only by a `commitClose` from a seat other than the owner/initiator (the design's 3 s window when someone else sequences) or by the seat leaving. This alone held for `away` only, not for `seatLeave`: see the review-round-5 line below — the owner can shut the peer out neither before nor after committing, except as the sequencer through `away` before the commit. Liveness cost, accepted: a sequencer owner's round with a truly absent peer waits until that peer returns (its client must then contribute to every round still waiting for it, state-driven, not only on seeing the `shuffleCommit`/`rngCommit` — note for M1-P8) or M1-P7's recovery runs; in 1v1 the design already pauses play with the opponent gone. No contract change (no participant snapshot on ShuffleRound/RngRound). Not covered: a sequencer forging an action in another seat's name (e.g. its `shuffleNonce`) — actions are unsigned by design (DESIGN Handshake), so only the victim's own client could notice.
- [M1-P2 retry] An `undo`'s inverse must carry the undo sender's `by` (except a `lineAdd` restoring a removed line, whose `by` names the line's creator and authorises nothing) and must be `isUndoable` (no `flip` with `ref`) — the handlers authorise on the inner `by`, so s1 could otherwise flip s0's card up with a forged identity (blaming s0 via `suspect`) or draw a non-top card in s0's name (review round 4, minor).
- [M1-P2 retry] The reducer refuses a `seatLeave` whose `seat` is not the sender's `by` unless that seat is marked away (reducer/secret.ts `checkSeatLeave`, called from the step in reducer/index.ts) — any seat (in ffa a non-sequencer, through ordinary proposals) could otherwise empty the other chairs, then commit a shuffle or open a roll that waits for nobody and decide it with a nonce ground offline, and the emptied seats could sit down again (review round 5; regression tests in tests/unit/secrecy/participants.test.ts). A chair move is unaffected (M1-P5 stamps the leave with the old chair); a player gone for good is cleared after the sequencer's away mark (M1-P7). Remaining hole, of the sequencer-forgery class: the sequencer can mark a peer away and empty its chair before committing; the log shows both.
- [M1-P2 retry] Not changed: a seat that sits down in an empty chair while a shuffle is open joins it and may send the last nonce after seeing the others. Refusing such a `seatTake` would not help — any colluding seat already seated can wait and send its nonce last, since peer nonces travel in the clear by design (Secret shuffle 2) — and it would block chair-taking while a round waits for an away seat. For the gate report.
- [M1-P2 retry] Hand-overs for M1-P8 (review round 5): the client keeps one `shuffleNonce` per sid (with the pending rng nonce) and reuses it on every re-propose after a nack, so a sequencer owner cannot nack until a nonce it likes arrives; when a peer's own cid comes back sequenced it compares the payload with its pending copy and on a mismatch for `shuffleNonce`, `rngCommit` or `rngReveal` shows a table-wide integrity warning (actions are unsigned, DESIGN Handshake, so a sequencer can rewrite them). On reconnect the client contributes to every round still waiting for it. For the gate report: rounds wait for away seats (a sequencer's own round with a dropped peer stalls until it returns or leaves), and a re-issue's commits are checked only by the post-game audit.
- [M1-P2 retry] Files outside the package's list touched for wiring and consumers: src/core/reducer/index.ts (step hooks, handlers), src/core/reducer/draft.ts (`rng`/`shuffles` records), src/session/solo.ts (`undoPlan` re-reveals), and tests under tests/unit/core and tests/unit/stack adapted to them; none is a contract file. For the integrator to accept.
- [M1-P1] A claimant does not answer `syncReq` while it claims: it keeps the latest request per peer and answers them when it activates, and drops them if it gives the claim up (the senders ask again) — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 1, finding 1: an answer during the claim describes a history the claim may still replace, and a follower took it as the verification of the sequencer's new history. A rival claimant never waits for such an answer, since a claim completes only once every connected peer acknowledged it.
- [M1-P1] A follower that receives a claim from its sequencer sends the overlap read with its claimAck and drops that sequencer's seqs until the read is answered; verification epochs number these reads, and only the answer to a read of the current epoch that covers the follower's head (a loaded snapshot, a snapshot at exactly its head whose state hash matches, or a tail from at or before the head that reaches it) completes it — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 1, findings 1 and 2 (a late pre-claim stamp, or an answer that verified nothing, let a follower stack the adopted history on its forked copy). The reviewer's alternative (verify on the first seq above the claim's `fromSeq`) was not taken: a claimant that adopts a shorter fork stamps seqs at or below its `fromSeq`.
- [M1-P1] `syncRes` carries no request id, so the engine keeps the requests it sent (for STALE_ASK_MS = 4 × SYNC_RETRY_MS) and matches an answer to the oldest one to that peer it fits (a snapshot, or a tail starting right after `haveSeq`); an answer is used only if that request is the one the engine still waits for or an identical resend of it, else dropped, also when it comes from the sequencer — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 1, finding 2 (an unasked late answer was applied on top of a replica rewound for another peer's history). A verification read avoids the haveSeq of a still-unanswered request from an earlier epoch.
- [M1-P1] A claimant whose checked peer answers a tail that ends below that peer's claimAck resets the claimAck and sends the claim to it again — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 1, finding 3 (the peer had rewound after acknowledging; the claimant waited for ever for seqs it no longer held).
- [M1-P1] `adopt` rewinds again when the source it already adopts from shows a fork below the replica's head, and abandoned own actions accumulate across forks instead of being replaced; when the repair source leaves or the sequencer changes to another peer, they are proposed again rather than forgotten — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 1, finding 2 (hardening); the sequencer drops any it already holds.
- [M1-P1] A state-hash mismatch reload also proposes again the own actions of the retained tail that the sequencer's snapshot tail lacks (filtered by cid; those inside the snapshot are not) — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 1 (hardening): a fork no overlap check saw must not end as silent loss of acknowledged actions.
- [M1-P1] Beyond QUEUE_LIMIT held proposals, the oldest one held for the proposer that just added one goes on without its `prev`, not the globally oldest — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 1 (minor): one peer flooding proposals whose `prev` never comes must not push other proposers' held proposals out of order.
- [M1-P1] Not changed: a seated player whose page closes between the `seatTake` and the `seatLeave` of a chair move keeps both chairs until it leaves one; only the holder may leave a chair (DESIGN "Players, seats and formats"). Listed for the M1-P8 gate report — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 1, a design question rather than a defect; the session (M1-P5/M1-P8) could re-send the pending `seatLeave` on resume.
- [M1] Playwright runs go through scripts/pw-guard.mjs: one run per machine (lock in the OS temp dir, stale-lock takeover kills the dead run's process tree), abort on a macOS SecurityAgent prompt, maxFailures 20 and a 45-min global timeout locally; the config refuses unguarded local runs — after a crash, parallel agents' e2e runs plus WebKit keychain modals had stacked up browsers and exhausted RAM.
- [M1-P1] A follower still verifying its sequencer (after a change of sequencer or a claim) never adopts from a single differing `seq`: the seq is dropped and the epoch's overlap read finds the earliest fork; the sequencer's state hashes received meanwhile are kept and compared only once the seq is (re)applied — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 2: adopting from the first differing seq rewound too little and stacked the new history on an abandoned part below it.
- [M1-P1] A claimant answers every catch-up request it held during the claim (in order, at most 16 per peer), not only the latest per peer — reason: review M1-P1 retry round 2: the asker matches answers one for one, oldest first, so an unanswered older request swallowed the answer to the current verification read.
- [M1-P1] Own actions re-proposed from an abandoned history (a split that healed) may be sequenced after newer actions of the same proposer; a losing side's sequencer may stamp an action right after the heal before it learns of the claimant — reason: inherent to split-brain; nothing acknowledged is lost and all peers converge (review M1-P1 retry round 2, for the gate report).
- [M1-P1] A follower that is still verifying its sequencer never adopts from a single seq that differs. It drops the seq and waits for the overlap read; the sequencer's hashes received meanwhile are kept and compared only when that seq is applied again. Reason: review round 2, where adopting from the first differing seq rewound too little.
- [M1-P1] A claimant answers every catch-up request it held during the claim, at most 16 per peer, instead of only the latest. Reason: the asker matches answers to requests one for one, oldest first.
- [M1-P1] Own actions re-proposed from an abandoned history may be sequenced after newer actions from the same proposer, and the losing side's sequencer may stamp an action right after a heal. Reason: this is inherent to split-brain; nothing acknowledged is lost (informational, for the gate report).
- [workflow] Interruption check: the worktree was clean at commit 38c210d, so no work was in progress. No Playwright or orphaned test browsers were running and no Playwright lock was held; the browser processes found belonged to the user's own apps. pnpm e2e was run once, through scripts/pw-guard.mjs, with no SecurityAgent prompt showing. It passed (327 passed, 9 skipped) and left no browsers running.
- [verification] The reviewer's scratch sweeps were run against the fix. Three-way: seeds 0-299, 4 split groupings, drop 0/0.05/0.1, 3600 passed. Two-sided: seeds 0-299, drop 0/0.05, 600 passed. The reviewer's repro.test.ts with hash messages to peer3 dropped (NOHASH) showed no mixed states at seeds 180 and 425.
- [M1-P8] The table route joins through a new optional `AppProps.openRoom(link): Promise<NetSession>` (additive contract change in ui/types.ts) rather than main.ts handing a pre-made `AppProps.session` — reason: the start page navigates to a room by hash without a reload, and RoomRoute already owns the joining, slow-relay and failure states; RoomRoute leaves a session it opened when the route goes away, and still takes `AppProps.session` when its roomId matches.
- [M1-P8] A browser without a chair listens for SETTLE_MS (1.5 s) after joining, waits while the engine is syncing, then takes the lowest free chair; a lost race (seatTake nacked) takes the next free one, at most 4 attempts — reason: M1-P1's note (a fresh joiner must not act before it synced; the creator sits before others join). A browser whose save seats it resumes at once. With Trystero a joiner slower than 1.5 s to connect can still sit alone first; it then loses the tie or re-sits.
- [M1-P8] `takeSeat` reads `opts.identity.nickname` at each call; main.ts hands a live identity (session/live-identity.ts) whose nickname follows renames through the IdentityStore it gives the lobby and start page, and the session falls back to 'Player' — reason: M1-P5's notes without a contract change (`takeSeat` is synchronous, the store is async).
- [M1-P8] Another seat's look at a whole own hidden zone is refused (`lookAnswer` with `allowZoneLook: false`); a peek at an own face-down card is answered — reason: M1-P2's decision requires the owner's agreement and no consent UI exists yet; refusing fails closed.
- [M1-P8] The `rngReveal` the HUD dispatches right after its `rngCommit` is held (its nonce kept in the nonce store) and sent once every commit is in — reason: ui/commands.ts sends both at once, which only works with no other seat; the networked session makes that path follow Public randomness 3 without touching the HUD.
- [M1-P8] Open-round nonces (own rng nonces, own shuffle nonces for other seats' shuffles) live in local storage keyed by room and peer id (session/nonces.ts), and are reused for every re-proposal; refusals of one contribution (local or a sequencer nack) stop its retries after 3 — reason: M1-P2's hand-overs (no SecretStore slot; a sequencer must not nack until it likes a nonce) without endless re-proposals.
- [M1-P8] The `exposed` set (own ids another seat saw since issued) is rebuilt on load from the stored log by scanning own reveals, flips with an identity, targeted reveals and other seats' looks; a zone look counts every card currently in that zone — reason: M1-P2's note; the stored log has no per-step secret orders, and an extra re-issue is harmless.
- [M1-P8] solo.ts and net.ts share session/persist.ts (the write queue, snapshot rules) and session/effects.ts (reveal-before-public, face-down, undo re-reveal rules); persist.ts also rebases when an action does not follow the stored log or arrives with the replica past it (catch-up from a snapshot, a rewound history) — reason: "reuse solo.ts's persistence path rather than a second one", and a networked replica can skip or replace seqs.
- [M1-P8] Once the hand is re-issued after a public card returned to it, the undo steps that name a retired hand id are dropped (the return itself, and earlier steps of those cards) — reason: the re-issue (M1-P2, privacy) replaces the ids an inverse names, so those steps could never apply and would block every later undo.
- [M1-P8] Deleted the stand-in (ui/lobby/local-session.ts, ui/lobby/dev-room.ts, tests/unit/lobby/local-session.test.ts) and edited outside the package for it: ui/diag/DiagRoute.svelte (drops its dev-room fallback; main.ts now always passes `diag` over the page transport), tests/unit/lobby/diag.test.ts (imports the failure transports from session/transports.ts), tests/sim/net/chairs.test.ts (its stand-in describe moved to tests/sim/session/session.test.ts on the real session) — reason: the brief's "delete when replaced".
- [M1-P8] Removed the webkit+darwin keychain skip from tests/e2e/lobby; all lobby and session specs pass on Playwright WebKit on macOS since the JWK identity fix (7b61414). tests/e2e/ui/hud.spec.ts still skips its `#/lobby` case on webkit+darwin (not in this package's files).
- [M1-P8] The networked session no longer proposes a `reveal` ahead of the action that makes an own card public: the action goes alone (the reducer puts the card down face down) and, once it is sequenced, the owner proposes `flip {face: 'up', ref, salt}` (verified, needs a public card); a flip up of an own face-down card carries ref and salt itself, and an undo's re-reveal waits for the undo to be sequenced — reason: review round 1, a reveal sequenced before an action the sequencer then refused published a card still in the hand (DESIGN "Multiplayer architecture", Hidden information 2). On the sequencer's own page the flip follows in the same tick; elsewhere the card shows its back for one round trip. solo.ts keeps reveal-first (no concurrency).
- [M1-P8] An own card lying face down without identity in a public zone that shows faces (graveyard, stack, command) is turned up by its owner with a verified flip whoever moved it there, at most 3 refused attempts per card — reason: DESIGN "Table model" (only a battlefield and exile hold face-down cards; the owner publishes the identity when a card becomes public).
- [M1-P8] After a non-exact fold (catch-up from a snapshot, rewound history) every own card counts as seen in the `exposed` set, so the next shuffle of a zone re-issues its ids — reason: the skipped actions may have shown any of them; an extra re-issue is harmless, a missed one lets a peer follow a card.
- [M1-P8] `openNetSession(opts, internals, signal)` takes an AbortSignal; main.ts aborts a join still under way when the page leaves that link's route (hashchange), which leaves the engine and its transport; leaving a room also drops `window.__spellplane.net` — reason: review round 1 (a join through unreachable relays lived on for the page's life).
- [M1-P8] The session leak test keeps delay and loss on the public channels only (`faultyChannels: ['act', 'ctl', 'pres']`), as M1-P2's secrecy acceptance test does — reason: the pair channel has no retransmission (it rides WebRTC's reliable data channel), so a dropped targeted reveal made the script's wait fail depending on the seed's random sequence.
- [M1-P8] Supersedes the last sentence of the SETTLE_MS line: a browser that did not create the room and holds no chair in its save waits up to LONE_WAIT_MS (8 s, from the join) for a peer before it sits alone; main.ts passes 0 on the local transport (BroadcastChannel peers connect at once) and marks rooms created on the start page in the tab's sessionStorage (`createdHere`: the creator sits after SETTLE_MS) — reason: review round 2, Trystero's join resolves before any WebRTC peer is up, and a joiner that sat alone at s0 with a history as long as the creator's kept a second sequencer for good. The joiner still sits alone after LONE_WAIT_MS (the M1 gate expects the lobby on a network where nobody ever connects), so the safety net is in the session: a browser that did not create the room, whose table is still in the lobby, seats nobody else and holds only its own actions, and that meets a connected peer claiming its chair, leaves with that engine and joins again from the seq-0 table with a new engine and transport (waiting for the peer, then the free chair), says its chat lines again and asks for Ready again if it had committed a deck alone; the creator never gives up; at most 3 such rejoins per session. The engine's equal-seq, same-seat case (two sequencers that never reconcile) remains an M1-P1 gap for the integrator.
- [M1-P8] A peek at an own face-down card is answered only for the seat that controls it in the confirmed state and does not own it (a stolen morph); every other look request is refused, and showing the card to anyone else is the owner's `revealTo` — reason: review round 2, the answerer ignored the asking seat, so any opponent could log `lookAt {card}` and read a morph (DESIGN "Table model", Face-down on the battlefield: "its identity stays with its controller until turned up"; Decision log, Secrets: "Nobody can peek at an opponent"). Supersedes the "peek is answered" half of the earlier line.
- [M1-P8] A turn-up owed after an own action made a card public is also kept in the nonce store (kind 'turnUp': instance id → the action's cid; nothing secret) until the card shows its identity or leaves the public zones, and is resumed after a reload once the replica is in step — reason: review round 2, a reload between the move being sequenced and its verified flip left a card played face up lying face down; the log cannot tell such a move from `playFaceDown`.
- [M1-P8] The hand re-issue after a public card returned to it is owed state that `drive` retries (after a local refusal on the next sequenced action, after a sequencer nack at once), up to 3 refusals, then logs a warning — reason: review round 2, a single attempt left the old ids when a concurrent action made it fail.
- [M1-P8] Supersedes the kept turn-up line: a turn-up kept in the nonce store is honoured only once the replica is in step ('connected', not 'alone' or 'syncing' right after a resume) and its cid is in the sequenced log as an action on that card after which the card neither moved to another zone nor turned (tap, rotate, counter, note, attach, detach and moves within the same zone keep it owed); it is dropped once its cid is in the log and the card moved or turned since, or when the replica is in step and the cid is neither sequenced nor pending; `playFaceDown` and any own action on a card that lies in a hidden zone drop it first; the scan for own face-down cards in a zone that shows faces also waits for 'connected' — reason: review round 3, a face-up play lost before it was sequenced (crash or reload in flight) turned up the same card played face down after the reload when the peers came up late (DESIGN "Table model", Face-down on the battlefield), and a resumed replica dropped a turn-up whose move was sequenced but not yet caught up.
- [M1-P8] Supersedes the `AppProps.openRoom` line: ui/types.ts is unchanged; main.ts hands `openRoom` next to AppProps and ui/lobby/RoomRoute.svelte declares it locally (`RoomOpener`), reaching it through the props App passes the route — reason: review round 3, contract files are edited only by a contracts package; the integrator may take `openRoom?: (link: RoomLink) => Promise<NetSession>` into AppProps in a contracts commit.
- [M1-P8] A rejoin's new save queue starts after the old one's last write (`TablePersistenceOptions.after`), and the alone table's kept turn-ups are dropped — reason: review round 3 hardening, two unordered queues could let a late write of the abandoned table land over the new seq-0 record.
- [M1-G fix 2] M1-P8 merged from `m1-p8` at `a8bde85` after one focused review of the turn-up and resume paths (`keptTurnUp`, `turnUpOwed`, `forgetTurnUp`, rejoin): a kept turn-up is honoured only for a sequenced action on that card with no later move out of the zone or turn, only while 'connected', and the flip fails closed unless the card is public without identity in both the confirmed state and the view (undo, another seat's move, a catch-up past the cid and the rejoins all end in 'stale' or a refused flip, never a turn-up of a card the owner put face down) — reason: the gate needs the networked session; the round-3 fix held up. The `openRoom` contract question stays as the branch left it (local `RoomOpener` in RoomRoute.svelte, ui/types.ts unchanged).
- [M1-G fix 2] `TableSession.roll` resolves with the 0-based outcome (contract); the session sim test that expected 1..6 was wrong and flaky (it failed whenever the die came up 0) — reason: tests/sim/session/session.test.ts 'commit windows'.
- [M1-G fix 2] Another seat's `rngReveal` logs as "<seat> adds to <roller>'s d6", not as a roll of its own — reason: the gate's log showed "Ann rolls a d6: 1" and "Ben rolls a d6: 1" for Ben's one roll.
- [M1-G fix 2] The fit's top inset reserves the stack chip below the turn strip (74 px instead of 40), also while the stack is empty, so the fitted view does not jump when a spell goes on the stack — reason: gate M1 finding, the chip covered the opponent's row of backs.
- [M1-G fix 2] Pile labels (library, graveyard, exile) sit beside their pile on the battlefield side, anchored away from it, for seats the viewer reads along (own and opposite); sideways seats keep the label in the gap below the pile — reason: gate M1 finding, the enlarged labels crossed the pile outlines in the 12 mm gap.
- [M1-P7 partial, gate fix] The pure parts of M1-P7 landed: `chooseResume` (highest seq wins, own save wins ties, first offer among equal peers; the rule `NetEngine.deferTo` already follows, nothing calls it yet), `recoveryLibrarySize`/`recoveryRemainder` (tokens excluded; multiset subtraction by sid and foil; for face-down public cards the last cards of the re-import are dropped; null when the re-import lacks a public card), and the `recover`/`recoverCommit` reducer in `core/reducer/recovery.ts` with log lines and the shared leak scenario. `recover` drops the seat's own unfinished shuffles of its hidden zones and is exempt from the frozen-zone and round-participant guards for exactly those dropped rounds (no order is left to choose); the hand count is 0..deck size − public cards; its round label is "new hand of N?". Not done: the session's recovery driver and `ui/recovery/*` (the "my save is lost" entry point) — reason: one fix round; the reload and reopen paths the gate exercises are M1-P8's real resume (`loadSaved`, rejoin, `deferTo`).
- [M1-G verify] tests/e2e/ui/hud.spec.ts no longer skips its `#/lobby` route case on macOS WebKit — the JWK identity fix (7b61414) removed the CryptoKey-in-IndexedDB hang; the case passes on Chromium and WebKit.
- [M1-P7] The lost-save recovery driver is `NetTable.recover(deck, hand)` (session/net.ts), typed by `SessionRecovery` in session/resume.ts and found with `canRecover(session)`, so no contract file changes: it pre-checks the re-import (`recoveryProblem`: the hand count, a public card the deck lacks, a deck too small for the library; nothing is proposed on a problem), proposes `recover` with a fresh round id, waits until that round passes (rejects when another proposal or another round replaces it: propose again), commits `recoveryRemainder` with ids no commitment of the seat ever listed (`recoverCommit`, with the re-import's sideboard as the pool), then runs the ordinary secret shuffle and draws the agreed hand; the undo stack is cleared as after a mulligan. Called again with the same count while that round is still current (a reload mid-recovery) it waits for that round instead of proposing again — reason: the simplest driver on the M1-C contract; no second save or restore path.
- [M1-P7] "My save is lost" is detected, not declared: own hand or library ids whose identity the secret store lacks (`lostHiddenCards`); the panel suggests the hand size the table last saw. A lost hand is never re-issued (`reissueHand` skips it; the recovery replaces it) — reason: reissueZone throws on ids it cannot see.
- [M1-P7] The recovery needs the same browser identity: the chair is rejoined by key pair, so a player whose key pair is gone too (cleared site data, another device) cannot take the chair back in M1 (`seatTake` refuses a taken chair; a takeover of an away chair would need its own consent step) — reason: DESIGN rejoins "by key pair"; flagged for the gate report as a gap against "cleared storage, different device".
- [M1-P7] ui/recovery/RecoveryPanel.svelte is mounted once in ui/lobby/TableRoom.svelte, for the lobby and the HUD stage alike (fixed, top centre, not modal; reuses the HUD's DeckImport dialog); it shows only to the player who lost hidden cards or has a recovery open, with the other seats' answers (OK, holds, waiting). The other players answer in their ordinary confirmation prompt ("new hand of 7?") — reason: the HUD and lobby belong to other packages; one line there is the least intrusive entry point.
- [M1-P7] `chooseResume` stays the pure rule, not wired: the resume sims assert that the engine reconciles to the copy it names (`deferTo` already follows highest seq) — reason: a second chooser in the session would race the engine's. `store/tables.ts` unchanged: no gap found (the save of a recovered seat is M1-P8's ordinary path).
- [M1-P7] The `away` stand-ins are gone: `?harness=confirm`'s `away()` and the M1-P3 sim table sequence a real `away` through the reducer (a repeated mark is refused; regression test in tests/sim/confirm/rounds.test.ts).
- [M1-P7 fix 1] A recovery stays resumable after its `recoverCommit` (which clears `Seat.recovering`): just before the commit is proposed the session keeps `{id, hand, shuffle}` in the page's local storage (session/nonces.ts kind 'recovery', nothing secret: one committed instance id, the agreed count, the shuffle id once proposed); it is owed while the seat's commitment lists the id, no newer `recover` is open and this browser holds its library. `drive` finishes it once per page on a replica in step (the shuffle of the new library unless that shuffle is sequenced, then draws until the hand holds the agreed count, at most ROUND_ATTEMPTS rounds, else it rejects), `recoveryStatus` reports phase 'committed' and the panel offers "Finish recovery" (`finishRecovery`); `draw` is refused while the new library is not shuffled — reason: review finding, a reload after the commit left an unshuffled library in decklist order and no hand; local storage like the shuffle nonces, no contract change.
- [M1-P7 fix 1] A browser that lost its save takes back the identities of its public cards from the sequenced log (`identitiesFromLog`: own `reveal` and verified `flip` actions whose ref and salt match the seat's commitment, for cards still on the table under that id) when it recovers and when a card it cannot name comes back to its hand, so a bounce re-issues the hand and the card turns up verified again. Reveals a catch-up from a snapshot skipped cannot be recovered (the snapshot carries no salts): such a card stays unnamed in the hand, the hand is not re-issued, and it cannot be turned up verified — contract gap for the gate report. "Lost save" (phase 'lost') now also requires that this browser holds no card of its library, so an unnamed hand card never offers a recovery that would discard the hidden cards it holds — reason: review finding.
- [M1-P7 fix 1] `recoveryProblem` checks the hand count against deck size minus public cards before the reducer's dry run and passes any other refusal through verbatim; the recovery's fresh-id generator throws after 100 collisions instead of returning a used id — reason: review minors.
- [M1-P7 fix 2] `identitiesFromLog` takes back only cards in a public zone or the seat's hand (never one back in the library or another hidden zone), and phase 'lost' ignores library cards whose identity the seat's own log entries published (`ownPublishedIds`, passed by the panel from `session.log()`): only a library card the log never named shows that the save survived — reason: review finding, a revealed card put back on top of the library (id kept) hid the recovery entry point for the rest of the game.
- [M1-P7 fix 2] A recovery's record is owed only until the new library was shuffled after the `recoverCommit` (the recovery's shuffle or any own library shuffle) and the own draws sequenced after it reach the agreed count (by the recovery or by hand); the finish draws that difference, not up to the current hand size (fallback to the hand size only when the log lacks the commit after a snapshot catch-up), and `drive` drops a record that is no longer owed — reason: review finding, a failed finish left a record that drew into a played-out hand on every load.
- [M1-P7 fix 2] While the new library is not shuffled, `dispatch` also refuses (with a warning, nothing proposed) a `draw` and a `toZone`/`move` that takes a card out of the own library, not only `draw()` — reason: review finding (DESIGN "Hidden information" 3).
- [M1-P7 fix 2] `recoveryStatus.publicCards` and `lostHiddenCards` use the reducer's notion of the seat's hidden zones (visibility not public, owned by the seat): a card in an own sideboard or pack zone is not counted as kept and is reported in `lost.other`; only lost hand or library cards trigger phase 'lost' (an own pack may hold cards the store does not name) — reason: review finding, maxHand was one lower than the reducer allows.
