- [M1] After the second retry parked M1-P7 with an unreviewed fix, the main session retried it itself: reviewed 2cc474e..587447f (both open findings fixed, each with a regression test), ran pnpm check, the formerly flaky recovery sim 25 times, pnpm e2e and both gates, and merged — reason: PROMPT step 3 (retry each parked package once with full context).
- [M1] M1-P8 was merged by the gate fixer after its own review of the unreviewed round-3 fix; an independent read-only reviewer then approved a8bde85 at m1 HEAD (150 randomized turn-up runs, no leak). Its minor findings (turn-up lost after a snapshot catch-up, undo invisible to the kept turn-up rule, stale view after reload) are listed in docs/gates/M1.md — reason: keep a second pair of eyes on hidden-information code merged outside the review loop.

## M2 Draft evening

- [M2-C] The shared draft shapes (`DraftState`, `DraftPack`, the per-format settings, `LobbySettings`, `DraftSecrets`, `PoolCard`, `DeckBuild`) live in `core/types.ts`, and `draft/types.ts` re-exports them — reason: `TableState` and `SecretStore` must hold them and core imports nothing outside core (module boundaries); the plan's `draft/types.ts` keeps the client-side seams (`DraftHost`, `DraftDriver`, `DraftControls`, presets).
- [M2-C] New optional `TableState` slots `lobby`, `draft` and `games`, and `SecretStore` slots `draft` and `build`; absent keys are omitted — reason: M1 snapshots and saves stay loadable and every M0/M1 state hash is unchanged.
- [M2-C] During a hidden-pack draft shared state tracks packs by count, commitments and queue position, never as card instances in zones: a hidden `pick` names only a count — reason: DESIGN Draft packs ("identities of picked cards stay with the picker"); naming picked ids would tell the pack's previous holder exactly what was taken. Rochester and Grid cards are public (`DraftCard` with identity); `packOpen` with `public: true` carries `cards` (instance id, ref, salt) so every peer verifies them against the commits in the same step.
- [M2-C] `packOpen` gains optional `instanceIds`, `sid`, `number`, `set` and `cards` (optional so M0's shape compiles; M2-P2/P3 require what they need) — reason: a receiver verifies a handed-over pack against the pack's own `Commitment` with `verifyReveal`, and the audit links a pack to its secret-shuffle round.
- [M2-C] New actions `lobbySet {lobby}` (meta; host only, lobby stage only, structural check `lobbyProblem`, implemented by M2-C) and `draftTimeout {packId, picks}` (draft; sequencer only, reducer M2-P2) — reason: the lobby settings are shared state the host changes; the pick timer is decided by the sequencer's clock (DESIGN Timers), the holder then picks the marked or first card.
- [M2-C] `stageSet` gains optional `round`, the passed confirmation round with which the others agreed to leave a match — reason: DESIGN Evening stages ("any player proposes 'next game' or 'new draft' and the others confirm"); M2-P7 checks it in `stageProblem`.
- [M2-C] Stage edges: lobby → draft | match; draft → build | lobby; build → match | lobby; match → build | lobby. `lobby → match` keeps M1's constructed path; `draft → lobby` lets the host abandon a draft; rematch is `nextGame` (match → build) — reason: the simplest machine that holds the design's evening and M1's flow. `stageProblem` returns null and `enterStage` only sets the stage until M2-P7, so M0/M1 behaviour is unchanged.
- [M2-C] The host is the seated player with the lowest seat index (`hostSeat`) — reason: DESIGN names a host but not who it is; the same rule elects the sequencer.
- [M2-C] Lobby limits: packs 1–12, picks per pass 1–4, burn 0–14, grids 1–18, 1–12 set codes (alphanumeric, at most 16 characters) — reason: the design gives defaults only; bounds keep malformed proposals out of shared state.
- [M2-C] `LobbySettings.sets` is a set per pack number, a shorter list repeating its last entry (`setForPack`); the table format stays `TableState.format` and the pass timer `TableSettings.passTimerMs` — reason: DESIGN Lobby settings ("set per pack … a different set per pack is allowed") without moving M0 fields.
- [M2-C] Pass direction: 0-based round 0, 2, … left (next higher seat index), 1, 3, … right (`passDirection`, `passTarget`) — reason: DESIGN "left for odd rounds and right for even rounds" counting from 1; "left means to the next seat clockwise".
- [M2-C] Presets: booster 2–4 seats (3 packs, pick 1), Pick-Two exactly 4 (3, 2), sealed 2–4 (6), Rochester 2–4 (3, burn 0), Winston 2 (6 packs in total), Grid 2 (18 grids), Two-Headed Giant draft 4 (6 per team, 2 picks) and sealed 4 (8 per team) — reason: DESIGN mode table and Draft formats.
- [M2-C] Winston's dealing is left to M2-P3: `WinstonState.dealer` names the browser that holds the face-down stack and answers pile looks with the new `draftCards` pair message — reason: without a trusted dealer, keeping the stack hidden from both players needs mental-poker machinery the design does not ask for; M2-P3 must record what each player can know and add its scenario to the leak test.
- [M2-C] The builder state is `DeckBuild {cards, main, poses?}` in the SecretStore, read and written through `DraftControls.build/setBuild`; the builder table (scene, M2-P5) and the list view (ui, M2-P6) are both views of it — reason: DESIGN "two views of the same pool, switchable at any time", and a reload in the build stage must keep the deck (PLAN M2-P7). Basics from the dispenser are pool cards with `basic: true`.
- [M2-C] `BuilderSort`, the draft and builder surface interfaces live in `scene/types.ts`, `BuilderStats` and the panel props in `ui/types.ts` — reason: scene may not import draft/ or ui/, and the list view (ui) reuses the sort key.
- [M2-C] Drafted pools are saved as `DeckRecord`s with source 'drafted' plus optional `pool` (the `DeckBuild`) and `origin` (format and sets); library export/import uses `LibraryFile {format: 'spellplane-decks', version: 1, exportedAt, decks}` — reason: DESIGN Local deck library ("decks and drafted pools … exportable and importable as files") with one store and one file format.
- [M2-C] `TableSession` gains optional `draft` (`DraftControls`) and `evening` (`EveningControls`), and both session option types optional `sets` and `generatePack`; `SpellplaneDebug` gains a `draft` part — reason: the HUD and gate need one entry point; M2-P7 wires them (no M2 package row lists the session, so the ownership note gives it to M2-P7).
- [M2-C] `CardDataSource.searchCards` is an optional method (M2-P6) — reason: additive; the existing sources and fakes keep compiling.
- [M2-P3] Contract amendment requested from the integrator (not a package decision; CLAUDE.md forbids packages to edit contract files): optional `DraftState.taken` (ids each drafter took in an open format, in pick order), `WinstonState.stackIds`/`pileIds` (opaque ids of the dealt stack and piles), `DraftHost.sendCards(to, about, cards): boolean` (false when `to` has no connected peer) and `DraftHost.onCards` (the `draftCards` pair message), `DraftHost.confirmedState()` (wired to NetEngine `confirmedState`), and the `draftCards` case in net/engine.ts — reason: pools and the end-state accounting become pure functions of shared state (reload-safe, verifiable by the other player), a driver had no seam to the pair channel for Winston looks, and a disclosure must know whether it went out; the branch carries them until M2-C lands them on m2.
- [M2-P3] Every open-format pack has its own pack-seed round: `packOpen` must name a sealed or finished 'pack' round of the opener that is the opener's latest one and started after the opener's previous pack of this draft was opened (one round backs one pack; the opener cannot pick among several seeds), with sorted commitments; a public pack (Rochester, Grid) lists its sorted ids and carries every card, each opening one of the commitments, else it is refused — reason: DESIGN Draft packs (generated with the secret-shuffle seed protocol, the owner could not choose it) and Commitments; sorted lists say nothing about pack order.
- [M2-P3] Draft card instance ids of the open formats are drawn from the pack seed's byte stream right after the pack (core/open.ts `seededCardId`, 'c' + 16 base-32 characters); salts stay host entropy — reason: the Winston stack order is the sorted ids, so the dealer must not choose them (DESIGN "6 packs shuffled into one face-down stack"), and an audit can recompute them from the PackRecord. Not enforced (residual risk for the gate report): the reducer cannot regenerate a pack (no set data in core/) and no audit replays the Winston or Grid PackRecords yet, so a modified dealer client could choose its ids and with them arrange the whole Winston stack (in Rochester and Grid the freedom only affects ids); the dealer could also abandon pack-seed rounds it dislikes before opening a pack (the abandoned rounds stay visible in shared state).
- [M2-P3] Rochester: pack number = round = floor(packs opened / seats), its set `setForPack(sets, round)`; a pack leaves the table as soon as at most `burn` cards remain (at once when it opens that small), and its burned cards stay listed in the pack — reason: DESIGN "until `burn` cards remain"; keeping them makes every card accountable.
- [M2-P3] Winston dealing: the dealer (first seat) generates and opens all packs face down, one seed each; a Winston `packOpen` publishes its sorted commitments and no ids (its `DraftPack.commitment.instanceIds` stays empty), and the last one lists the sorted ids of the whole stack and deals it, top first, one card per pile; the other player checks a shown card against the commitments of all Winston packs together with the dealt ids (core/open.ts `winstonCommitment`); the dealer's browser holds every identity and could know the whole stack (trusted dealer, [M2-C]); the other player is sent a pile only while the confirmed state has it looking at that pile, plus the cards it took — reason: DESIGN "6 packs shuffled into one face-down stack" and "pile contents visible to whoever looks" without mental-poker machinery; per-pack ids would tie every face-down card to its pack, its set and (by elimination) its rare slot.
- [M2-P3] Winston at the end of the stack: empty piles are skipped; declining the last pile that holds a card adds a stack card and takes the next when at least two remain, takes the last one without adding when one remains, and is refused when none remain (the player takes the pile) — reason: the design does not say what happens when the stack runs out; this never leaves a turn without a card.
- [M2-P3] Grid: the grid's first player lays it; each grid is one booster from its own pack seed, shuffled by the same stream, first nine cards face up (the rest is never shown; the PackRecord keeps it for the audit); a grid counts as one pack for the set per pack; the second player must take a line that still holds a card, and a grid with no such line left ends after the first take — reason: DESIGN "nine face-up cards from shuffled packs" with the audit's reproducibility and a lay that needs no extra secret state.
- [M2-P3] Open-format pools are filled from `DraftState.taken` of confirmed states only, once per id (`DraftSecrets.held['open:pool']`), up to the first taken id whose identity is not known yet (a late Winston disclosure holds back later picks, so the pool stays in pick order); public pack cards are kept in `held[packId]` only when a sequenced `packOpen`'s cards open every commitment of the pack of that id in the confirmed state, opened by its sender (a refused `packOpen` is still sequenced and must not squat the next pack's id), and Winston identities shown to the other player in `held['winston:seen']` — reason: optimistic picks can be reordered or refused; the held copies survive a reload.
- [M2-P3] No pick timer in Rochester, Winston or Grid (view `timer` is null) — reason: `draftDeadlines` (M2-P2) times packs at the front of a queue, and the open formats queue none; the design's timer is per held pack.
- [M2-P3] net/engine.ts forwards `draftCards` to the pair-channel listeners like `pack` (one case line, part of the contract amendment above for the integrator to take) — reason: the engine dropped the new pair message, so no Winston look could arrive in the app.
- [M2-P3] Winston disclosures are marked shown only once `sendCards` delivered them, and retried on every sequenced action and drive() until then; the other player sends an empty `draftCards` with about `winston:need` whenever its confirmed state lets it see a card (its look, its taken cards) it has no identity for (at once on a new seq, then every 25 drive() calls), and the dealer answers with everything that player may see now — reason: a disclosure can be lost after delivery when the peer drops right then; the session should call drive() when a peer (re)joins, since that is the driver's only clock.
- [M2-P3] The Winston leak scenario is tests/sim/draft/open/leak.test.ts (plain run and a kill/revive run, with a pair check that no state ties a face-down id to a pack), on the shared scanner like the earlier scenarios (tests/sim/secrecy, resume, session) — reason: CLAUDE.md shared leak test; the earlier packages also keep their scenario next to their own sim tests.
- [M2-P3] Updated two expectations of earlier tests that pinned the stubs (`rochesterOrder` throwing, and pileLook/gridTake explaining "M2-P3"; now "no draft is in progress") — reason: the stubs are implemented.
- [M2-P3] Open-format drivers start from `DraftHost.confirmedState()` (null when the session does not provide it, never the optimistic view): the Winston dealer discloses and answers only from confirmed states, so a pending own take never shows a pile; the other player may ask from the view before its first confirmed state (a request carries nothing); with the seam a reloaded browser asks for, or answers, a missing pile from drive() alone — reason: in Winston only the player waiting for the pile can act, so after a reload no sequenced action may ever come, and a pile shown from an optimistic state may never have a reveal path (DESIGN "Pile contents visible to whoever looks").
- [M2-P3] Winston keeps disclosing (dealer) and asking for (other player) the other player's taken cards after the draft ends, in any stage while `state.draft` is still the Winston draft; pile looks only in the draft stage; M2-P7 must keep the Winston driver alive (calling drive()) until the other player's pool covers `taken` — reason: the last look can end the draft right after the other player took a stack card it was never shown, and the automatic draft → build advance would otherwise strand that card.
- [M2-P3] Rochester and Grid drivers remember every face-up card they see in a confirmed state or in the view a pick is made from (`DraftSecrets.held['open:seen']`, refs only), besides the verified `packOpen` copy — reason: a driver created after the `packOpen` (a reload, lazy creation, a snapshot resync) could otherwise never name a card once it is picked off the table, and the pool would stop at it.
- [M2-P3] Grid draws each grid from its own booster (nine cards shown, the other six never in play) and repeats the last set once the sets list runs out — design-interpretation item for the gate report: "18 grids of nine face-up cards from shuffled packs" could also mean pooling boosters and dealing them into grids (about 11 boosters for 18 grids); the owner should confirm.
- [M2-P3] The lobby still offers the pick timer for Rochester, Winston and Grid, where it does nothing — gate-report item for M2-P4/M2-P7: hide the setting for the open formats, or extend `draftDeadlines` (M2-P2) to time the Rochester picker.
- [M2-P1] Generation is independent of JSON key order: layouts in listed order, a layout's sheets in sorted name order, a sheet's cards in sorted uuid order — reason: two browsers (and the audit) must derive the same pack from the same seed even if the set data went through IndexedDB or canonicalisation.
- [M2-P1] Weighted draws without replacement draw from the remaining weight (equivalent in distribution to the design's "redrawing duplicates", without wasted bytes); ranges above 2^32 (M21's foil sheet totals about 2.4e12) are drawn as a high and a low 32-bit part with rejection — reason: `ByteStream.below` stops at 2^32 and must stay unbiased.
- [M2-P1] A non-foil card is never repeated within a pack, also across sheets; a foil may repeat a non-foil card of the same pack; `allowDuplicates` sheets repeat freely; a `fixed` sheet yields its cards in sorted uuid order, each `weight` times, without randomness — reason: matches real packs and MTGJSON's flags; the design is silent on cross-sheet repeats and fixed sheets.
- [M2-P1] Colour balancing draws one mono-coloured card per colour in WUBRG order (a colour without a mono-coloured card on the sheet is skipped), then fills from the whole sheet — reason: DESIGN Pack generation 3; the order only fixes how bytes are consumed.
- [M2-P1] Pack order by card: rare, mythic and special first, then uncommons, non-land commons, the foil, then common lands (the land slot) — reason: DESIGN Pack generation 4 names no place for the foil; a rare land stays with the rares.
- [M2-P1] `trimSetFile` drops sheet cards without a positive integer weight or a Scryfall id (cards and tokens are both searched), drops layouts whose sheets cannot yield their counts, recomputes total weights, and falls through to the next booster type (draft, default, play) when none survives — reason: generation must never fail on a set the picker offers.
- [M2-P1] The picker lists FEATURED_SETS first (names and release dates in `data/sets.ts`, so they show offline), then cached sets and MTGJSON SetList sets of type core, expansion, masters, draft_innovation or funny that are not online-only, by release date oldest first, ties by code; a set whose file has no usable booster data is dropped once loaded — reason: SetList carries no booster data, and fetching every set file to check would cost hundreds of MB.
- [M2-P1] Cached set files older than 30 days are served and refreshed in the background (the card cache's policy); a 404 or a file without booster data answers null, other HTTP and network errors reject and are not remembered; cache failures never block loading — reason: DESIGN Caching ("rebuildable, never depends on it").
- [M2-P1] The acceptance simulation checks every per-pack property on 50,000 packs per set and the rates with the plan's tolerances on those 50,000, not 10,000 — reason: at 10,000 packs ±0.5 points is only 1.5 standard errors of a 1-in-8 rate, so a correct generator misses it for about one seed in eight per set (observed for M12 with the first seed tried, while 200,000 packs landed within 0.07 points); at 50,000 the same tolerance is 3.4 standard errors, so the result does not hinge on the choice of seed.
- [M2] Playwright guard: up to four runs per machine (SPELLPLANE_PW_SLOTS, default 4), one per worktree; slot 0 keeps the old single-lock path so older guards count against it — owner's request to speed up the run; memory and prompt watchdogs unchanged.
- [M2-P6] The list view takes the deck library (`decks: DeckStore`) and a drafted pool's `origin` as extra props (`ListLibraryProps`, ui/builder/list/props.ts) beside `ListViewProps`, which stays as M2-C left it; the builder container (M2-P5) passes `BuilderProps.decks`, the draft's origin and `search = cardData.searchCards` — reason: contract files are edited only in a contracts package; adding both to `ListViewProps` is requested from the integrator.
- [M2-P6] The list view shows identical cards (same Scryfall id and finish) as one row with a count; a click moves one copy, Shift+click or "all" moves every copy; each column is grouped by the sort key (colour W U B R G, multicoloured, colourless, lands; mana value 0…7+, lands; type creature, planeswalker, battle, instant, sorcery, artifact, enchantment, land), rows by mana value, then name, non-foil first — reason: DESIGN asks for a compact list with counts; lands as their own group mirror the builder table's columns.
- [M2-P6] Filters: text (every word in name, type line or either face's rules text), colours (any selected; M multicoloured, C colourless incl. lands) and card type; they narrow both columns and the headers show "showing n" beside the unfiltered totals. Cards whose data is still loading show only without filters, and the list re-reads card data every 500 ms while any card of the build (filtered out or not) is loading (the `card` lookup has no change signal).
- [M2-P6] The list follows the builder's `sort` until the player picks a sort in the list; filters, that sort, the search box (query, results, last error) and the current library record live in module state (ui/builder/list/state.svelte.ts, `resetListUi`) so switching views keeps them; the deck itself lives only in the shared DeckBuild, and the list never touches ids, card order or `poses` of cards it does not move.
- [M2-P6] Moving a dispensed basic (`basic: true`) out of the deck returns it to the dispenser: it leaves the build with its pose (one click takes the most recently added dispensed copy first); "+" on a basic-land row of the deck dispenses one more of that printing; a basic land added by search is a dispensed basic. Ids of added cards are `list-add-<n>` / `list-basic-<n>`, the smallest unused — reason: the design's dispenser is unlimited, so a returned basic has no place in the pool.
- [M2-P6] `searchCards`: `(<query>) game:paper`, `unique=cards`, `order=name`, first result page only (at most 175 cards, one request through the shared limiter); results join the card cache. No printing chooser in the search box (Scryfall's default printing is added) — reason: one request per search keeps within 2 requests/s; the printing chooser belongs to the pasted-list path.
- [M2-P6] Library: saving stores the whole DeckBuild (`pool`, with table positions) plus `main`/`side` refs, source 'drafted' with `origin` when the builder has one, else 'built'; a record without a pool (pasted) opens as a pool of its deck and sideboard (ids d1…dn). "Save changes" updates the record last saved or opened in this page, "Save as new" copies; Open (when the builder holds cards) and Delete ask inline, never modally.
- [M2-P6] Library files: `importLibrary` reports problems with the file as a whole as one error with index -1 and per-record problems with the record's 0-based index; records are checked field by field (unknown fields dropped, a blank name becomes "Untitled deck", a pool must hold the same cards as main and side, poses of cards not in the pool dropped); limits 2000 records, 5000 cards per list, 20 MB per file. Importing never overwrites (`planImport`): an equal record is skipped, a different deck under a taken id is added as a copy with a new id.
- [M2-P6] The list view's browser specs mount the real ListView in a stand-in builder container bundled from tests/e2e/builder/list/page.ts and served by a Playwright route (as the net specs do), because the `?harness=` registry in main.ts belongs to M2-P7 and the builder container to M2-P5; Scryfall's /cards/search is answered from fixtures by a route. The app's fixture card source (debug/fixture-cards.ts) has no `searchCards`, so app-level browser tests see no search box until it gains one.
- [M2-P6] "Save changes" targets the record last saved or opened only while the builder still holds that pool: the list remembers the pool's non-basic cards (`id|sid|foil`) and treats a build that lacks any of them as another pool (no current record, empty name, "Save to library" makes a new record) — reason: DESIGN Local deck library ("reusable across evenings"): a new draft on the same page without a reload must never overwrite an earlier pool; edits (moves, search adds, dispensed basics) only add or return basics, so they keep the pool's identity without a reset seam from the evening flow.
- [M2-P6] Saving normalises the build as a library file holds it (one card per id, deck without unknown or repeated ids, positions only of pool cards, each x/y/rot), and import compares a stored record read as a file would be — reason: PLAN M2-P5/P6 "export then import reproduces the deck" and re-importing one's own export reports "already in the library" instead of a copy.
- [M2-P6] The search query is parenthesised before `game:paper` — reason: Scryfall's AND binds tighter than OR, so the paper filter must cover every clause.
- [M2-P6] The library's Save buttons wait for the first listing, and Save falls back to `decks.get` for the current record if the listing failed — reason: right after the list view remounts, a quick Save must still update the current record, not add one.
- [M2] The Playwright guard also waits while the 1-minute load average is above 3 × CPUs and aborts a run (exit 5) when it stays above 8 × CPUs for 30 s — both machine crashes were macOS watchdog panics under extreme load (load 280–450 was seen during the M2 run), and browser tests under such load only time out
- [M2] Workflow agents are told that a user message relayed mid-run is a status question, not a new task — in the M2 run, M2-P2, M2-P5 and the gate fixer each received "what's the progress?" and stopped after reporting status
- [M2-C amendment] Optional WinstonState.stackIds/pileIds and DraftState.taken (core/types.ts), optional DraftHost.confirmedState/sendCards/onCards (draft/types.ts), and the engine passes the draftCards pair message to its listeners (net/engine.ts) — M2-P3 needs them for Winston and the open formats; landed by the main session as a contracts amendment, copied from the m2-p3 branch, so the package diff no longer touches contract files
- [M2-P4] The draft surface (scene/draft.ts) is its own small Three.js scene on a private draft table (bird's-eye, fitted): the seats' waiting packs as piles of backs in a column at the left, the pack above (two arced rows of up to eight, never overlapping; three rows for the 3×3 grid or a pack over sixteen), the pool below in eight fixed colour columns W, U, B, R, G, multicolour, colourless, land (a land is a land whatever its colour; a double-faced card sorts by its front face; unknown data sorts as colourless), each column ordered by mana value, name, then pick order, cascade 14 compressed past 256 mm — reason: DESIGN "the current pack is a fan or grid on the player's private table … pool area, which auto-sorts by colour and stays visible"; fixed columns keep a card from jumping when a new colour arrives.
- [M2-P4] Mark and confirm: with one pick per pass a click marks (moving any other mark), a click on the marked card or Enter confirms; with N picks per pass clicks add marks up to N (a further card replaces the oldest), a click on a marked card confirms once N are marked and otherwise unmarks it; a short pack needs only what is left. Escape clears the marks; the arrow keys move the mark (Shift adds) for keyboard-only drafting; a Take button confirms for touch — reason: DESIGN gives the click-again rule for one card only; Pick-Two needs a rule for the second click.
- [M2-P4] Grid lines are taken with row and column handles beside the shared 3×3 (DOM buttons the surface positions; a line with no card left is disabled); a click on a grid card does nothing — reason: the design's Grid takes a row or column, never a card.
- [M2-P4] The waiting indicator counts every pack at a seat, the one being picked from included (`DraftState.queues[drafter].length`), shown both as a capped pile of backs (at most 8) with the count on the draft table and in the HUD's seat strip, where the seats the table waits for are emphasised; Two-Headed Giant seats show their team's queue. In formats that pass no packs (sealed, Rochester, Winston, Grid) there are no piles and the strip shows each seat's pool count — reason: DESIGN "waiting packs queue visibly behind each seat so everyone sees who is holding things up".
- [M2-P4] The pick timer shows m:ss counted from when this browser applied the pack's `sinceSeq` (`SeqClock`: a seq older than any seen, e.g. after a reload, counts from the first sighting; one not yet seen shows the full time), urgent below 10 s; the sequencer alone decides expiry — reason: DESIGN Timers.
- [M2-P4] Foils on the draft table carry a sheen and a badge, neither touching the face: under a theme with `cardDressing.foilSheen` the card gets a thin rim outside the face (0.9 mm, the highlight slot, so a hover or mark outline replaces it) whose light hue drifts over 6 s, each card out of phase, redrawn at 20 fps only while foils are shown and held still with reduced motion; the badge is a small ✦ ("Foil" as title and label) sized to the 8 mm gutter left of the card's top strip, so it covers no card, not even the one above it in a pool cascade — reason: DESIGN "Foils are marked with a subtle sheen and a badge"; card faces are never recoloured.
- [M2-P4] The lobby settings panel edits only for the host in the lobby (core/stage.ts `hostSeat`, the reducer's rule) and shows the same shared state read-only to everyone else; it offers the presets of `DRAFT_PRESETS` (Two-Headed Giant modes only at a Two-Headed Giant table) plus "Constructed decks (no draft)", shows a pass draft of 3 packs and 2 picks as the Pick-Two preset, clamps numbers to `LOBBY_LIMITS`, keeps the set list canonical (a per-pack list without trailing repeats, trimmed to the pack count; Grid has one set), lists the featured sets in FEATURED_SETS order and the rest in the source's order, keeps a chosen set the list lacks, warns in plain words when the seated count does not suit the mode, and sets the pass timer (`settings.passTimerMs`, off or 2–30 s). The table format is shown, not edited — reason: DESIGN Lobby settings; the table format is chosen with the chairs (M1-P5), and the stage check of seat counts is M2-P7's.
- [M2-P4] No contract file is edited: the pool's colour sort reads card data through `DraftSurfaceOptionsWithData` (scene/draft.ts: DraftSurfaceOptions plus optional `card(ref)`, as BuilderSurfaceOptions has), which Draft.svelte builds and `createDraftSurface` reads; the draft HUD uses Magic's game definition (`mtg`). Needed contracts amendment for the integrator: `DraftSurfaceOptions.card?(ref): CardData | null` — reason: CLAUDE.md, no contract edits outside a contracts package; without card data every pool card sorts as colourless.
- [M2-P4] The draft UI's browser specs run on a module page bundled from source by the spec itself (tests/e2e/draft/bundle.ts, like tests/e2e/net/bundle.ts) over `ui/draft/fakes.ts`: a table shared by local seats whose public actions go through the real reducer, and a simulated pass, Rochester, Grid, Winston and sealed draft standing in for the M2-P2/M2-P3 drivers — reason: `main.ts`'s harness list belongs to M2-P7 and the drivers are not merged yet.
- [M2-P4] Touch on the draft table: a tap marks or confirms like a click, a touch held 500 ms on a card without moving inspects it (touch has no hover key or middle button), a one-finger drag pans, and a second finger turns any one-finger press into a two-finger camera gesture (pinch zoom centred between the fingers plus two-finger pan) until the last finger lifts; the zoom follows the finger spread relative to the gesture's start, so the spread's jitter while panning cannot creep the zoom at the clamp — reason: DESIGN "Input and interaction design", Touch ("two-finger gestures always drive the camera") and "Camera and navigation" (two-finger pinch).
- [M2-P4] Marks count only while their card is in the pack shown (`liveMarks`): the status line, the Take button, Enter and the surface agree even when a driver keeps a stale mark (a Rochester card another seat took) — reason: one rule for what a confirm takes.
- [M2-P4] The grid and a Winston pile take no single card: the surface gets `picks: 0` for them and ignores card clicks, and the HUD sends mark and pick only for a pack; a Winston pile is taken or declined whole with the HUD's buttons — reason: DESIGN mode table, Winston "look at a pile and take it, or add a card and move on"; the DraftDriver reserves `pick` for pass and Rochester.
- [M2-P4] Hold Space with no card under the pointer inspects the card marked last, so keyboard-only drafting can read the marked card — reason: DESIGN Accessibility, every card action from the keyboard.
- [M2-P4] Cursors on the draft table are shared only while the pack shown lies on the shared table (a Rochester pack, the Grid): Draft.svelte then attaches scene/presence.ts over the draft surface (whose handle gains `worldToScreen` and `screenToTable`), reporting the pointer in table mm and naming a hovered card only when it belongs to the shared pack (never the own pool); nothing is dragged there, so no drag is sent. Over a pass pack or a Winston pile the layer is detached (the own cursor withdrawn) and nothing is sent or drawn — reason: DESIGN "Rochester picks happen on the shared table with cursors"; hidden packs must not leak even a pointer position. Shared packs are laid out alike for every viewer, so table points line up.
- [M2-P4] A drag anywhere on the draft table pans, a drag starting on a pack card included, with release inertia (not under reduced motion) — reason: dragging a draft card has no meaning and the pack covers most of the view, so a card-only dead zone would leave little room to pan; DESIGN "release inertia on pan".
- [M2-P4] A Winston pile is labelled "Pile k · N cards" and the status line shows "Stack N · piles a, b, c" for both seats — reason: DESIGN mode table, Winston: "A shared face-down stack and three piles"; the label rides on the view as `packLabel` (DraftSurfaceViewWithLabel in scene/draft.ts), no contract edit.
- [M2-P4] The draft HUD follows the prefers-reduced-motion media query live; the surface builds its theme with the current setting and rebuilds it when the setting changes; a theme change with a different card back gives the surface a fresh face texture set (FaceTextures takes its back once) — reason: DESIGN Accessibility and Themes; scene/faces.ts is not this package's.
- [M2-P4] The lobby panel's one-way controls (mode, set, per-pack sets, pick timer, pass timer) show what the table holds right after each write, so a refused or no-op write does not leave the rejected choice on screen — reason: shared state is the only truth.
- [M2-P4] The draft UI's fakes and module page stay in src/ui/draft (fakes.ts, harness.ts), as src/ui/fakes.ts and the `src/<name>/harness.ts` convention do; nothing in the app imports them — reason: CLAUDE.md module test pages convention.
- [M2-P4] Merged into m2 by the main session after the workflow's integrator stopped on machine-load e2e failures (its local merge f66bf17 was never pushed); e2e is rerun at the gate
- [M2-P3] Merged by the main session after the contracts amendment; no further review round, since round 3's only finding was the contract-file edits and the merge changed no behaviour
- [M2] Back to two Playwright runs per machine (from four), the memory floor raised to 25% available, and local Vitest runs capped at 4 workers (SPELLPLANE_TEST_WORKERS) — with four runs plus several parallel pnpm check runs the Mac ran out of memory and macOS killed all applications (12 Nov 2026)
- [M2-P2] A hidden `packOpen` (pass draft, sealed) lists the pack's sorted opaque ids and sorted commitments only, and must name a sealed pack-seed round (purpose 'pack') of its opener that is the opener's latest and newer than its previous pack, with the set of its pack number; a pass-draft pack must be of the current round, one per drafter per round — reason: DESIGN Draft packs (the generator commits, only it knows the contents) and Secret shuffle (the owner could not choose the seed), as M2-P3 does for the open formats.
- [M2-P2] `pick {packId, count}` names no card and must take exactly min(picks per pass, cards left) from the front pack of the picker's queue; the picked pack then stays with the picker (`holder`) until its `packPass`, which must go to `passTarget` of the pack's number with the exact count left; a round ends when all of its packs are open and empty — reason: DESIGN "each picks `picks` cards and passes the rest"; "a new round starts when all packs of the round are empty"; Pick-Two takes the last card of an odd pack alone.
- [M2-P2] Which cards a pick took is decided privately once the pick is sequenced: the marked cards of that pack, filled up with its first cards in pack order; `pick()` marks the chosen cards before proposing — reason: a `draftTimeout` ("the marked card, or else the first card") that races the drafter's own pick then takes the same cards, and identities never enter the public action.
- [M2-P2] `draftTimeout` takes the pick for the holder in shared state (the sequencer's clock, `draftDeadlines` keyed `pick:<pack>:<picks>`, from the `sinceSeq` at which the pack reached the front of its holder's queue), so the timer works while the holder is away; the holder resolves the cards when it has them — reason: DESIGN Timers (only the sequencer's clock decides) and pick timer.
- [M2-P2] Everything private about a pack follows from its visits (`packVisit`: visit k is the k-th holder from the opener in the pack's direction, holding size − k·picks cards), not from which actions a browser happened to apply: the pair channel accepts a `pack` only from the previous visit's holder to the current one with exactly that visit's cards (each opening the pack's commitment), also after the timer took that visit's pick and for the last visit of an emptied pack, in any stage of that draft — reason: a snapshot, a reload or an outage skips actions; without this a timed-out last pick of an away holder was lost, and a pass seen only after the pack emptied could put handed-on cards into the passer's pool.
- [M2-P2] A lost hand-over is recovered by asking: a holder of a visit without its cards sends an empty `pack` (a request) to the previous visit's holder after 2 s and every 2 s, who answers from a kept copy (`DraftSecrets.held['sent:<pack>']`); the copy is dropped when the receiver's visit is over, or, for the last visit of an emptied pack (no acknowledgement exists), when the match starts or the draft is replaced — reason: the pair channel has no acknowledgement and adding a message type was not needed.
- [M2-P2] Sealed: each browser opens its `packs` packs one at a time, each from its own pack seed, as face-down `packOpen`s whose count goes straight to the opener's pool (no picks, passes or timer); the cards enter the own pool in pack order once each `packOpen` is sequenced and never leave the browser — reason: DESIGN Sealed ("pools are generated and shown to their owners"; hidden information "own pool only") with the same commitments as the draft, so a later reveal can be checked.
- [M2-P2] `draftTimeout` joins `SEQUENCER_ONLY` in net/sequencer.ts (not a contract file; applied by the M2-P2 fixer on the review's blocking finding) — reason: the reducer cannot know the sequencer, so without it any seated peer could time out another seat's front pack before its timer ran out; DESIGN Timers "only the sequencer's clock decides".
- [M2-P2] Contracts amendment needed (not edited, CLAUDE.md): core/actions.ts DraftTimeoutAction still says "the holder's browser then sends the `pick`", and net/types.ts Sequencer.sequence lists only `away`, `commitClose` (and claims) as sequencer-only; they should say the reducer takes the pick for the holder (its own later `pick` is refused) and the holder's browser resolves the cards privately, and that `draftTimeout` is sequencer-only too — reason: M2-P7's session wiring reads these comments.
- [M2-P2] The pass driver never hands on a card of its own pool (`send` drops such a hand-over) — reason: DESIGN Draft packs, picked identities stay with the picker; the receiver's commitment and count checks cannot tell a picked card from a left one.
- [M2-P2] Gate-report items: (1) the timer takes an away holder's picks but its `packPass` still needs the holder, so with the timer on the draft still waits behind an away drafter until it returns (the design is silent; letting `draftTimeout` also move the pack is a later option); (2) a dishonest passer can hand on a card it picked and keep a left one, undetectably, since picks name no card — a later audit could check pools across seats for duplicate instance ids at the deck commitments.
- [M2-P2] The pass and sealed scenarios of the shared leak test are tests/sim/draft/pass/leak.test.ts on the shared scanner (tests/leak/draft.ts `packLeakScan`): what a seat may know is derived from the owners' side only (generated records, the public log, the pools), and every message it received, its state, log, secret store and view are scanned for any other card's salt, Scryfall id, id-to-card pairing and other seats' pack-seed nonces, with a negative control — reason: CLAUDE.md shared leak test, as M2-P3 placed its scenario.
- [M2-P2] Updated two expectations of earlier tests that pinned the stubs (tests/unit/core/m2-contracts.test.ts: packOpen/draftTimeout naming "M2-P2", now "no draft is in progress"; tests/unit/secrecy/private.test.ts: any pack delivered to anyone, now refused outside a pass draft) — reason: the stubs are implemented; the positive hand-over cases are in tests/unit/draft/pass/handover.test.ts and tests/sim/draft/pass/.
- [M2-P5] The builder table is a private 760 × 800 mm table split into a deck area (upper half) and a sideboard area (lower half); where a card lies decides deck membership, so a drop in the other area moves it and a double-click sends a card (or the selection) to the bottom of its column in the other area — reason: DESIGN "a deck area and a sideboard area are marked on the table" with "the same gestures as play"; membership by area keeps one rule for drags, selections and cascades.
- [M2-P5] Sort columns: colour W, U, B, R, G, multicoloured, colourless, lands; mana value 0…7+, lands; type with the list view's groups and precedence (creature first); cards still loading in a last column. Inside a column by mana value, name, printing, non-foil first, then pool order; cascade offset 14, compressed so a long column stays inside its area — reason: same groups as M2-P6's list view so both views read alike; ties by pool order make the sort stable.
- [M2-P5] Sorts form a stack: pressing the key of the sort now showing (nothing moved since) restores the layout from before that sort exactly and steps back one sort, so hand-made, colour, type, type, colour ends at the hand-made layout; a restore also brings back the column key in force before the undone sort (column labels, double-click and dispenser placement follow the layout on screen), and the surface reports that key to the container so the list view and a sort pressed while it shows use the same one; any move, dispense or list edit, or card data arriving for cards in the loading column, forgets the whole stack and the key sorts again — reason: PLAN M2-P5 "sorting is stable and reversible" without a separate undo control.
- [M2-P5] A dispensed basic dropped in the sideboard area leaves the build (back to the unlimited dispenser), as M2-P6 does in the list; table-dispensed ids are `table-basic-<n>`, the smallest unused. The dispenser's printings are the basics of the pool's sets by collector number (set data), then printings found in the pool, then one resolved printing per missing name; the first is the default and each dispensed basic keeps the printing it was dispensed with. Its count shows every basic of that name in the deck; − returns only the latest dispensed copy, never a drafted one. — reason: DESIGN "hands out unlimited basics of any chosen printing" (the pool's own sets first, so the deck matches what was drafted); returning only dispensed copies keeps drafted basics, which are part of the pool, from vanishing.
- [M2-P5] Readout from the main deck only: lands by the front face's type line, curve of non-lands by mana value (7+ together), colour split counts coloured mana symbols (a hybrid counts once for each of its colours, {C} as colourless; split, flip and adventure cards count both halves, transforming and modal double-faced cards their front face only); cards whose data is loading count toward the total only — reason: ui/types.ts BuilderStats; the front face is how a double-faced card is cast.
- [M2-P5] Ready calls `DraftControls.ready()` (the M2-C seam), which commits `TableSession.importDeck(deckImportOf(build))`; `deckImportOf` moved to src/session/deck-build.ts so M2-P7's DraftControls can use it (draft/ and session/ may not import ui/), and the builder harness's DraftControls stand-in does exactly that. Integrator/M2-P7: importDeck replaces `SecretStore.pool` with the sideboard, so P7's ready() should keep the drafted picks if anything later reads `pool` as the whole pool: the deck in deck order as the library, the rest of the pool as the sideboard without dispensed basics; it is allowed below 40 cards (hint only) and off with an empty deck. The builder counts as locked while the seat is ready or holds commitments: drags, double-clicks, sorts and the dispenser change nothing, camera and inspect still work — reason: DESIGN "Ready locks the deck and publishes its commitment"; the M1 commitment path already covers the leak test.
- [M2-P5] Surface extras beyond the M2-C contract (the lock in the view, `sorted` and `selection` events, debug cards with area and deck membership) are typed in scene/builder.ts (`BuilderSurfaceViewExt`, `BuilderSurfaceOptionsExt`, `BuilderSurfaceHandle`) and used only when the surface is this module's (`builderHandleOf`) — reason: contract files stay untouched; a test double of the contract still works.
- [M2-P5] The builder container takes optional `origin` (the deck library's origin; else derived from the draft), `sets` (SetDataSource for the dispenser's printings) and `view` (initial view) beside BuilderProps; M2-P7 passes `sets` from main.ts. Keys: 1 colour, 2 mana value, 3 type, 0 fit, Escape clears the selection or closes inspect, hold Space over a card to inspect. — reason: BuilderProps (ui/types.ts) is a contract file and lacks them; optional props keep M2-P7's call site free to pass them or not.
- [M2-P5] A double-click on a card of a multi-card selection sends the whole selection across, although the double-click's first click would narrow the selection to that card (the surface remembers the selection the click replaced) — reason: DESIGN "All actions apply to the selection".
- [M2-P5] The builder's Playwright specs mount the real container over a solo session in a page bundled from tests/e2e/builder/page.ts and served by a Playwright route (as the draft and list specs do), because the `?harness=` registry in main.ts belongs to M2-P7.
- [M2-P5] A card belongs to the area nearer its centre (y ≥ 0 deck, else sideboard), so the unmarked band between the outlines and the space past them count for the nearer area; a moved card in a cascade or squared pile follows its pile's base card (`areasOf`), so a pile dragged or dropped across the line is wholly deck or wholly sideboard; a drop never changes the membership of a card that was not moved, even when the dropped card becomes the base of a cascade over it (such a card keeps its spot while its own spot lies in its membership's area, `settledArea`) — reason: DESIGN "a deck area and a sideboard area are marked on the table" with piles moving as one; snapping drops into an area would move cards the player placed.
- [M2-P5] Squared piles on the builder table show as on the play table: members step up 0.4 mm per card (capped at 20) and a `×n` count badge sits at the top card's top-left corner; a column with no room for another 14 mm step (a dispensed basic, a double-click across, a list edit) re-lays its cards as one cascade compressed to fit the area instead of stacking the new card on the last one — reason: DESIGN "Dropping squarely on a card makes a squared pile with a count badge"; a limited deck's 19 lands no longer fit 14 apart in the deck area.
- [M2-P5] Builder gestures that deliberately differ from play (gate report): double-click sends a card or the selection to the other area instead of tapping or fanning (there is nothing to tap in the builder; a sort or a drag fans piles); Tidy (Y) is left out (the sort keys re-flow the table); the drag lift has no 4° tilt, since the shared CardLayer has no tilt input yet; foils carry the draft's sheen (scene/draft.ts `foilSheenColour` in the highlight slot, not while selected or hovered) and a badge; a squared pile is dragged whole by its count badge, as on the play table (M0-P3), any other press on its top card pulls that card out; the selection label shows the count and summed power/toughness — reason: DESIGN "the same gestures as play" read for a table without game actions; tilt belongs to the shared card renderer, not to one surface.
- [M2-P5] The builder table's frame loop stops while the list view hides it (display: none) and restarts on the next resize; its debug `idle()` reports idle while hidden; the camera's fit insets follow the panel, which moves to the bottom 40% below 720 px — reason: no rendering nobody sees, and a fit that keeps the table clear of the panel in both layouts.
- [M2-P7] Stage rules (core/stage.ts `stageProblem`): the sender holds a seat; lobby → draft by the host when the lobby chose a draft and the seated count suits it (`FORMAT_SEATS`); lobby → match by the host with no Ready check in core (the constructed path: M1's lobby checks Ready, and the solo table goes straight to play); draft → build by anyone once `draft.done`; draft → lobby by the host (abandon); build → match by anyone once every seated player has a commitment; match → build, match → lobby and build → lobby only with `round` naming the current confirmation round, labelled for that proposal (`STAGE_ROUND_LABELS`: "Next game?", "New draft?") and passed — reason: DESIGN Evening stages ("the host advances from lobby; draft advances automatically; build advances when all seated players are Ready; from match any player proposes … and the others confirm"); a labelled round keeps an unrelated round ("attacks?") from moving the evening.
- [M2-P7] The host for stage moves is the lowest seated seat that is not away (`actingHost`), falling back to `hostSeat` — reason: the M1 lobby's start button already uses that rule, and an away host must not stall the evening (DESIGN "Multiplayer architecture": an away seat never stalls the table); `lobbySet` keeps M2-C's `hostSeat`.
- [M2-P7] `enterStage`: entering the draft creates `TableState.draft` on a cleared table; match → build clears the game (cards, zone contents, stack, lines, round, commitments, randomness rounds, game shuffles; Ready, auto-pass, counters and recoveries reset; life and the turn strip back to a game's start) and keeps chairs, settings, lobby, the draft and its pack-seed rounds; any → lobby (new draft) clears the game and drops the draft and its pack rounds; every entry into a match counts `games` (the M1 constructed path too, so M1 state hashes gain `games`; no test pins one) — reason: DESIGN "between games, players return to the builder to sideboard"; pools stay countable through build and match (M2-C).
- [M2-P7] A game's starting life and first phase are `GAME_START` in core/stage.ts (mtg: 20, 30 per team in Two-Headed Giant; untap), mirrored from games/mtg and kept in step by a unit test; for another game id life and phase stay as they were — reason: the reducer has no game definition, and a rematch must start at the starting life.
- [M2-P7] A next game is refused while a game shuffle is still open (M1-P2 freezes its zone); the acting host's browser proposes it again after the next sequenced action — reason: clearing a zone mid-shuffle would let the owner restart a shuffle whose peer nonces it may already know.
- [M2-P7] The Two-Headed Giant drafts are refused at the start until M3-P2 brings their drivers — reason: `DRAFT_DRIVERS` throws for them, and a draft that cannot run must not start.
- [M2-P7] The automatic steps (draft done → build, everyone Ready → match, a passed proposal → its stage) are proposed by the acting host's browser only, once per confirmed seq — reason: the table moves on together without every browser racing to send the same `stageSet` and logging refusals.
- [M2-P7] The draft driver lives as long as `TableState.draft` (through build and match) and is replaced when a new draft starts (`startSeq` changes); `drive()` runs after every sequenced action, whenever a peer joins or leaves, and every second (`DRIVE_MS`); sequenced actions reach the driver from a microtask queue, in order, each with its confirmed state — reason: hand-overs the pick timer took and Winston cards shown after the last look arrive after the draft ended (DECISIONS [M2-P2], [M2-P3]); a driver waiting for a lost hand-over has no other clock; a driver never runs inside the engine's own dispatch.
- [M2-P7] `DraftHost.packSeed` (session/net.ts): `shuffleCommit` purpose 'pack' on `pack:<seat>` with a fresh nonce kept in the local nonce store (kind 'shuffle') until the round is done; the session's `drive` finishes own pack rounds once ready, also after a reload; a round whose nonce was lost is closed unused (a random order hash) so the pack zone is free again; seeds run one after another — reason: DESIGN Draft packs (the secret-shuffle seed protocol with purpose 'pack'); a reload mid-seed must neither freeze the zone nor open a second round.
- [M2-P7] The deck being built from a drafted pool uses pool card ids `p1`, `p2`, … in pick order; picks that reach the pool after the build was made are added to the sideboard; Ready commits `deckImportOf(build)` and keeps `SecretStore.pool` as the drafted pool (importDeck would make it the sideboard) — reason: DESIGN "two views of the same pool"; a reload or a late hand-over must neither lose nor duplicate a card.
- [M2-P7] A constructed deck is kept as `SecretStore.build` when `importDeck` commits it at a table without a draft (main deck as deck, sideboard beside it), so "next game" after a constructed match also returns to the builder to sideboard — reason: DESIGN Evening stages (match → build) applies to every match; the builder needs a build.
- [M2-P7] Private state of an earlier draft is dropped (draft secrets, pool, build) when the confirmed table is back in the lobby, and when a new draft starts with no draft secrets yet (a constructed build or sideboard goes too) or with stale ones (a generated pack whose seed round the table no longer has, or more picks than the table counts) — reason: "new draft on the same link" must start from an empty pool, also for a page that missed the lobby.
- [M2-P7] The solo session offers neither `draft` nor `evening` — reason: the solo table has one player, every draft mode needs two, and it goes straight to play (M0).
- [M2-P7] The lobby stage shows M1's lobby beside a side panel with M2-P4's lobby settings and a start button for the chosen draft ("Start the draft", "Open the sealed pools", …; host only, with the reason it is blocked); M1's "Start the game" stays for constructed decks — reason: Lobby.svelte belongs to M1-P5, and both paths are edges of the evening (lobby → draft | match).
- [M2-P7] The evening bar sits at the bottom centre of the builder and the bottom-left corner of the match (below the own avatar, clear of the hand fan): it says what the table waits for and offers "Next game" (match) and "New draft" (match, builder); in a match the HUD's confirmation prompt answers a proposal, in the builder the bar's own OK and Hold — reason: the HUD already prompts for every round, the builder has no prompt.
- [M2-P7] The HUD is remounted for every game (keyed by `games`) and the builder for every draft (keyed by `draft.startSeq`) — reason: both read the table and the build once at mount; a fresh view per game avoids stale scene state.
- [M2-P7] The lobby offers the pick timer only for the pass drafts (ui/draft/settings.ts `hasPickTimer` drops Rochester) — reason: `draftDeadlines` times held packs only, so in Rochester, Winston and Grid the setting did nothing (gate-report item of M2-P3/M2-P4); timing the Rochester picker is left for later.
- [M2-P7] Browser tests inject MTGJSON booster data as `window.__spellplaneSets` (debug/fixture-sets.ts, read by main.ts in dev and test builds), and the fixture card source answers `searchCards` from the fixtures (name, face names and type line contain every word) — reason: tests never use the network; M2-P6's search box needs a source in app-level tests.
- [M2-P7] The debug hook gains a `flow` part (setLobby, start, propose, mark, pick, pileLook, gridTake, setBuild, ready) besides M2-C's `draft` part — reason: browser tests drive the evening the way the HUD does and assert on the hook, not on pixels.
- [M2-P7] ui/lobby/RoomRoute.svelte (M1-P5's) passes `sets` on to the table room; ui/lobby/TableRoom.svelte renders the stage switch ui/flow/Stage.svelte — reason: the plan gives M2-P7 the stage switch; the set picker needs the app's set data.
- [M2-G] The gate's two players share one browser context (two pages, distinct `?profile=`), not two contexts — BroadcastChannel (`?transport=local`) joins pages of one context only; the profiles keep IndexedDB and key pairs apart, as in the M1 gate.
- [M2-G] The draft leak scan derives what a seat may know from the other seat's side only (each remainder it passed on after a keyboard pick, plus its own generated packs) and scans the seat's received messages, state, secret store and view with tests/leak/draft.ts `packLeakScan` — the browser counterpart of the M2-P2 simulated scenario.
- [M2-G] Draft picks in the gate use ArrowRight then Enter (the design's keyboard path) rather than clicks on the draft surface — the app exposes no screen rectangles for draft cards through the debug hook, and the keys are a documented UI path; a failed key pick falls back to `flow.pick` and is listed as a gate failure.
- [M2-G] Merged M2-P7 into m2 in the gate fix round and accepted its one-line `sets` pass-through in ui/lobby/RoomRoute.svelte (M1-P5's file) — reason: the stage switch needs the app's set data and RoomRoute is the only path from App to TableRoom; moving it would mean a second prop route for one value.
- [M2] The Playwright guard counts a macOS prompt process only while it has a window on screen (CGWindowList via osascript JXA) — a UserNotificationCenter process lingered windowless for 45 minutes after the crash and blocked every browser run of the M2 workflow (P7, gate, fix round)
- [M2-G] Pages that inject card fixtures but no set data get an empty set source instead of MTGJSON — the evening's lobby asked MTGJSON for its set list in the M0/M1 gate specs, breaking 'tests never use the network' (found by the M1 gate rerun and the independent M2-P7 review)
- [M2-G] `withLatePicks` tops up a build only when every pool card id `p<n>` in it names `pool[n-1]` (sid and foil) — a drafted deck saved from an earlier draft keeps ids p1…pN, so the ids alone took tonight's pool as its late picks; comparing refs needs no new field in the DeckBuild contract and a pool card's ref never changes in the builder
- [M2-G] The session's hidden-pack leak scenarios (tests/sim/flow/leak.test.ts: four-seat pass draft, two-seat Winston) derive what a seat may know with the M2-P2/M2-P3 sim helpers, now taking any start state and log (`visitsOf`, `allowedFrom`), over the host's log replayed from the table before the draft and checked against its confirmed draft — one derivation for the sim and the session scenarios, independent of the disclosure code under test
- [M2-G] Those scenarios reload a page on a new transport id (`reloadPage`), as Trystero gives every page load its own id — the harness's `reload` reuses the loopback id, so messages in flight to the old page reached the new one, failed its handshake and forked the history in mid-draft, which a real reload cannot do
- [M2-G] Gate green after the main session ran the browser suites one at a time, had M2-P7 reviewed independently (it was merged unreviewed) and fixed the blocking network fetch plus two minor findings; the pass-draft driver acting on abandoned history is recorded as a follow-up in docs/gates/M2.md
- [M2-G] A claimant marks its fork check done on the answer to any non-tail read of the peer it takes over from, not only an `overlap` one — a claimant at seq 1 sends its overlap read as a snapshot request (the overlap would start at its head), and when that peer stood at seq 1 too the snapshot was not loaded, so the claim asked again for ever and nothing more was sequenced; CI hit it on WebKit (net/engine.spec) when Ben's page sequenced its first action alone and Anna's page caught up from it before claiming; regression test in tests/sim/net/protocol.test.ts (seeds 17 and 29)
- [M2-G] net/engine.spec waits for both pages to have met before a test acts — a page whose handshake has not finished sequences alone, and the lowest seat's takeover then logs a handover record, so the specs' seq counts depended on handshake speed
- [M2-G] confirm.spec checks what a gesture logged with the log minus the sequencer's timer passes (`acted`), never the raw log's last entry or length — a round's pass timer may run out during or after a slow gesture and its `respond … timeout` legitimately lands after it (CI: stackReorder and stackNote followed by Anna's browser passing Ben)
- [M2-G] confirm.spec runs tests whose premise is that a round still waits (answer, hold, resolve with a waiting round) with a pass timer no test outlives, and the tests about the timer with 4 s instead of 2 s — with 2 s the keys a test presses before the deadline had to fit in 2 s of a loaded CI runner
- [M2-G] The builder table's sorting acceptance test is split into "reversible" and "stable", sharing the hand-made-layout setup, and reads the column labels in one round trip — on the CI runner each Playwright call took ~250 ms and the single test ran out of its 60 s budget; no timeout was raised
- [M2-G] flow/evening.spec polls for Ben's pool to hold his fourth pick before it reads the pool and the front pack — `pick` returns once the pick is proposed, and the pool grows only when Anna's sequencer has applied it (found by the full e2e run of the CI fixes)

## M3 Four seats and voice

- [M3-C] Teams stay as M0 shaped them: `Team {seats, life, counters}` holds the one shared life total and `turn.active` names the team id, so the shared life and turn marker need no new state. `core/teams.ts` adds the format facts (`TABLE_FORMAT_SEATS`, `TABLE_FORMAT_TEAMS`: Two-Headed Giant teams are seats 0+1 = 't0' and 2+3 = 't1', matching the layout's seat angles 0°, 0°, 180°, 180°) and pure selectors (`teamOf`, `teammatesOf`, `activeSeats`, `isSeatActive`, `revealRecipients`) — reason: DESIGN "Data model and protocol" already carries teams and a team turn marker; changes are additive. The names avoid `FORMAT_SEATS` in core/stage.ts, which counts draft seats.
- [M3-C] New action `formatSet {format, life}` (meta, host only, lobby only, no seat Ready; reducer M3-P1, stub refuses). It rebuilds the seats, seat zones and teams for the format, and sets every life total to `life`, which the client takes from `startingLife` because the reducer holds no game definition — reason: DESIGN Lobby settings list the format. The table format was fixed at table creation, and a joiner at seq 0 could start a different one. `Draft` gains `format`/`setFormat`.
- [M3-C] A spectator's id is `v:<peerId>` (`spectatorIdOf`). It is the `by` of a spectator's actions and never a seat key; spectators are not in `TableState`. A spectator's `chat` carries its nickname in the new optional `name`. `TableSession.seat` is the spectator id for a spectator session, with `role: 'spectator'` — reason: the sequencer already refuses spectator actions whose `by` is a seat, the design keeps spectators out of shared state, and `seat: SeatId` stays non-null for every existing consumer.
- [M3-C] Spectators may propose `chat` and `ping` only (`net/roles.ts SPECTATOR_ACTIONS`, wired into the sequencer by M3-P5); today's sequencer allows the whole 'social' family, which lacks ping — reason: DESIGN Spectators "can chat and ping, and cannot move cards".
- [M3-C] A reveal to a named spectator is `revealTo` with the new optional `spectators` (seats may then be empty), and `NetEngine.sendPrivate`/`PrivateChannel.reveal` accept spectator ids (routing: M3-P5) — reason: additive; the identity still travels on one pair's `prv` channel only. A team reveal is `RevealTarget {kind: 'team'}`, expanded to the team's seats minus the revealer before dispatch, so the public action lists seats as before.
- [M3-C] Team text chat never enters the shared log. It goes to the teammate on `prv` as the new `teamChat {cid, text, atSeq}` message (`TeamChannel`, net/team.ts, M3-P2), the reducer now refuses `chat` on the 'team' channel, and `TeamControls.chatLog()` returns the lines as local log entries for the timeline — reason: DESIGN "A team channel carries voice and text only between teammates"; a shared action would reach the opponents' browsers.
- [M3-C] Team talk is not announced on the `speaking` presence message (it is broadcast). The teammate's browser lights the ring from the incoming audio, and opponents see no ring — reason: presence goes to every peer, and the team channel is private.
- [M3-C] Talk mode in settings is the new key `talk: {gate: 'ptt'|'turn'|'open', voice, dictation, muted, mouseButton}` (default push-to-talk, voice on, dictation off), plus `micDevice` and `peerAudio` (per identity peer id). The old `talkMode` is kept so older saves load, and M3-P3 maps it onto `talk` once — reason: DESIGN lets voice and dictation run together and combines dictation with the turn mic, which one four-valued mode cannot express. The talk key itself stays the keymap's 'talk' binding (hold V; Shift+V team talk).
- [M3-C] The talk rule is a pure function `talkState(TalkInputs) → TalkState {audio: off|all|team, dictation: off|caption|field, reason, muted}` (voice/modes.ts, M3-P3); the indicator states are noMic, muted, idle, live, team, dictating — reason: PLAN M3-P3's tests assert the track state for each mode, and the gate screenshots the indicator states.
- [M3-C] Speech engines: `SpeechEngineId` is 'webspeech' | 'local' | 'fake'. The local Whisper-class engine is a `LocalSpeechEngine` with `load(onProgress)`, created by a `SpeechEngineFactory` whose `create` dynamic-imports it, so nothing is downloaded unless it is chosen. Each engine states where audio goes ('vendor', 'device', 'none') — reason: DESIGN Engine and PLAN M3-P4 ("not downloaded unless chosen").
- [M3-C] Captions: `Caption {speaker, text, final, anchor, about?}` in voice/types.ts, drawn by the scene from the new optional `ViewState.captions` (`SceneCaption`). A final caption lingers 4 s (`CAPTION_LINGER_MS`) before it fades. A spectator's caption without an anchor sits at the table's near edge — reason: DESIGN says only "fades a few seconds after the final result"; the scene owns the anchor positions (cards, stack items, avatars).
- [M3-C] Layout descriptors: `TableLayout` gains optional `turnOrder` (seat indices clockwise from seat 0) and `teams` (`TeamLayout {seats, territory, avatar}`, Two-Headed Giant only), and `SeatLayout` gains `team`; mtgLayout fills them provisionally (team territory = the two adjoining territories, shared avatar outside the team's near edge, centred), and M3-P1 tunes them — reason: the shared life total and team turn marker need one anchor per team; additive.
- [M3-C] The teammate's hand fan is 14% of viewport height (`TEAM_FAN_HEIGHT`), above the own fan at 22% — reason: DESIGN "a second, smaller fan above the player's own" gives no size.
- [M3-C] `Transport` gains optional `removeStream` (Trystero has it), `TableSession` optional `role`, `team`, `voice`, `captions`, `NetSessionOptions` optional `role` and `voice`, and the debug hook `voice` and `speech` parts — reason: additive seams so M3-P2..P5 need no further contract edits; each package wires its own session module.
- [M3-C] Spectators join through `#/t/<room>/<key>/watch` (M3-P5) — reason: today the networked session takes a chair on joining, so the choice to watch must be made before the session starts; the same link plus a suffix keeps "joins by the same link".
- [M3-P1] Every format seats its players clockwise in seat-index order as seen from above (seat 0 at the bottom), so turn order (`TableLayout.turnOrder` = seat indices), the seat keys 2–4 ("clockwise from you", ui/commands.ts `clockwiseSeat`) and the draft's pass to the left all follow the seat index. Two-Headed Giant therefore puts seat 0 at the near right and seat 1 at the near left (seat 2 far left, seat 3 far right); `TeamLayout.seats` lists them left to right as the team sees its edge, t0 = [1, 0], t1 = [3, 2] — reason: the provisional layout ran its seats counter-clockwise there, which made the seat keys and the clockwise turn order disagree with the table.
- [M3-P1] Four players: the spec's 1100 × 440 territories centred on 1800-wide sides overlap 90 × 90 at every corner, so each is shifted 90 toward its owner's right (a pinwheel, `MTG_LAYOUT.ffa4Shift`) — reason: keeps the spec's table size, territory size and 920 × 920 centre with no overlap; each corner keeps a 440 × 260 unused patch instead of the spec's "360 corners" (which does not add up with the other numbers).
- [M3-P1] Three players keep the spec geometry (seat 0 along the whole near edge, seats 1 and 2 sharing the far edge): seats 1 and 2 see themselves at the bottom beside each other with seat 0 across, not "opponents at top-left and top-right", which only holds from seat 0 — reason: the geometry spec overrides the looser layout column, and one shared canonical frame turned per viewer cannot look the same from all three seats.
- [M3-P1] `formatSet` refuses while any chair the new format drops is occupied or still owns a card or stack item (occupied chairs are never moved, so a peer's seat id never changes under it), and while anyone is Ready; new chairs take the lowest seat colour nobody uses; lines that name a dropped seat go — reason: DECISIONS [M3-C] rules plus the simplest way to keep every invariant and seat identity.
- [M3-P1] The host's format choice is a select in the lobby settings panel (ui/draft/LobbySettings.svelte); its life comes from core/stage.ts `GAME_START` (the panel has no game definition), and a Two-Headed Giant draft mode falls back to the first mode offered when the table leaves Two-Headed Giant (and the reverse).
- [M3-P1] The unassigned-attacker stub counts opposing sides, not seats: a Two-Headed Giant team counts once (its creatures attack the team, so the target is implicit, as in 1v1) and an empty chair not at all.
- [M3-P1] The scene draws each seat's avatar spot (outside its near edge, where lines to a player end and cards dropped go to that hand) as a hairline ring with the player's name, upright for the viewer; a tap on it goes to that seat, as does a click on the name in a HUD avatar (new optional `ongoto` prop of ui/Avatar.svelte) — reason: DESIGN "Go to another seat: tap an avatar", and with three or four seats a line to a player needs a visible end on the table.
- [M3-P1] The table scene follows `TableState.format`: when it differs from its layout's format it takes `game.layout(format)` (the HUD mounts the scene once per match). The debug hook gains `tableView()` (format, viewer angle, camera centre, each seat's territory and avatar in table mm and on screen), and the scene harness takes `&format=ffa3|ffa4|2hg&viewer=s0..s3` — reason: browser tests assert the per-viewer rotation and line ends on the hook, not on pixels.
- [M3-P1] [games/mtg] Every format seats its players clockwise in seat-index order. In Two-Headed Giant this puts seat 0 at the near right and seat 1 at the near left, and TeamLayout.seats (left to right) becomes t0=[1,0], t1=[3,2] — reason: the placeholder layout ran counter-clockwise there, so the seat keys and turn order disagreed with the table
- [M3-P1] [games/mtg] Four players: each 1100x440 territory is shifted 90 mm toward its owner's right (a pinwheel) — reason: centred on the 1800 mm sides they overlap 90x90 at every corner; the shift keeps the spec's table size, territory size and 920x920 centre
- [M3-P1] [games/mtg] Three players keep the spec geometry, so seats 1 and 2 see themselves at the bottom side by side with seat 0 across; 'opponents top-left and top-right' only holds for seat 0 — reason: the geometry spec overrides the looser layout column
- [M3-P1] [core] formatSet refuses when a chair the new format drops is occupied or still owns a card or stack item, and when anyone is Ready; new chairs take the lowest free seat colour; lines naming a dropped seat are removed — reason: keeps every invariant and never changes a seat id under a peer
- [M3-P1] [ui/draft] The host's format choice is a select in LobbySettings; its starting life comes from core/stage GAME_START; a Two-Headed Giant draft mode falls back to the first offered mode when the format changes — reason: the panel has no game definition
- [M3-P1] [scene] The unassigned-attacker stub counts opposing sides: a Two-Headed Giant team counts once and an empty chair not at all — reason: the attack target is implicit against a single team, as in 1v1
- [M3-P1] [scene] Each taken seat's avatar spot is drawn as a ring with the player's name and is tappable to move the camera; HUD avatars got an optional ongoto prop (ui/Avatar.svelte, Hud.svelte: small additive edits to files from earlier milestones) — reason: DESIGN 'tap an avatar', and a line to a player needs a visible end
- [M3-P1] [scene] The table scene follows TableState.format (game.layout(format)); new debug-hook part tableView; the scene harness takes format and viewer parameters — reason: browser tests check rotation and line ends on the hook, not on pixels
- [M3-P1] [tests] Updated M0-P6's tests/unit/games/layout.test.ts and M3-C's tests/unit/core/m3-contracts.test.ts where they asserted the placeholder layouts, the formatSet stub and the old Two-Headed Giant team seat order
- [M3-P1] [follow-up for M3-P2 or the session] session/net.ts freshNetTable creates a Two-Headed Giant table without teams; formatSet adds them, so the lobby path works, but a room created directly as 2hg has none
- [M3-P5] A spectator's proposal is refused unless its type is `chat` or `ping`, its `by` is the sender's own spectator id `v:<peerId>` (never a seat, even when the spectator's key pair holds a chair), a chat goes on the table or spectator tab and its `name` is the nickname its connection announced in the handshake (net/roles.ts `spectatorChatName`). The sequencer's check runs the identity-free part (`spectatorActionProblem`: type, seat, tab); the engine runs `roleProblem` and `spectatorNameProblem` next to `senderPeerCheck` before calling `sequence`, so the `Sequencer` contract is unchanged — reason: DESIGN Spectators "can chat and ping, and cannot move cards"; the `by` and name checks need the sender's identity, which only the engine knows.
- [M3-P5] A spectator's session (session/spectator.ts) proposes every dispatch, card actions included, and lets the sequencer refuse them (rolled back with the reason as a system line), instead of filtering locally; only while no seated player's page is connected to sequence, a dispatch the role rule refuses is refused on the page at once (not left applied and pending). Its refused seat-only calls (draw, shuffle, mulligan, roll, importDeck, lookAt) return a rejected promise that is already handled, so the HUD's fire-and-forget calls raise no unhandled rejection — reason: the sequencer is the authority (PLAN M3-P5 acceptance: "dispatches of card actions are rejected by the sequencer"), and a filtered client would hide a broken rule; "cannot move cards" should hold on the spectator's own page too.
- [M3-P5] A spectator stores nothing: no table record, log or secrets in IndexedDB, and its replica starts from the seq-0 table and catches up from the players on every join — reason: it owns no hidden information to keep, and keeping a log would add a resume path for no benefit.
- [M3-P5] A page joined as a spectator holds no chair, even when its key pair holds one at this table: its engine's `seat()` is null, peers record it with seat null, a seat's pair-channel messages never go to it, its private messages carry no seat, and it does not keep that chair from being marked away. The spectator session leaves the room as soon as its replica shows its key pair holding a chair, and the watch page says so and offers "Take your chair" — reason: hidden information of a chair must only reach the page that plays it; the engine keeps one connection per identity (liveTwin), so a watch page left in the room would shut that chair's player page out until it closed (Multiplayer architecture, Resume: "Reloading or reopening the link rejoins the same seat by key pair").
- [M3-P5] Only a targeted reveal (`reveal` with purpose 'revealTo') goes to a spectator, and only to the verified, unseated connection of exactly that identity that joined as a spectator; a spectator takes it only once the owner's sequenced `revealTo` names it in `spectators` for that card (the sender's chair looked up by peer id when the reveal came before the page caught up), checks it against the owner's commitment, and forgets it when the card turns public, is re-issued or goes where nobody sees it (pruneReceived). The owner's side is net/roles.ts `spectatorRevealSender`, mounted by one line in session/net.ts: it sends on the sequenced own `revealTo` and keeps the reveal owed while the card stays hidden, the owner's and in the same zone, sending it again when that spectator's connection is verified again (a reconnect or a reload), at once and after 1.5, 5 and 15 s since the spectator's engine drops pair-channel messages from a peer it has not verified yet. A spectator that catches up from a snapshot rather than the action tail (more than 500 actions behind) never sees the `revealTo` and so does not take the repeated reveal — reason: DESIGN Targeted reveal, on that pair's channel only; a spectator keeps nothing, so a reload must not lose a hand shown to it.
- [M3-P5] "Reveal their hand to a named spectator" is a one-time reveal of the hand as it is (one `revealTo` per hand card naming that spectator, in one undo group), offered per connected spectator as "Show my hand" in a players' panel (bottom-right at the table; the lobby shows the watch link and who watches) — reason: the design does not say the reveal follows the hand; the Hud's "Reveal to…" menu (M0-P4's file) still lists seats only.
- [M3-P5] Spectators watch the match through the regular HUD from seat 0's side with a "Watching" bar that lists the cards shown to them; before the match they see the stage and the seated players; the HUD's toolbar is unchanged (its card tools are refused by the sequencer) — reason: the HUD already draws the public table, pings and chat, and its files are not M3-P5's.
- [M3-P5] A spectator chats on the table tab by default (the Hud offers no spectator tab yet); `chat(…, 'team')` from a spectator goes to the spectator tab. Log lines name a spectator by the nickname its chat carries ("Viv (spectator)"), which the engine binds to its handshake nickname (the name players see in the spectator list); a spectator's ping as "A spectator" (the `ping` action carries no name), and a reveal to spectators as "to a spectator" / "to N spectators" — reason: spectators are not in the shared state, so the log has no seat name for them.
