- [M3-P5] A player's join that fails (a full table) or is still joining offers "Watch this table as a spectator" (the /watch link); a pasted /watch link on the start page opens the watch route; the spectator page offers "Take a chair instead" — reason: DESIGN "joins by the same link without taking a chair"; the choice to watch must be made before the session starts (DECISIONS [M3-C]).
- [M3-P5] Tests owned by M1-P1 and M3-C were updated for the new rule: tests/sim/net/protocol.test.ts's spectator now chats as `v:<peerId>` (the engine refuses another `by`), and tests/unit/core/m3-contracts.test.ts no longer expects net/roles.ts stubs to throw — reason: a spectator's `by` must be its own spectator id.
- [M3-P5] Sequencer refuses a spectator proposal unless it is chat or ping, `by` is the sender's own `v:<peerId>` (never a seat, even when its key pair holds a chair), chat is on the table or spectator tab, and `name` is a 1–40 character nickname — reason: DESIGN Spectators 'can chat and ping, and cannot move cards'; the `by` check needs the sender's identity
- [M3-P5] The spectator session proposes every dispatch, card actions included, and the sequencer refuses them (rolled back with a system line) instead of filtering locally — reason: PLAN acceptance says the sequencer rejects them, and a filtered client would hide a broken rule
- [M3-P5] A spectator stores nothing in IndexedDB; its copy of the table starts at seq 0 and catches up from the players — reason: it owns no hidden information, and a saved log adds a resume path with no benefit
- [M3-P5] A spectator page holds no chair even under a seated key pair: engine seat() is null, peers record it with seat null, a seat's private messages never go to it, its own private messages carry no seat, and the watched chair is marked away — reason: a chair's hidden cards must only reach the page that plays it
- [M3-P5] Only a 'revealTo' reveal goes to a spectator, and only to that verified, unseated identity; the spectator takes it only after the owner's sequenced revealTo names it, checks it against the owner's commitment, and forgets it when the card turns public, is re-issued or goes back into a hidden zone — reason: DESIGN targeted reveal travels on one pair's channel only
- [M3-P5] Revealing a hand to a named spectator is a one-time reveal of the current hand (one revealTo per card, one undo group) through a 'Show my hand' button per spectator in a players' panel — reason: the design does not say the reveal follows the hand; the HUD's 'Reveal to…' menu is not M3-P5's file
- [M3-P5] Spectators watch the match through the existing HUD from seat 0's side plus a 'Watching' bar; before the match they see the stage and the seated players; the HUD toolbar is unchanged and its card tools are refused by the sequencer — reason: the HUD already draws the public table, chat and pings, and its files belong to other packages
- [M3-P5] Spectators chat on the table tab (the HUD has no spectator tab yet); a team-tab chat from a spectator goes to the spectator tab; log lines read 'Viv (spectator)', 'A spectator' for pings and 'to N spectators' for reveals (small edit in ui/describe.ts) — reason: spectators are not in the shared state, so the log has no seat name for them
- [M3-P5] A failed or slow join offers 'Watch this table as a spectator', a pasted /watch link on the start page opens the watch route (small edit in StartPage.svelte), and the spectator page offers 'Take a chair instead' — reason: the choice to watch must be made before the session starts
- [M3-P5] Updated other packages' tests for the new `by` rule (tests/unit/net/sequencer.test.ts, tests/sim/net/protocol.test.ts) and replaced the stub-throws assertions in tests/unit/core/m3-contracts.test.ts — reason: the rule changed by design and the stubs are now implemented
- [M3-P5] The two script-based sim suites in tests/sim/roles have a 120 s per-test timeout (no retries) — reason: they run the whole M1-P8 script first, and real time grew past vitest's 30 s default under a load average of about 20
- [M3-P5] The Sequencer contract is unchanged. The sequencer runs spectatorActionProblem (type, seat, tab). The engine runs roleProblem and spectatorNameProblem next to senderPeerCheck before sequencing. Reason: the by and name checks need the sender's identity, which only the engine has, and contract files belong to M3-C.
- [M3-P5] A spectator's chat name must equal spectatorChatName(handshake nickname). Reason: the log label then always matches the name players see in the spectator list, so a spectator cannot post under another name.
- [M3-P5] The owner-side spectatorRevealSender in net/roles.ts, mounted by one line in session/net.ts, sends on an own sequenced revealTo and keeps the reveal owed while the card stays hidden, the owner's and in the same zone. It re-sends when that spectator returns, at once and after 1.5, 5 and 15 s. Reason: spectators keep nothing across a reload, and their engine drops prv messages from peers it has not verified yet.
- [M3-P5] spectatorRevealsOver resolves the sender's chair by peerId when the revealTo is applied, if the reveal arrived before the page had caught up. Reason: a reloaded page's replica does not yet know the chairs.
- [M3-P5] The spectator session leaves the room once it sees that its own key pair holds a chair, and the watch view offers 'Take your chair'. Reason: the engine keeps one connection per identity (liveTwin), and DESIGN Resume says 'reopening the link rejoins the same seat by key pair'.
- [M3-P5] With no sequencer reachable, a spectator page refuses non-chat/ping dispatches locally. Refused seat-only promises are handled rejections. Reason: 'cannot move cards' should hold on the spectator's own page, and HUD fire-and-forget calls should not raise unhandled rejections.
- [integrator] Edits outside M3-P5 ownership that remain: session/net.ts has the spectatorRevealSender import and mount line plus a guard that throws in openNetSession for role 'spectator'. ui/describe.ts has the spectator log wording (actor label and 'to N spectators'). tests/sim/net/protocol.test.ts (M1-P1) has its spectator chatting as v:peer0, which the engine now requires. tests/unit/core/m3-contracts.test.ts (M3-C) no longer expects the roles.ts stubs to throw. net/engine.ts senderPeerCheck gained the spectator identity check. The earlier net/private.ts, net/types.ts and tests/unit/net/sequencer.test.ts edits are reverted.
- [M3-P3] Every connection gets its own clone of the microphone track in its own stream, added to that connection only (`addStream(stream, peer)`); the gate flips `enabled` on the clones, so push-to-talk, turn mic and open mic enable every connection and team talk only the teammate's. The microphone's own track is never sent; it feeds the level meter — reason: one shared track cannot be open on one connection and closed on another, and DESIGN wants opening the mic to be instant.
- [M3-P3] The microphone is acquired only by an explicit "Join voice" button in the talk indicator (the click DESIGN asks for, which also starts audio playback); nothing calls getUserMedia before it — reason: joining a table is a route, not a click, and an automatic request would prompt every player (and the macOS privacy dialog in WebKit tests) whether they want voice or not.
- [M3-P3] Talk rule details: mute closes every gate; the held team key (Shift+V) narrows an open turn mic or open mic to the teammate while held and never captions (a caption is public); without a teammate it opens nothing; dictation follows the key or the turn mic, not the open mic; the key held in a note field dictates into it and sends no key audio; audio waits for the microphone, dictation does not — reason: DESIGN's talk-mode table leaves these combinations open; each choice keeps words meant for a partner off the table.
- [M3-P3] The turn mic counts the own turn only in the 'match' stage (`isSeatActive` plus `stage === 'match'`) — reason: the lobby, draft and build stages carry a turn marker from the seq-0 table but have no turns; an open mic there would surprise the player.
- [M3-P3] Speaking rings: while the talk key is held the player counts as speaking; for the turn mic and open mic a Web Audio level meter (RMS above 0.02, held 400 ms) decides. Others' rings come from the `speaking` presence plus the incoming audio's level (which is how the teammate sees team talk, DECISIONS [M3-C]). The contract's `VoiceHost.presence` only announces, so the session passes the engine's presence `peers`/`subscribe` through the package-owned `VoiceInternals.presence` (`createVoiceWith`, voice/mic.ts) — reason: an always-lit ring for an open mic says nothing; a held key is a deliberate act and needs no meter (deterministic in tests).
- [M3-P3] Per-player volume (0..1, clamped) and mute are applied to an audio element per incoming stream and stored by identity peer id (`peerAudio`), so they survive reconnects; the device picker lists audio inputs only after the microphone was granted (before that browsers hide their names), stores `micDevice`, re-acquires with `deviceId: {exact}` and falls back to the default device when the stored one is gone; the picker reads the choice with `chosenDevice(voice)` (voice/mic.ts), not through the contract — reason: PLAN names a device picker but the contract has no getter for the choice, and a work package does not edit contract files. Proposed M3-C amendment (not made): `VoiceControls.device(): string | null` and `VoiceHost.presence: Pick<Presence, 'speaking' | 'peers' | 'subscribe'>`.
- [M3-P3] A pre-M3 `talkMode` other than 'ptt' is mapped onto `talk` once, while `talk` still holds its defaults, and `talkMode` is reset to 'ptt' — reason: DECISIONS [M3-C] asks for a one-time mapping and the settings store cannot tell a stored default from a missing key.
- [M3-P3] The session wiring is session/voice.ts (mounted in session/net.ts `openNetSession` and `leave`, options from main.ts): the voice host reads the session's current engine and transport, so a rejoin's new transport carries the audio. `KillableTransport` forwards `addStream`/`removeStream`/`onStream`, and `TrysteroTransport` gains `removeStream` (and swallows Trystero's per-peer renegotiation rejections) — reason: the M3-C seams name the methods; the wrappers did not forward them.
- [M3-P3] The talk indicator and voice panel sit in the HUD's top-left column under the toolbar; avatars get an optional `speaking` prop (outline in the seat colour); note inputs carry `data-note-field` so focus there reports `noteFocus`. Holding the talk key inside a note field reaches the controller only once M3-P4 wires dictation into note fields (the HUD drops every key in text fields) — reason: the toolbar row overlapped the turn strip at 1440 px; note dictation is M3-P4's.
- [M3-P3] Browser specs: `?harness=voice` (src/ui/voice/harness.ts) mounts the real HUD and the real controller on Chromium's fake microphone over a fake Two-Headed Giant session and a recording transport, and a two-page real-app spec asserts the tracks through the debug hook (BroadcastChannel carries no media). WebKit runs only the chat-leak spec, without joining voice — reason: Trystero cannot run without the network in tests, and WebKit would show the macOS microphone prompt.
- [M3-P3] Incoming audio whose `play()` the browser refuses (autoplay rules, Safari) is retried on the next pointer, key or touch gesture anywhere on the page, not only on "Join voice" — reason: a player with voice off or dictation only never clicks "Join voice" and must still hear the table.
- [M3-P3] Spectators hear talk to everyone (push-to-talk, turn mic, open mic) like the players; team talk never reaches them (only teammate seats are enabled) — reason: DESIGN does not say; talk to everyone is as public as table chat, which spectators read, and the mesh diagram carries audio to spectators.
- [M3-P3] Per-player volume uses `HTMLMediaElement.volume`, which iOS/iPadOS Safari ignores (read-only 1): there only per-player mute works; a GainNode route is left as a follow-up — reason: Chromium does not play a remote WebRTC stream through Web Audio unless it is also attached to a media element, so the change needs browser testing on tablets this package cannot run.
- [M3-P3] The talk mouse button can be the back or forward button only (`cleanTalk` drops 0–2): left selects, middle inspects and pans, right opens menus, and the scene reads pointer events before any mousedown handler could stop them — reason: DESIGN 'Input and interaction design' gives the middle button to inspect and 'Camera and navigation' to pan.
- [M3-P3] Device picks are last-one-wins: a microphone granted after a newer pick is released unused; a talk press re-reads `document.activeElement` instead of trusting only focusin/focusout; the panel's selects blur after a choice — reason: overlapping getUserMedia calls resolve in any order, a removed field may never fire focusout, and a focused select counts as a text field (and would take V).
- [M3-P3] Each connection gets its own copy of the mic track, sent to that connection only; the gate switches the copies on or off, so team talk reaches only the teammate's connection and opening the mic is instant
- [M3-P3] The mic is acquired only from an explicit 'Join voice' button in the talk indicator, so no page asks for the microphone on its own and WebKit tests never trigger the macOS prompt
- [M3-P3] Mute closes every gate. Shift+V narrows an open turn mic or open mic to the teammate while held and never makes captions. Without a teammate, Shift+V opens nothing. Dictation follows the key or the turn mic, not the open mic. The key in a note field dictates into it
- [M3-P3] The turn mic opens only in the 'match' stage; the lobby, draft and build have a turn marker but no turns
- [M3-P3] Speaking ring: lit while the talk key is held; for the turn mic and open mic a Web Audio level meter decides; the teammate's ring during team talk comes from the incoming audio
- [M3-P3] Per-player volume and mute are stored by identity peer id; the device picker lists microphones only after joining, re-acquires with the exact device id and falls back to the default when that device is gone
- [M3-P3] An old talkMode setting is converted to the new talk setting once and talkMode is reset to 'ptt'
- [M3-P3] session/voice.ts always uses the session's current engine and transport, so voice survives a rejoin; KillableTransport passes audio through; TrysteroTransport gains removeStream
- [M3-P3] The talk indicator sits in its own row under the toolbar, because the toolbar row overlapped the turn strip at 1440 px
- [M3-P3] Note inputs carry data-note-field; holding V in a note field does not dictate yet, because connecting that key is M3-P4's (dictation into note fields)
- [M3-P3] The browser tests use a ?harness=voice page (registered in main.ts) because tests cannot use Trystero without the network
- [M3-P3] Others' speaking presence reaches the controller through VoiceInternals.presence and the picker reads the choice with chosenDevice(), so the voice contract stays M3-C's. Reason: a work package does not edit contract files. Proposed amendment: VoiceControls.device() and VoiceHost.presence with peers/subscribe.
- [M3-P3] Refused incoming playback is retried on the next pointer, key or touch gesture anywhere on the page. Reason: a player with voice off or dictation only never clicks 'Join voice' but must still hear the table.
- [M3-P3] Spectators hear talk to everyone and never hear team talk. Reason: the design does not say; talk to everyone is as public as table chat, and the mesh diagram carries audio to spectators.
- [M3-P3] Per-player volume uses HTMLMediaElement.volume (ignored on iOS Safari, where only mute works); a GainNode route is a follow-up. Reason: Chromium needs remote WebRTC audio attached to a media element, so the change needs browser testing on tablets that this package cannot run.
- [M3-P3] The talk mouse button can be only the back or forward button (cleanTalk drops 0-2) — left selects, middle inspects and pans (DESIGN Input and interaction design; Camera and navigation), right opens menus, and the scene reads pointer events before a mousedown handler could stop them
- [M3-P3] Device picks are last-one-wins, and a mic granted after a newer pick is released unused — overlapping getUserMedia calls resolve in any order
- [M3-P3] A talk press re-reads document.activeElement instead of trusting only focusin/focusout — a removed field may never fire focusout
- [M3-P3] The panel's selects blur after a choice — a focused select counts as a text field and would take V
- [M3-P3] Without a teammate, the held team key still leaves an open turn-mic or open-mic audio gate as it is (as before), but captions stay off — a caption is public, and the existing rule keeps the gate's audio
- [M3-P2] In the Two-Headed Giant draft every team member opens one pack per round until the team has opened its `packs` (6 packs, two members: 3 rounds; an odd count leaves the second member without a pack in the last round); in Two-Headed Giant sealed member j opens the team's packs j, j + m, j + 2m … (`number` is the team pack index) — reason: DESIGN gives packs per team and "after 3 rounds", not who opens them; each pack still comes from its opener's own pack seed.
- [M3-P2] A Two-Headed Giant `pick` names the opaque ids it takes (`cards`), recorded in `DraftState.taken[team]` in pick order, and hands the rest of the pack straight on to the next team (no `packPass`, which the reducer refuses in this format) — reason: both teammates' browsers must add the same cards to the shared pool whoever picked, and a pack must not wait for one teammate's browser; opaque ids say nothing about the cards, and the receiving team learns which ids are gone from the cards it gets anyway.
- [M3-P2] Team packs and pools travel as `draftCards` (about `team:cards`, requests `team:need`), never as `pack`: every browser sends each seat only what that seat's team may know by the confirmed state (remaining cards of a pack its team holds, cards its team took, its team's sealed packs), and a browser lacking such cards asks every other seat after 2 s — reason: net/private.ts `pack` hand-overs are seat-to-seat visits (M2-P2's) and the team is the holder; any knowing seat can answer, so an away teammate never blocks the pool.
- [M3-P2] The pick timer runs for the Two-Headed Giant draft (core/drafting.ts `draftDeadlines` covers 'teamDraft'); its `draftTimeout` takes the first remaining ids in sorted order — reason: the lobby offers the timer for this mode (ui/draft/settings.ts `hasPickTimer`), and the sequencer knows neither a team's marks nor pack order; sorted opaque ids are effectively a random choice.
- [M3-P2] The team pool is stored under `DraftSecrets.held['team:pool']` (ids) and `SecretStore.pool` (refs), the draft order of picks or team pack order for sealed; each teammate builds a deck from the whole team pool (splitting the cards is left to the players) — reason: DESIGN "shared pool"; the key keeps M2-P7's stale-secrets check (which counts the per-seat pool keys) from discarding a team pool.
- [M3-P2] core/stage.ts lists the Two-Headed Giant drafts as playable and asks for at least two teams among the seated players; session/net.ts `freshNetTable` gives Two-Headed Giant chairs their team (TABLE_FORMAT_TEAMS) — reason: one-line enabling edits in M2-P7's and M1-P8's files; without teams at creation a '2hg' table had no team to sit in.
- [M3-P2] Team creation in the lobby is sitting down: every Two-Headed Giant chair belongs to a team, the chair map outlines the two team areas and labels each chair "Team 1"/"Team 2", and a free chair of the other team switches team — reason: DESIGN fixes teammates side by side on one long edge, so the chair already decides the team; no separate team picker is needed.
- [M3-P2] The teammate hand is sent whole (`teamHand`) after every change of the confirmed hand zone and again on every peer (re)connect, only to a teammate while `teamHandsVisible`, only cards the confirmed state has in the own hand; the receiver verifies each card against the deck commitment, keeps the last 8 copies in memory only (never IndexedDB) and shows the latest copy matching the teammate's public hand zone, else the closest one without cards that left the hand — reason: messages can overtake the log, and a teammate's hidden cards must not outlive the page.
- [M3-P2] Team chat lines are stamped like actions but go only to the teammate as `teamChat` and into `TeamControls.chatLog()`; net/engine.ts now passes `teamChat` to the pair-channel listeners (one case line) — reason: the M3-C contract added the message but the engine dropped it.
- [M3-P2] The HUD mounts ui/hand/TeamHands.svelte above the own fan when the session has a team view, and the reveal picker offers "My team (…)" and "Team …" targets expanded with `revealRecipients` — reason: the plan lists the team reveal target and the second fan; two mount points in Hud.svelte (M0-P4's).
- [M3-P2] The browser test of the teammate hand runs on a bundled harness page (tests/e2e/teams) that opens real networked sessions at a '2hg' table, not the app — reason: the spec asserts on the received messages and secrets of each page for the leak scan, which the app's debug hook does not expose; `formatSet` (M3-P1, now on m3) and `freshNetTable` give the same seats the same `teamIdOf` teams (tests/unit/teams/reducer.test.ts), so the harness table is the table the lobby makes.
- [M3-P2] The Two-Headed Giant pick timer is decided by the team's browsers: when the front pack's timer runs out (counted from the moment the browser applied its `sinceSeq`, as the HUD shows it), a teammate's driver sends an ordinary `pick` of its marked cards topped up with the first cards in pack order; a browser without marks waits 2 s longer and the second member 1 s longer, so a marking teammate's pick comes first. The sequencer's `draftTimeout` (first remaining ids in sorted order) comes only `TEAM_PICK_GRACE_MS` (6 s) after the timer, as the fallback for a team with no browser that holds the pack's cards. Supersedes the timeout line above — reason: DESIGN "on expiry the marked card, or else the first card, is taken", and only the team's browsers know the marks and the pack order; core/drafting.ts gains the constant and the grace (one more line in M2-P2's file).
- [M3-P2] Team chat lines live in memory only (`TeamView`, both pages): a reload or rejoin starts the team tab empty, unlike the table chat restored from the saved log; a team line longer than `MAX_TEAM_CHAT` (2000) is cut to it before it is stamped, so the sender's timeline shows what the teammate receives — reason: the table store (store/types.ts, M0-P5's contract) has no record for lines outside the shared log, and the chat input already stops at 500 characters.
- [M3-P2] The HUD merges `session.team.chatLog()` into the timeline (subscribed to `team.subscribe`), and a team line it sends is not kept as a pending line — reason: team lines never enter the shared log, so the receiving teammate's team tab was empty and the sender's line stayed pending for good (third edit in Hud.svelte, M0-P4's).
- [M3-P2] The team harness page (tests/e2e/teams) mounts the real HUD over a recording scene instead of the fan alone — reason: the team chat and the teammate fan are asserted where the app mounts them.
- [M3-P2] The lobby's set list of a Two-Headed Giant draft is per team pack, as in sealed: member j of a team of m opens team pack round · m + j (core/teams.ts `teamDraftPackIndex`) of that slot's set; `packOpen.number` stays the round — reason: DESIGN "set per pack … a different set per pack is allowed" and "6 packs per team", and the lobby (ui/draft/settings.ts `setsPerPack`) offers one slot per team pack.
- [M3-P2] A Two-Headed Giant team's drafting members are the team's seats that are seated or have opened a pack of this draft (core/teams.ts `draftMembers`), so a player who leaves the chair mid-draft keeps the place: the member count, opening duties and set slots do not change and the round waits for that chair, as a pass draft waits for a drafter — reason: counting only seated players let a round end early or stall; DraftState has no member list to freeze (contract file).
- [M3-P2] net/team.ts `teamChannelOver` returns a `SendingTeamChannel` whose `sendHand` returns the engine's `sendPrivate` result; session/team.ts remembers a hand as sent only when it went out, so the next sync after the teammate's peer is verified sends it — reason: the TeamChannel contract's `sendHand` returns void, and a peer can become verified without a peers event.
- [M3-P2] The team draft driver keeps the local apply time of every seq from the oldest front pack's `sinceSeq` on (not a rolling window of 256), so a browser's timed-out pick is never late — reason: the timer is counted from when this browser applied `sinceSeq`.
- [M3-P2] Each team member opens one pack per round until the team has opened its packs (6 packs, 2 members: 3 rounds); in team sealed member j opens team packs j, j+m, j+2m… (pack number = team pack index) — design gives packs per team, not who opens them; each pack still comes from its opener's own pack seed
- [M3-P2] A team pick names the card ids it takes (recorded in DraftState.taken[team]) and hands the rest straight to the next team; packPass is refused in this format — both teammates must add the same cards to the shared pool, and a pack must not wait for one teammate's browser; the ids say nothing about the cards, and the receiving team can tell which are gone anyway
- [M3-P2] Team packs and pools travel as draftCards ('team:cards', with 'team:need' requests), not as pack messages; each seat is sent only what its team may know in the agreed table state, and any seat that knows the cards can answer a request — the existing pack hand-over is seat to seat, but here the team holds the pack
- [M3-P2] The pick timer runs in team drafts; a timeout takes the first remaining ids in sorted order — the lobby already offers the timer for this mode, and the sequencer knows neither the team's marks nor pack order
- [M3-P2] The team pool is stored under DraftSecrets.held['team:pool'] and SecretStore.pool; each teammate builds a deck from the whole pool and splitting the cards is left to the players — the design says 'shared pool', and the separate key keeps M2-P7's stale-secrets check from discarding it
- [M3-P2] Two-line enabling edits: core/stage.ts makes the Two-Headed Giant modes playable (with at least two teams), and session/net.ts freshNetTable gives each Two-Headed Giant chair its team — without teams at creation a Two-Headed Giant table had no team to sit in
- [M3-P2] Team creation in the lobby is sitting down: every Two-Headed Giant chair belongs to a team, the chair map shows the team areas and labels, and moving to a free chair of the other team switches team — the design fixes teammates side by side, so the chair already decides the team
- [M3-P2] The teammate hand is sent whole after every change of the agreed hand zone and again on every reconnect, only to the teammate while team hands are on, only cards actually in the hand; the receiver checks each card, keeps the last 8 copies in memory only and shows the copy matching the teammate's hand zone — messages can overtake the action log, and a teammate's hidden cards must not outlive the page
- [M3-P2] Team chat is sent only to the teammate as teamChat and kept in TeamControls.chatLog(); net/engine.ts now delivers teamChat (one line) — the M3-C contract added the message but the engine dropped it
- [M3-P2] Hud.svelte mounts the teammate fan above the own fan and adds 'My team' and opposing-team reveal targets expanded with revealRecipients — two small mount points in M0-P4's file
- [M3-P2] The browser test runs on a bundled test page with four real networked sessions at a Two-Headed Giant table rather than the app — the app cannot start such a table until M3-P1's formatSet is implemented
- [M3-P2] When the 2HG pick timer runs out, the team's browsers pick the marked cards, then the first in pack order. A browser without marks waits +2 s and the second member +1 s. The sequencer's draftTimeout is a fallback after TEAM_PICK_GRACE_MS (6 s) — reason: DESIGN "on expiry the marked card, or else the first card, is taken", and only the team's browsers know the marks and the pack order.
- [M3-P2] Team chat stays in memory only, and lines longer than MAX_TEAM_CHAT are cut before stamping — reason: store/types.ts (a contract) has no record for lines outside the shared log, and the chat input already stops at 500 characters.
- [M3-P2] The HUD merges session.team.chatLog() into the timeline and keeps team lines out of pendingChat — reason: team lines never enter the shared log.
- [M3-P2] The tests/e2e/teams harness mounts the real HUD over a RecordingScene instead of TeamHands alone (Container.svelte removed) — reason: the teammate fan and team chat are asserted where the app mounts them.
- [M3-P2] The lobby's set list of a Two-Headed Giant draft is per team pack, as in sealed: member j of m opens team pack round*m + j of that slot's set, and packOpen.number stays the round. Reason: DESIGN says 'set per pack ... a different set per pack is allowed' and '6 packs per team', and the lobby offers one slot per team pack.
- [M3-P2] A team's drafting members are its seats that are seated or have opened a pack in this draft (core/teams.ts draftMembers), so a player who leaves mid-draft keeps their place and the round waits for their chair. Reason: DraftState, a contract file, has no member list to freeze, and counting only seated players let a round end early or stall.
- [M3-P2] teamChannelOver returns SendingTeamChannel, whose sendHand returns a boolean, and session/team.ts remembers a hand only once it was sent. Reason: the TeamChannel contract's sendHand returns void, and a peer can become verified without a peers event.
- [M3-P2] The team draft driver keeps apply times from the oldest front pack's sinceSeq onward instead of a rolling window of 256. Reason: the pick timer counts from when this browser applied sinceSeq.
- [M3-P2] The e2e harness page stays because the spec reads each page's received messages and secrets for the leak scan, which the app's debug hook does not expose. formatSet and freshNetTable give the same seats the same teams (unit test).
- [M3-G] The gate reads "each layout from seat 1 and seat 3" as the user-facing seat numbers (the seat keys 1–4), i.e. the pages of seats s0 and s2, and covers all three M3 layouts: the Two-Headed Giant game, the three-player opening and an added four-player opening. Reason: seat keys are 1-based in the design, and s0/s2 sit on opposite teams in Two-Headed Giant.
- [M3-G] The spectator watches the Two-Headed Giant table from its lobby onward, not the free-for-all. Reason: that game carries the most hidden information (team hands, a team reveal), so the spectator leak scan covers the most.
- [M3-G] The host turns the pass timer off in the lobby for the gate's tables, and every confirmation is answered with an explicit key. Reason: with four pages a 5 s timer can time a slow page out between steps; the gate asserts on answers, not timeouts.
- [M3-G] On WebKit the gate replaces `navigator.mediaDevices.getUserMedia` with a silent Web Audio stream (init script); Chromium keeps its fake device. Reason: WebKit has no fake media device and the real call would raise the macOS microphone prompt (pw-guard exit 3); the "Join voice" click and everything after it stay unchanged.
- [M3-G] The voice checks ignore the spectator's connection for push-to-talk and the turn mic, and require team talk to open nothing but the teammate's. Reason: the app adds an outgoing track on the spectator's connection too, and the design is silent on whether spectators hear the table; the gate reports it for the owner.
- [M3-G] `pnpm e2e:gate` runs M3 and later gates with one worker (SPELLPLANE_PW_WORKERS=1 unless set). Reason: one gate test drives five pages.
- [M3-G] Start-page specs wait for the identity to load before typing a nickname (`startPageLoaded` in tests/e2e/lobby/helpers.ts). Reason: StartPage.svelte overwrites the field with the stored nickname when the identity arrives; on slow CI WebKit that emptied the typed nickname and left "Join" disabled (CI red on m3). The product race is reported as a finding.
- [M3-P4] A caption's card context is the speaker's last own action, read back from the log: bookkeeping, chat, captions, answers to a round and undone actions are passed over; a card or stack action gives the anchor and `about` (a spell is its card, also once resolved; an ability or trigger its stack item; a line or ping its source or target); any other action (turn pass, life, die roll, shuffle) means no card context, so the caption sits at the avatar — reason: DESIGN "the card or stack item the speaker last acted on"; a caption about a card touched turns ago would point at the wrong thing
- [M3-P4] A hovered card on the table wins over the last-touched one as the anchor, while `about` stays the last action (DESIGN's example: cast Shock, hover the Elf, "two damage to your Elf" is filed on the cast); anchor and `about` are fixed when the utterance starts, so the bubble does not jump — reason: DESIGN "last acted on or is hovering" names both without an order
- [M3-P4] A caption's anchor is only ever a card in a public zone, a stack item or a seat: a hovered hand card or a last-touched card now in a hand or library is never named on presence or in the `caption` action (a shared-leak scenario covers it, tests/sim/session/captions-leak.test.ts) — reason: CLAUDE.md hidden information; which hand card a player is pointing at is a tell
- [M3-P4] Other players' interim captions come from their presence; the final `caption` action replaces the interim under the same caption id (one bubble), and the speaker clears its presence caption 2 s after the final so peers never flicker before the action lands; an interim that stops changing for 10 s without a final is dropped; after the talk key is released an engine has 3 s to deliver the final, else the words already shown become the final — reason: presence is unreliable and unordered against the sequenced log
- [M3-P4] The final bubble lingers CAPTION_LINGER_MS (4 s) from when each page receives it and then fades over 400 ms in the scene (a cut with reduced motion); the scene owns the fade, so SceneCaption.opacity stays 1 — reason: the fade is presentation, the controller only says what is shown
- [M3-P4] Caption bubbles are DOM over the canvas (scene/captions.ts), at most 280 px wide; a caption longer than three lines shows its last three (it grows at the end); fill is the speaker's seat colour with black or white text by WCAG contrast, a spectator's is the theme's muted colour — reason: DESIGN "upright for every viewer", Screen layer limits
- [M3-P4] Without voice controls (M3-P3's `session.voice` absent) the session opens dictation from the stored talk mode where no key is needed: dictation with the open gate, or with the turn gate during the own turn; the talk key itself is M3-P3's. The e2e tests store that talk mode (dictation, open gate, no voice) in the profile's settings before joining, which M3-P3's talk rule should also read as dictation — reason: the package must be testable on its own, and the stored talk mode is the player's real setting
- [M3-P4] Notes by voice: while the HUD's note field has focus the transcript goes into the field (interim words previewed, the final appended after the text already typed), and the player saves it with Enter as usual; the controller's `cardNote`/`stackNote` targets append to the existing note with a `note`/`stackNote` action (capped at 80 characters, the field's limit) — reason: DESIGN "dictates into that field"; writing the note behind an open field would be overwritten when it is saved
- [M3-P4] The stored default language 'en' stands for "the browser's language" (navigator.language); the settings store full tags with a region (en-US, de-DE, …) — reason: DESIGN "defaulting to the browser locale", and DEFAULT_SETTINGS.speechLang cannot tell unset from chosen
- [M3-P4] The on-device engine is Whisper base (multilingual, 74 M parameters, `onnx-community/whisper-base`) through transformers.js 3.8.1 loaded from the jsDelivr CDN by a dynamic import inside the lazily imported `voice/speech/local.ts`, not as an npm dependency; WebGPU runs an fp32 encoder with a 4-bit decoder, WASM 8-bit weights (about 80 MB on first use, cached by the browser); interim text re-transcribes the utterance so far once a second — reason: PLAN "loaded on demand"; bundling would put the ONNX runtime into the static site and need a lockfile change for a feature most players never choose. Accuracy: Whisper base reports about 5 % WER on LibriSpeech clean (OpenAI's paper), against tiny's 7.5 %; to be confirmed at the M3 gate
- [M3-P4] Choosing the on-device model in the settings asks for a confirmation that states its size before anything is fetched; a failed download leaves the engine as it was, and only a successful one is stored — reason: PLAN "not downloaded unless chosen"; a download of tens of MB should not start on a stray click
- [M3-P4] The engine factories of voice/speech/index.ts carry an extra `available()` (typed there as `ProbedSpeechEngineFactory`; the contract's `SpeechEngineFactory` is unchanged) and the caption controller asks it when present, else creates the engine to ask it — reason: listing the local model must not import its module, and contract files are M3-C's (a contract addition can be requested from the integrator)
- [M3-P4] The log line of a caption stays the speaker's chat line ("Anna: two damage to your Elf"); its `about` links it to the action in the data. Rendering it as "Anna casts Shock: '…'" needs a change in ui/describe.ts, which is not M3-P4's — reason: ownership; reported to the integrator
- [M3-P4] The speech settings open from a Dictation button on a row of its own below the HUD toolbar; the shared toolbar's layout is unchanged — reason: the HUD had no settings panel, and the toolbar's layout is not M3-P4's
- [M3-P4] After a peer's final caption, an interim of that peer whose words equal or begin the final's (case and punctuation ignored) is a late one of the same utterance and not shown, until the peer's presence caption was cleared once — reason: presence is unordered against the log, and a new utterance must still show before the speaker's clear arrives
- [M3-P4] Dictation follows `session.voice` when the session has it, else the stored talk mode (re-read when the turn passes); voice controls mounted after the captions are adopted on the next microtask or state change, but the integrator should mount voice before captions in openNetSession — reason: both packages add their mount line there
- [M3-P4] The on-device engine records through its own getUserMedia stream (disabled between utterances, released when the engine is switched away or the table closes) rather than M3-P3's joined microphone — reason: CaptionHost offers no stream; reusing the joined one needs a seam the integrator can add after M3-P3 lands
- [M3-P4] A log that shrinks (a snapshot resync) is taken as history: its captions are not shown again as bubbles — reason: old captions popping up at once would mislead
- [M3-P4] The on-device engine cuts listening into segments, one final transcript each: a segment ends after 700 ms below an RMS of 0.01 following speech, at 25 s without a pause, or when listening stops; silence before any speech is dropped, and interim passes transcribe only the current segment; trailing silence at stop is not transcribed — reason: with the open or turn gate the engine is never stopped between phrases, and a whole turn as one utterance would anchor to a stale card, reach the log only at the end and re-transcribe unbounded audio (review round 2)
- [M3-P4] An own utterance's anchor is checked again before every interim presence message and the final `caption` action; a card that left the public zones is no longer named (the utterance then sits at the avatar) — reason: the hidden-information rule of the anchor decision above
- [M3-P4] A final longer than 500 characters goes to the log as several `caption` actions cut at word boundaries (same anchor and `about`), and the interim presence carries the last 500 characters — reason: the log keeps the whole text and the bubble keeps growing at its end
- [M3-P4] Other players' captions are found in the log by comparing entries with the log as last seen (a rewritten tail is read again) and deduplicated by cid; a final whose words do not begin with the speaker's current presence interim gets a bubble of its own while that interim keeps its bubble — reason: a sequencer handover re-sequences the tail at unchanged length, and Web Speech's next phrase can reach presence before the previous phrase's action
- [M3-P4] Web Speech restarts after an error (other than 'no-speech') wait 0.5 s, doubling each time, and listening ends after 5 failed sessions in a row; a result resets the count — reason: an unreachable speech service must not loop start, error, end at full speed
- [M3-P4] Dictation that opens without a key (open gate, turn gate) waits for the player's first click or key press on the page (sticky user activation) without voice controls, and for `VoiceControls.joined` with them; the talk key opens it at once — reason: DESIGN Live voice ties microphone acquisition to the join click; a reload must not raise a microphone prompt by itself
- [M3-P4] The debug hook's `speech.engine()` reports the caption controller's engine in use (null before any engine was created); a local engine that failed to load is released and the settings' choice shows the engine in use again — reason: review round 2
- [M3-P4] transformers.js stays a pinned jsDelivr import rather than an npm dependency for now: @huggingface/transformers 3.x pulls onnxruntime-node and sharp (native binaries with install scripts) into every install and CI run, and still fetches the ONNX runtime's WASM from jsDelivr by default; the origin exposure of third-party code (no SRI on dynamic import, no offline precache) is left to the M3 gate — reason: PLAN "may add", not "must"; the trade-off is the gate's call
- [M3-P4] Each press of the talk key is its own listening run; a run outlives its stop by FINAL_GRACE_MS (3 s), and a final the engine still owes it reaches that run's utterance even after the next press. A final that only arrives after the release (no interim words) is placed at the anchor and note field current at the release; Web Speech leaves a stopped session to deliver its final instead of aborting it on the next start (aborting it only where the browser refuses a second recognition) — reason: review round 3, quick successive presses lost the on-device model's finals
- [M3-P4] Caption bubbles lead with the speaker's seat glyph (the avatar's shape) in the text colour, and carry the speaker's name for screen readers only; a spectator's bubble has no glyph (it has no seat) — reason: DESIGN Accessibility, nothing depends on hue alone; a visible name would cost one of the three caption lines
- [M3-P4] Where the chosen engine does not run in the browser (Web Speech in Firefox), the speech settings say dictation is unavailable and name an engine that runs, rather than stating the vendor; the controller does not switch to the on-device model by itself — reason: DESIGN Engine, the local model is downloaded only once the player chooses it
- [M3-P4] The microphone capture tries a 16 kHz AudioContext and falls back to the device's rate with a box-filter downsampler to 16 kHz when the browser refuses to connect the microphone to it (Firefox) — reason: DESIGN Engine, the local model "works in every browser"; to be confirmed on Firefox at the M3 gate
- [M3-P4] Note dictation rebases on the field's current text when the player typed since dictation last wrote (minus the interim words still in it), so typed characters are kept — reason: review round 3
- [M3-G] M3-P4 is merged into m3 at the gate fix round (conflicts resolved: voice mounts before captions; the HUD keeps M3-P3's talk wiring and adds the Dictation button to the talk-indicator row). Its open round-3 finding (a short phrase lost on a quick re-press with the on-device engine) is covered by the reviewer's reproduction as a unit test, which passes; no further review round ran — reason: the gate's caption step cannot pass without M3-P4.
- [M3-G] Dictation follows M3-P3's talk rule once the session has voice controls: the held talk key or the turn mic; the open gate sends audio but never captions. The M3-P4 browser and leak tests hold the talk key instead of storing an open gate — reason: DESIGN "Chat and voice", Talk modes table ("Dictation: while the talk key is held, or for the whole own turn when combined with Turn mic").
- [M3-G] A spectator's page shows the players' captions (interim from presence, final from the log) through a caption controller without a talk source or engine (session/captions.ts `watchCaptions`) — reason: PLAN M3-G, captions "on every page"; a spectator sees the public table and never dictates.
- [M3-G] A spectator's avatars show life and non-zero counters without −/+ controls, and "Take a chair" shows only while the format has a chair nobody holds — reason: DESIGN "Players, seats and formats", Spectators ("cannot move cards"); gate finding.
- [M3-G] With three or more other players the left avatar column uses compact avatars (smaller life figure, zero counters hidden, "+ counter" as a small + in the header) — reason: at 1440×900 four full panels overflowed the column into the own avatar (gate finding).
- [M3-G] At a Two-Headed Giant table the table fit also keeps the teammate's fan clear (bottom inset + 12 px gap + 14 % of the viewport height + 22 px label) — reason: the teammate's fan hid the own avatar spots and the lines aimed at them (gate finding; DESIGN: "a second, smaller fan above the player's own").
- [M3-G] Pile labels of a seat the viewer sees sideways turn a quarter and read away from the pile toward that seat's battlefield, like the own seat's labels — reason: upright labels of a row of piles ran into each other (gate finding, four-player side seats).
- [M3-G] Life lines read "Anna: 20 → 14" for the actor's own life (actor and text joined by a colon), "Cleo: team 30 → 28" for the own team's, and "Anna changes life of Ben: 20 → 18" otherwise; line kinds take "an" before a vowel ("draws an attack line") — reason: gate finding, "Cleo Cleo & Dan: 30 → 28" and "draws a attack line".
- [M3-G] A chair taken again by its holder logs "changes their seat colour" or "changes their name from …" (nothing when nothing changed or when the old name was the "Player" placeholder), and a chair move logs "moves to another chair" without a "leaves the table" line — reason: the lobby re-takes the chair for colour and name changes, which logged "takes a seat" twice per player (gate finding).
- [M3-G] The start page keeps a nickname typed before the identity finished loading — reason: a fresh profile's empty stored nickname used to wipe it (gate finding, CI WebKit).
- [M3-G] With a teammate's fan shown, the HUD's above-hand stack (confirmation prompt, toast, untap offer) sits above that fan and its label (`teamFanTop` in ui/hand/team.ts + 8 px), at z-index 20 over the teammate's fan (19) and under a hovered own hand card — reason: the fan covered OK and Hold at every Two-Headed Giant table (gate finding); DESIGN "Screen layer" puts the prompt above the hand, centred, and moving it keeps both the prompt and the teammate's cards readable, where raising it alone would hide the teammate's cards.
- [M3-G] A caption's log line is told with the action it is attached to (`about`), as in the gate's "Anna draws an attack line: 'swinging at both of you'", and stays a chat line for the filters; with no `about`, an action outside the loaded log or one by another seat it reads "Anna: …" — reason: DESIGN "Chat and voice", Record ("Anna casts Shock: 'two damage to your Elf'"); gate finding.
- [M3-G] The voice settings panel drops down over the avatar column (absolute below the voice row, z-index 30) instead of taking room in it — reason: opening it pushed the other avatars into the own avatar (gate finding); it now behaves like the Dictation settings panel, which already overlays.
- [M3-G] `SpeechEngine` gains an optional `settled(on)` (additive contract change): it resolves once a stopped listener is owed no transcript. The on-device engine counts the transcriptions queued per listener; the caption run then stays open until that count is zero, capped at FINAL_SAFETY_MS (60 s). Engines without it (Web Speech, the fake engine) keep the 3 s FINAL_GRACE_MS measured from the release — reason: the on-device model transcribes phrases one after another after the release, so a queued or long phrase finished after 3 s and its final was dropped or a stale interim was logged (review finding); Web Speech delivers its final right after `stop`.
- [M3-G] A caption run has a fixed route, captions or one note field, taken from the talk state ('field') or a focused note field (`dictateInto`), whichever says field; a change of either while listening stops the run and starts a new one. A note run whose field is gone drops its words, and the caption context is always taken at the stop — reason: words spoken into a note field became a public caption and log entry when the field closed, blurred or was removed under the held key (review finding); dropping is the privacy-safe side.
- [M3-G] Presence always shows the newest own caption utterance, and only a caption utterance cancels the pending presence clear — reason: a late interim of an older press overwrote the newer press's words on peers, and a note dictation kept a finished caption's interim on presence (review findings).
- [M3-G] The HUD's note-field dictation target is bound to the note open at focus and writes only while that note is still open — reason: a late final could land in another card's note (review finding).
- [M3-G] The on-device model's download confirmation names its origins next to its size: jsDelivr (transformers.js and its ONNX runtime) and Hugging Face (the Whisper weights), kept as `LOCAL_MODEL_SOURCES` in voice/speech/index.ts — reason: DESIGN asks for a plain statement of what is downloaded; the settings must not load local.ts for it.
- [M3-G] When the HUD closes the note editor it re-reads what has focus and tells the voice controller (`focus(focusKind(document.activeElement))`) — reason: WebKit fires no focusout for a removed focused field, so the talk state stayed on 'field' with no target; the old caption code then captioned those words (the e2e step relied on that leak), and with note runs dropping words it would lose them instead.
- [M3-G] Gate green after the main session had M3-P4 reviewed independently (merged unreviewed by the gate fix round) and fixed the two reproduced blocking defects, plus the teammate fan hiding the confirmation prompt found in the screenshot review

## M4 Themes and 3D

- [M4-C] Theme v2 is additive: every v1 field keeps its shape; required new fields are `label`, `lighting` (lit flag, light rig, tone mapping, exposure, background, fog), `shadows` (default on/off, shadow map, contact shadows), `post` (bloom, vignette) and the card dressing's `sleeveMaterial`, `edge`, `faceShade`, `held` and `pending`; optional ones are `tokens.hud`, `SeatColour.ink`/`glow`, `HighlightStyle.glow`, `LineStyle.animation`/`ornament`, `SoundSet.ambience`/`onByDefault`, the `QualityLevel` extras (`shadowMapSize`, `anisotropy`, `msaa`), `CameraDefaults.birdseyeTiltDeg`/`ego`, and `SceneContext.post`/`materials`/`viewerAngle` — reason: Paper and the M0–M3 scene code compile unchanged in shape, while each lit theme must state its light, shadow and post settings explicitly.
- [M4-C] Motion constants stay `MotionConstants` in core/geometry.ts (springs and durations already cover cards, flips, taps and camera); a theme's held-card lift and tilt live in the card dressing (`held`) — reason: the design's motion list is already complete there; lift is dressing (Stonetable lifts and tilts, Paper does not tilt).
- [M4-C] Lights in a theme are given in the viewer's frame (x right, y away from the viewer, z up, mm, origin at the table centre) and rotated by `SceneContext.viewerAngle` — reason: a lamp or torch must stand behind the viewer's own edge whichever seat they hold; two players may use different themes and seats.
- [M4-C] The card face's shading is capped at `CARD_FACE_MAX_SHADE` = 0.03 (`CardDressing.faceShade`, 0 in Paper); `MaterialLibrary.cardFace` makes the near-unlit material — reason: PLAN M4-P2 allows less than 3% average colour difference from the source texture; the rule lives in one constant.
- [M4-C] The twelve functional signals (themes/signals.ts) are PLAN M4-P6's list with "each seat colour" as one per-seat signal; they are also the "twelve gate states" of M4-P3..P5 and the M4-G matrix — reason: PLAN names twelve gate states without listing them, and the signal list has exactly twelve entries.
- [M4-C] Signal conformance metric: a controlled screenshot pair passes when at least 2% of the probe region's pixels change by more than 24/255 in some channel (hover 1%, foil 16/255 with reduced motion for a static sheen); the region is the union of the neutral and signalled regions, found through the debug hook (`cards`, `drawnLines`, `stack`) or the avatar's `data-speaking`/`data-away` — reason: PLAN asks for "a measured contrast threshold" without a number; hover's hairline outline covers about 3% of a card's region.
- [M4-C] Seat colours must reach 3:1 contrast against the theme's table surface (WCAG 1.4.11) and be at least 0.15 apart pairwise (sRGB Euclidean, 0..1 channels; Paper's closest pair is 0.24) (`SEAT_CONTRAST`); pattern and glyph per seat index stay the same in every theme — reason: DESIGN "tuned per theme for contrast" and "nothing depends on hue alone".
- [M4-C] Ego rig limits beyond the spec: lean ±400 mm, dolly up to 700 mm forward from the seat and never backward, camera never below 120 mm above the table (`DEFAULT_EGO_RIG`); a theme may override them through `camera.ego` — reason: DESIGN gives "within limits" without numbers; M4-P1 may tune them and report.
- [M4-C] The debug hook gains three parts instead of one: `render` (stats from renderer.info: draw calls, triangles, textures, geometries, programs, texture bytes, frame CPU ms, post passes, shadows, quality, theme; and quality get/set) and `rig` (rig, setRig with anchor, blend, follow toggles, pose), both registered by the table scene now with stub bodies, and `theme` (current, set, loaded), registered by M4-P6's switcher — reason: registerDebug merges top-level keys, so parts owned by different packages must be separate keys; switching the theme needs the HUD (CSS custom properties, stored setting), not only the scene.
- [M4-C] `TableScene` gains required `setQuality`, `quality` and `renderStats`, and its `camera` becomes `TableCameraControls` (adds `pitchBy`, `follow`, `setFollow`, `blend`, `pose`; `setRig` takes optional `{animate, anchor}`); the table scene and the HUD's RecordingScene implement them as stubs that store the value — reason: consumers (M4-P6, the HUD) call them without optional chaining; the draft and builder surfaces keep the plain `CameraControls`.
- [M4-C] Dusk, Wyrm and Stonetable are stub themes until their packages land: Paper's look under their own id, label and default camera (Dusk bird's-eye tilted 10°, Wyrm and Stonetable ego), with a `stub` field naming the package; `loadTheme` imports each with its own dynamic import — reason: M4-P6 builds the picker, lazy loading and hot-swap in parallel with M4-P2 and before the themes exist.
- [M4-C] New setting `sound: 'theme' | 'on' | 'off'` (default 'theme', following `SoundSet.onByDefault`; Paper has no set) — reason: DESIGN makes sound optional and off by default in Paper, so the viewer needs a choice that works across themes.
- [M4-C] HUD tokens: `tokensToCssVars` now also emits `--sp-hud-scheme`, `--sp-hud-panel`, `--sp-hud-panel-border`, `--sp-hud-shadow`, `--sp-hud-blur`, `--sp-hud-overlay` and per seat `--sp-seat-<i>-ink`/`-glow`, with Paper's light values when a theme has no `hud` — reason: dark themes need panel and colour-scheme tokens; existing variables are unchanged, so the HUD looks the same until M4-P6 uses them.
- [M4-C] docs/ASSETS.md is created as a skeleton (columns, shared font row, one section per theme) — reason: three theme packages and M4-P6 all add to it; one agreed format avoids merge conflicts.
- [M4-P2] The table renders two scenes: the world (theme table, lights, card boxes, contact shadows, zone markings, ghost outlines) goes through the post chain and tone mapping; the overlay (card faces, text over the cards, displacement markers, lines, pings) is drawn after it straight to the canvas without tone mapping — reason: DESIGN "Card faces are never restyled": no bloom, vignette, tone mapping or theme pass (Wyrm's shimmer) can touch a printed face, and lines keep their exact seat colours; a theme object above the table (particles) therefore never covers a card face
- [M4-P2] With bloom on, lines and ping rings are drawn a second time into the image that blooms (the `glow` pass), so they glow on the table while staying crisp on top — reason: Dusk's "soft bloom on lines" and Wyrm's "fiery line glow" need the lines in the bloomed image, which the overlay keeps out
- [M4-P2] The pipeline (scene/theme-runtime.ts) builds the light rig from `lighting.lights` before the theme's build (scene/lights.ts), applies tone mapping, exposure, background and fog, and applies ShadowSettings (bias, normal bias, radius, the level's map size) to every light that asks for shadows, including lights a theme adds itself; a theme adds only extra lights — reason: one implementation of the LightSpec frame rotation and of the quality's shadow switch for all themes
- [M4-P2] A card face's shading is `1 − faceShade · (1 − n·l)` toward the theme's key light (the first shadow-casting placed light, else the first placed light), so a face is darkened by at most `faceShade` ≤ CARD_FACE_MAX_SHADE and never tinted; Paper's 0 leaves faces untouched — reason: themes/types.ts faceShade; the face may follow the lamp a little, never more than the 3% rule allows
- [M4-P2] Card boxes are the rounded card (63 × 88, r 3) plus a 0.8 mm sleeve margin when the dressing has a sleeve, extruded by `thickness`; caps take the sleeve material, sides the edge material (MaterialLibrary.cardSides), drawn after the table markings so a hairline never shows on a card's border — reason: DESIGN geometry table (thickness 0.6) and "sleeves receive light and shadow"; the margin is where a sleeve is visible around the printed face
- [M4-P2] Contact shadows are one instanced soft rounded-rectangle quad per card lying on the table (the bottom card of a pile) and per held card, opacity fading linearly to 0 at `contact.heightMm` and blur growing with height; no depth pass — reason: one draw call and no textures for "soft contact shadows"; `held.shadow` false keeps held cards from casting (shadow map and contact)
- [M4-P2] A held card's lift springs with the card spring (cut with reduced motion); its tilt dips the leading edge toward the drag velocity, scaled by speed up to `held.tiltDeg` at 400 mm/s, none at rest or with reduced motion — reason: DESIGN "4° tilt toward the drag direction"; a still card under the pointer is flat
- [M4-P2] Lit themes draw every frame unless motion is reduced (lamp flicker, particles, shimmer); Paper and unlit themes keep drawing on demand — reason: ThemeInstance has no "animating" signal; Paper stays cheap
- [M4-P2] The post chain's two half-float targets get at most 160 MB; MSAA samples are lowered (to none below two) until they fit, so a high-density or very large screen runs the chain without MSAA — reason: PLAN texture memory under 400 MB with 300 cards; MSAA 4 at 2880 × 1800 alone took over 400 MB, and the faces and lines over the chain keep the canvas's own antialiasing
- [M4-P2] RenderStats.textureBytes counts allocated GPU memory: the face texture arrays (all layers with mip-maps), the post targets and bloom chain, and shadow maps; `shadows` is true while shadow maps or contact shadows are on — reason: PLAN "texture memory under 400 MB" is about what the GPU holds, not the decoded-image cache alone
- [M4-P2] The 6 ms main-thread frame bound is measured on the debug hook (`frameMs`: the scene's CPU work per frame, layout to render submission) and reported as a test annotation, not asserted — reason: this run's rule forbids timing assertions in browser specs (CI renders in software); locally about 1 ms with 300 cards in Paper and the lit test theme
- [M4-P2] `pcfSoft` shadow maps render as PCF with the theme's `radius` — reason: three r187 removed PCFSoftShadowMap (it warns and falls back)
- [M4-P2] PostPipeline pass order: render, glow (with bloom), bloom (order 100), theme passes (default 500, ascending, ties in insertion order), vignette (900), output; `removePass` never disposes a theme's pass — reason: contract says "before the output pass in ascending order" without numbers
- [M4-P2] A MaterialSpec with `tileMm` repeats its maps every tileMm of UV space, so lit surfaces give UVs in millimetres; maps are shared by source and freed with their last material — reason: themes/types.ts tileMm ("keeps its scale on every table size")
- [M4-P2] The theme's `setStackFocus` gets the stack lane's centre while the stack holds items and null otherwise, again after every rebuild — reason: Wyrm's heat shimmer follows the stack
- [M4-P2] A theme switch to a different card back rebuilds the face textures (as the draft surface does) — reason: FaceTextures takes its back once; the old arrays are freed
- [M4-P2] The draft and builder surfaces use the same theme runtime without post-processing (lights, tone mapping and materials only) — reason: the pre-game surfaces draw a single scene with cards in it; the table is where the lit look matters
- [M4-P2] Theme rules checked over every theme in tests/unit/render/themes.test.ts: faceShade ≤ CARD_FACE_MAX_SHADE, thickness 0 or 0.6, held lift 12, tilt 4° exactly in lit themes and 0 in unlit ones, a quality ladder that drops shadows, then post, before particles and resolution (scene/quality.ts qualityLadderProblems) — reason: DESIGN geometry table and Renderer; M4-P3..P5 meet them as they land
- [M4-P2] The leak check takes its baseline after one warm-up visit to a lit theme — reason: three creates one DFG lookup texture per renderer the first time a physically based material draws, and the SDF text builds its glyph atlas after the first frame; both are one-time caches, a leak grows with switches (ten switches leave the count unchanged)
- [M4-P2] Test-only lit theme (themes/test-lit.ts) and render harness (`?harness=render`, scene/render-harness.ts) — reason: PLAN asks for a way to test the pipeline before M4-P3..P5; the theme is loaded only by the harness, never by the app; tests/unit/themes/m4-contracts.test.ts loses its three M4-P2 "stub throws" rows now that the stubs are implemented
- [M4-P6] The viewer's theme is a `ThemeChoice` (ui/settings/theme.ts) that App creates over `Settings.theme` and hands to the shells through the Svelte context (HUD, lobby, spectator view, the evening stage, draft and builder follow it); the latest pick wins, a chunk that fails to load leaves the current theme and says why, and the table route waits for the stored choice (at most 3 s) so the scene is built once in the stored theme — reason: DESIGN "chosen per viewer … switchable mid-game without reloading"; a context keeps the HudProps contract unchanged and harness pages keep their fixed theme.
- [M4-P6] `hudCssVars` (themes/index.ts): inside the HUD `--sp-surface` and `--sp-border` are the HUD panel tokens (`tokens.hud.panel`/`panelBorder`), the table's own colours stay available as `--sp-table-surface`/`--sp-table-border`, and `color-scheme` follows `hud.scheme`; App also sets them on `<html>`, whose background is `--sp-bg` — reason: about fifty HUD rules paint panels with `--sp-surface`; the DOM never draws the table, and in Paper both pairs are the same colours.
- [M4-P6] Paper's seat colour 1 is #c8404f (was #d1495b) and seats 2 and 4 (green, orange) carry the ink #111111 — reason: the theme checklist requires 4.5:1 for text on a seat-coloured chip (WCAG AA, "Paper meets AA throughout"); white on the old red, the green and the orange was 3.9–4.4:1.
- [M4-P6] Theme checklist (tests/unit/themes/conformance/themes.test.ts, every registered theme): SEAT_CONTRAST against the table surface and pairwise, seat colours 3:1 against the HUD panel (an rgba panel composited over `bg`), ink 4.5:1 on its seat colour, `fg` and `muted` 4.5:1 on the panel, the same pattern and glyph per seat index as Paper, every `styledBy` field present and boolean switches (foil sheen) on — reason: DESIGN "tuned per theme for contrast", "nothing depends on hue alone"; the seat colours mark HUD panels too (avatar stripe, log lines).
- [M4-P6] Signal conformance runs on the solo table with reduced motion and the bird's-eye rig forced (the gate's matrix covers both rigs), regions from the debug hook, and the signal's share of a pair is its changed fraction minus the fraction that changes between two neutral captures (noise floor: particles, flicker); the measured numbers are test annotations and the cropped pairs go to artifacts/shots/M4/signals/ — reason: PLAN "measured contrast threshold in a controlled screenshot pair" with M4-C's thresholds; a busy theme must not pass on noise.
- [M4-P6] Controlled pairs: attacking is the A move (110 mm forward, tapped; with one opponent the target is implicit, so no line is required, any drawn one is in the region); face-down is the far seat's card as this viewer sees it; foil is the same printing removed and created foil at the same pose; seat colour is a target line between two own cards measured in a band of ±max(3 mm, 4 px) of table surface between them, against the bare surface and against every other seat colour; pending confirmation is a stack item without a round, then an `ask` about it with a second named chair (the solo table runs no pass timer); away is the own avatar with `away` set; speaking runs on a two-player networked table with fake media and the talk key held, Chromium only — reason: each pair changes only the signal, through the same actions the HUD or a peer would send.
- [M4-P6] A theme whose `themes/<id>/index.ts` still calls `stubTheme(` is skipped in tests/e2e/signals.spec.ts with a marker naming its package — reason: the stubs are Paper under another id; the M4 gate runs the spec over all four.
- [M4-P6] Known gap, reported: the play table (scene/table-scene.ts `draw`) draws no foil sheen or badge (only the draft and builder surfaces do, M2-P4), so the foil pair changes nothing; its test is marked `test.fail` with that reason and turns red ("unexpectedly passed") once the table marks foils — reason: the fix belongs in the table scene's render loop (M4-P2's), not in M4-P6's files.
- [M4-P6] The HUD's theme button is a compact ◐ (accessible name "Theme") at the end of the table toolbar, opening the picker as a popover; the lobby offers the same picker under the seat colours — reason: the word "Theme" collided with the centred turn strip at 1440 px.
- [M4-P6] docs/ASSETS.md format and check (tests/unit/themes/conformance/assets.test.ts, in `pnpm check`): fixed six columns, sections Shared plus one per theme, every texture/model/sound/font file under src/ or public/ listed, every listed file existing, sources https or `procedural` (procedural rows name the generating module), theme rows CC0 or procedural — reason: PLAN M4-P3..P5 "every asset is listed … with a CC0 source or is procedural".
- [M4-P6] ui/flow/Stage.svelte changed in its theme lines only (it is the HUD shell of a networked table; the ownership note names the other shells) — reason: without it a networked table stayed in Paper.
- [M4-P6] Text the HUD draws on a seat colour reads the theme's seat ink: a component that sets `--seat` also sets `--seat-ink` from `--sp-seat-<i>-ink` (Avatar's "asks" tag, the attribution flash name, the lobby swatch glyph; a flash by a seat without colour uses `--sp-bg` on `--sp-fg`), and tests/unit/themes/conformance/seat-ink.test.ts fails any HUD rule that paints `var(--seat)` behind text set in another colour; the browser check of the drawn colours is in signals.spec.ts — reason: review round 1, white (`--sp-surface`) on Paper's green and orange was 4.07:1 and 3.90:1, and in dark themes `--sp-surface` is the panel colour.
- [M4-P6] Only the routes that build a table scene (solo, room) wait for the stored theme, with a "Loading…" status that appears after 400 ms; the start page and the connection check render at once — reason: review round 1, a slow settings read left every route blank for up to 3 s.
- [M4-P6] A theme chunk that fails to load says "… could not be loaded (reason). <current> stays; reload the page to try again." — reason: browsers keep a failed dynamic import per URL, so picking again on the same page fails the same way; cache-busting a hashed Vite chunk would need a second import path for each theme.
- [M4-P6] Paper seat colours 2 (green) and 4 (orange) darkened to #25834d and #a96000 so all eight reach 4.5:1 on the white table and panel, with white ink on every chip — reason: the HUD also sets text in a seat colour on a panel (ConfirmPrompt holder and waiting names, 12 px bold) and "Paper meets WCAG AA contrast throughout"; dark themes keep the 3:1 panel floor (AA is a Paper requirement; M5-P4 owns the full audit).
- [M4-P6] The foil pair is no longer `test.fail`: it is measured as evidence and asserts only the known gap (signal below the threshold, message naming the missing table foil marker), so any other failure stays red and closing the gap turns it red until the threshold is asserted — reason: a whole-test expected failure hid setup errors; the table foil sheen is owed by M4-P2 (scene/table-scene.ts).
- [M4-P6] The pending-confirmation neutral is the stack item after a finished round about it (asked, then the second seat answered OK), replacing "a stack item without a round" — reason: themes/signals.ts defines the neutral as "a stack item after every player passed"; the pair now also shows a finished round is styled apart from a waiting one.
- [M4-P6] The HUD theme popover closes on Esc (as the topmost transient overlay) and on a press outside it — reason: DESIGN allows inline, dismissible prompts; nothing about the picker is modal. The picker stays on the table HUD and the lobby only; the draft and builder stages follow the theme but carry no picker (their toolbars belong to M2 packages).
- [M4-P4] Wyrm is entirely procedural: the slab's maps are computed in TypeScript (overlapping round scales in rows half a scale apart, 8 × 16 scales per 288 mm tile, 768 px, cached per page), embers and shimmer are shaders, the card back is a canvas drawing; no file is bundled — reason: DESIGN Assets ("ornament stays geometric … generated procedurally"; "embers … glow … are shaders"); nothing to license or fetch
- [M4-P4] The slab is a MeshStandardMaterial built by the theme (with an emissive map and a few injected shader lines for the travelling heat wave), not a MaterialSpec through SceneContext.materials — reason: MaterialSpec has no emissive map, and the seams glow from one; the theme disposes it with its textures
- [M4-P4] Wyrm quality: high has shadow maps, contact shadows, bloom, vignette, heat shimmer and embers; medium drops shadows and the shimmer (a full-screen pass) but keeps bloom, vignette and embers; low drops post-processing and the embers (pixel ratio 1) — reason: DESIGN "drops shadows and post-processing first"; task: particles and shimmer drop at lower levels; the ladder passes qualityLadderProblems
- [M4-P4] The heat shimmer is a post pass (`wyrm-shimmer`, order 500) wavering an ellipse around the projected stack lane (radius: the lane's half diagonal + 20 mm, so the lane fits in either orientation) while the stack holds items, fading in and out over 400 ms; it is in the chain only while visible — reason: DESIGN "heat shimmer on the stack"; card faces are drawn after the post chain (scene/post.ts), so the shimmer never distorts or recolours a face, and an empty stack costs nothing
- [M4-P4] With reduced motion Wyrm has no shimmer, the embers stand still at a fixed moment and the seams' heat wave stops — reason: DESIGN "With reduced motion, all of these are cuts"; a still shimmer is only a blur
- [M4-P4] Wyrm uses neutral tone mapping and a vignette darkness ≤ 1 — reason: three's VignetteShader mixes toward 1 − darkness, and neutral tone mapping turns the negative values of a darkness above 1 into grey corners
- [M4-P4] Wyrm's seat colours are bright on the obsidian (3:1 or more against the surface `#17100e`, at least 0.15 apart from each other and from the ember orange of the seams), with dark ink for text on them and a glow tint mixed 20% toward the ember; patterns and glyphs as in Paper — reason: DESIGN Tokens, themes/signals.ts SEAT_CONTRAST
- [M4-P4] Wyrm lines: 2.5 px ribbons in seat colours with Paper's seat dashes, chevron arrowheads (dot for attachments, none for marks), glow 1 (marks 0.5), `flicker` animation and `embers` ornament; the fiery glow comes from the bloom (threshold 0.22) over the lines' glow copy — reason: DESIGN "fiery line glow"; scene/lines.ts does not read `glow`, `animation`, `ornament` or `SeatColour.glow` yet (pipeline hook reported, not added: themes do not edit scene/)
- [M4-P4] Wyrm has no sound set — reason: DESIGN makes sound optional and the theme table names none for Wyrm
- [M4-P4] Debug part `__spellplane.wyrm.instances()` (embers, shimmer state and screen region, quality per live build), typed by augmenting util/debug.ts DebugParts from themes/wyrm/debug.ts — reason: behaviour asserted on the debug hook, not pixels; no contract file changed
- [M4-P4] tests/unit/themes/m4-contracts.test.ts no longer expects Wyrm to be a stub (merged with M4-P5's matching edit: only Dusk remains a stub); tests/e2e/render/hot-swap.spec.ts is M4-P5's version unchanged (it reads bloom from the theme), Wyrm's exact pass lists are asserted in tests/e2e/themes/wyrm/wyrm.spec.ts — reason: the stub expectations were written against M4-C; keeping edits outside M4-P4's files to the one line that must change
- [M4-P4] The table's gate states in Wyrm are exercised on the render harness (ten scene-drawn states, screenshots in artifacts/shots/M4/wyrm/); "speaking", "away" and "pending confirmation" need the app's HUD and a confirmation round, which reach Wyrm with M4-P6's theme switch and are measured by its signals spec; the ego-rig variant skips until M4-P1 lands — reason: on m4 the app is fixed to Paper and the table has only the bird's-eye rig
- [M4-P4] Wyrm's pending confirmation is not measurable from themes/wyrm, and tests/e2e/signals.spec.ts "pending confirmation" stays red on Chromium for Wyrm and for Stonetable (already on m4): the scene's stack outline (scene/table-scene.ts entryHighlight, M4-P2) widens the controller's seat-coloured ring from 0.8 mm to the 2 mm pad and never reads `cardDressing.pending`; at the spec's zoom (≈ 0.3 px/mm, device pixel ratio 1 on Chromium) the ring pixels change by at most 22/255 on the dark slab and stone, under the 24/255 threshold, whatever the seat colour (WebKit renders at ratio 2 and passes). Not fixed here because PLAN M4 Ownership says a theme "needing a pipeline hook … reports it instead of editing scene/". Requested hook: entryHighlight returns `{ colour: theme.cardDressing.pending.colour, width: max(0.8, pending.width) + (2 − max(0.8, pending.width)) × pulse }` for a pending entry; tried locally (not committed), it turns the pair green for Paper, Wyrm and Stonetable on Chromium and WebKit. Wyrm's pending style (#ff6a20, 1.4 mm, glow 1) is unit-checked again: 3:1 on the slab, apart from hover, selection and every seat colour — reason: scene/ is outside M4-P4
- [M4-P4] The slab tile stays 768 px, computed synchronously on the first Wyrm build (≈ 55–80 ms in Node on the dev Mac, once per page); the page cache keeps only the four byte maps (≈ 9 MB) and a quality change sets the maps' anisotropy in place (scene/quality.ts setTextureAnisotropy, clamped to the GPU maximum, which the debug hook reports) instead of re-uploading them — reason: one tile per page costs less than a theme switch's shader compiles; a worker would add a second bundle and an async build to a synchronous `Theme.build`
- [M4-P4] Wyrm's animation clocks wrap at a period where the motion repeats exactly: the seam wave at 200π s and the shimmer at 20π s (whole cycles of each wave frequency), the embers at life × 16 × 64 s (≈ 2.1 h) with ember speeds quantised to sixteenths (0.6875–1.3125) and rebirth places repeating every 64 lives of an ember — reason: float32 times in the shaders lose precision after hours of play and the motion would step; a seamless wrap needs whole cycles of every term
- [M4-P4] The shimmer region cuts its two rings at the camera's near plane and keeps the part in front (UV clamped to −0.5…1.5) instead of dropping the region when any point is behind the camera — reason: an ego dolly into the lane would otherwise switch the shimmer off while the stack burns; null only when both rings are wholly behind or off screen
- [M4-P3] Dusk's light rig is one warm spot lamp (#ffd2a0, 45° cone, penumbra 1, decay 0, intensity 4.2, 1700 mm above a point 220 mm toward the viewer) plus a faint cool ambient fill; no shadow maps, only the pipeline's soft contact shadows; ACES tone mapping — reason: DESIGN "single warm lamp, soft contact shadows, slow light falloff"; a full penumbra over a cone wider than the table fades the light toward the corners instead of cutting it (every corner of every format's table, 2HG and four-player included, lies at least 3° inside the cone — review round 1 widened it from 38°, which left those corners to the ambient fill, and lowered the intensity from 4.6 so the felt half-way out keeps the surface token); real shadows are Stonetable's character
- [M4-P3] Dusk's bloom threshold (0.34, linear luminance as three's high-pass computes it) sits between everything on the table (felt, rail, zone hairlines, muted labels, sleeves) and every seat colour, and Dusk's lines are 3 px — reason: "soft bloom on lines" without a glowing table; the bloom samples the image at half resolution, so 2 px strokes fell under any threshold that kept the labels crisp
- [M4-P3] Dusk's vignette darkness is 1 (three's VignetteShader mixes toward 1 − darkness) — reason: a darkness above 1 drives colours negative before ACES, which renders as a grey haze and red/cyan fringes at the rail (seen with 1.15; the lit test theme's 1.6 does the same, reported for M4-P2)
- [M4-P3] Dusk's sleeves are matte (standard, roughness 0.62, no clearcoat) — reason: a clear-coated sleeve mirrored the overhead lamp into an HDR highlight that the bloom turned into a white blob over the cards
- [M4-P3] Dusk's surface token is the felt as rendered half-way between the table's middle and its corners (#1d3520, checked by tests/e2e/themes/dusk/look.spec.ts); seat colours are light pastels (each ≥ 5.7:1 on it, closest pair 0.21 apart), border 3.3:1 and muted 4.6:1 on it — reason: SEAT_CONTRAST and the HUD contrast rules must hold against what the viewer sees, not the felt material's base colour
