- [M4-P3] Dusk has its own neutral card back (night blue, brass frame, eight-pointed star, drawn in code) — reason: DESIGN Card dressing "the neutral card back" per theme; the face-down signal must read on dark felt; no official back
- [M4-P3] Dusk's sound set is synthesised with Web Audio (filtered seeded noise, sine partials) and on by default (`onByDefault: true`); "muted" means each cue's summed envelopes stay under 25% of the viewer's gain, the room tone under 5%, every envelope starts and ends silent and every cue stops within 1.6 s — reason: DESIGN makes sound off by default only in Paper and names Dusk's "muted ambience"; no audio files to license
- [M4-P3] Dusk's quality ladder: high (contact shadows, bloom and vignette, MSAA 4, anisotropy 8), medium (no shadows), low (no post-processing, pixel ratio 1); `setQuality` is a no-op since nothing of Dusk's table depends on the level — reason: DESIGN "drops shadows and post-processing first"; avoids a rebuild on a quality change
- [M4-P3] Dusk's table is a felt plane exactly the layout's table size (UVs in mm, nap repeating every 180 mm) and a flat 46 mm wooden rail outside it — reason: the rail must never cover a zone or occlude cards in the ego view; the felt keeps its scale on every table size
- [M4-P3] Dusk's own browser specs bundle their own page from source (tests/e2e/themes/dusk/page.ts: real table scene, real reducer, real Avatar.svelte with the app's `hudCssVars`) and keep only Dusk-specific evidence (the look, the sounds, the ten scene signals); speaking, away and pending confirmation are measured only by M4-P6's tests/e2e/signals.spec.ts in the real app — reason: review round 1, the duplicates used an easier setup that hid a Chromium failure; the page stays because it drives the scene, sounds and quality levels directly
- [M4-P3] tests/unit/themes/m4-contracts.test.ts checks only that a theme still stubbed names its package (instead of requiring Dusk to be a stub) — reason: the stub check had to change once Dusk replaced its stub; the relaxed form also lets Wyrm and Stonetable land without touching it
- [M4-P3] Known gaps reported, not fixed (scene/ is M4-P2's): the play table draws no foil marker in any theme (Dusk renders the foil state without errors; the pair is recorded); the scene does not read `HighlightStyle.glow` or `LineStyle.glow`/`animation` (Dusk sets them for when it does); `camera.birdseyeTiltDeg` waits for M4-P1's rig
- [M4-P3] Dusk's docs/ASSETS.md rows follow M4-P6's format: one row per generating module (`src/themes/dusk/textures.ts` texture, `src/themes/dusk/sounds.ts` sound), source `procedural`, licence `MIT (this repository)`, the exported names in Notes; table.ts is not listed (materials over those textures, not an asset kind) — reason: review round 1, the merged manifest check rejected the old rows
- [M4-P3] Stopping Dusk's room tone holds the level and the breath depth where they are (cancelAndHoldAtTime, or cancel plus a set to the current value where a browser lacks it) and glides both to silence — reason: review round 1, cancelScheduledValues during the fade-in dropped the ramp and the depth was zeroed at once, both audible clicks
- [M4-P3] tests/unit/themes/m4-contracts.test.ts also fails a non-Paper theme without a stub marker whose build or lighting is Paper's — reason: review round 1, the relaxed stub check otherwise accepted a stub that lost its marker
- [M4-P5] Stonetable's stone, runes, card back, dust and sounds are all procedural (stone.ts: tileable 512 px albedo, normal and roughness maps from seeded value noise, one tile per 640 mm; runes.ts: an invented straight-stroke alphabet; motes.ts: a point shader; sounds.ts: Web Audio) instead of downloaded CC0 files — reason: the run prefers procedural assets, nothing to license or fetch, and the maps tile on every table size; docs/ASSETS.md lists each generating module
- [M4-P5] The torch is a shadow-casting spot light high behind the viewer's left shoulder (viewer frame [-640, -980, 1380], cone 52° covering every corner of every format's table) with a 9% flicker, rather than a directional light — reason: the design's "torch-like directional light" needs both near-parallel shadows and a torch's falloff and flicker, and LightSpec flicker exists only on spot and point lights; at that distance the rays cross the table nearly parallel
- [M4-P5] The slab is one box flush with the table plane (top at height 0) reaching 80 mm beyond the table; the glowing rune band runs 10–56 mm outside the table edge on that rim, never under a card — reason: cards rest at height 0 and the play area stays plain stone so faces, outlines and lines read cleanly; three draw calls (slab, band, motes) whatever the card count
- [M4-P5] Stonetable lines are seat-coloured ribbons with `ornament: 'runes'`, `animation: 'flow'` (marks still), chevron heads and the seat's Paper dash pattern; they glow through the pipeline's glow-and-bloom pass. The scene's line layer (scene/lines.ts, not this package's) does not draw ribbon geometry, ornaments or animation yet, so runes along lines appear once it does — reason: PLAN M4: a theme needing a pipeline hook beyond SceneContext.post/materials reports it instead of editing scene/
- [M4-P5] Dust motes and the rune band's slow breathing freeze with reduced motion (the torch flicker is already stilled by scene/lights.ts); motes are drawn only at quality levels with particles and switch without a rebuild (ThemeInstance.setQuality) — reason: DESIGN Motion "with reduced motion, all of these are cuts"; QUALITY_DROP_ORDER
- [M4-P5] Quality ladder: high (shadow map 2048, contact shadows, bloom and vignette, motes, MSAA 4), medium (no shadows, MSAA 2), low (no post, no motes, pixel ratio 1) — reason: DESIGN Renderer "drops shadows and post-processing first"; 300 cards measure at most 28 draw calls at the high level (render/performance.spec.ts), the budget is 60
- [M4-P5] The surface token is the slab albedo's average (#5d5b56, unit-tested), seat colours are bright "rune light" tones reaching 3:1 on it and on the HUD panel with dark ink, Paper's patterns and glyphs kept — reason: themes/signals.ts SEAT_CONTRAST and M4-P6's conformance rules; the card shader dims owner-viewed face-down cards toward this colour
- [M4-P5] Browser gate-state checks run Stonetable in the render harness for the scene states it can reach (tapped, selected, hovered, displaced, attacking, blocking, face-down, seat colour) as controlled screenshot pairs; speaking, away and pending confirmation need the app's HUD and engine, which only switch theme through M4-P6's switcher, so tests/e2e/signals.spec.ts covers them for Stonetable after both merge; foil is rendered but not measured because the table scene draws no foil sheen yet (only draft and builder do) — reason: render-harness.ts, the app and the scene belong to other packages
- [M4-P5] tests/unit/themes/m4-contracts.test.ts no longer expects Stonetable to be the M4-C stub — reason: this package replaces the stub; Dusk and Wyrm's entries stay until their packages land
- [M4-P5] tests/e2e/render/hot-swap.spec.ts reads whether a theme blooms from the theme itself instead of assuming every theme but the lit test theme is a post-free stub — reason: Stonetable replaced its stub and runs bloom; Dusk and Wyrm need no further edit when they land
- [M4-P5] Integration: a stack entry whose confirmation round waits is outlined in the theme's `cardDressing.pending` colour (pulsing in width as before) instead of its controller's seat colour (scene/stack.ts entryOutline) — reason: themes/signals.ts styles pendingConfirmation by cardDressing.pending, which the table scene ignored; on Stonetable's stone the thin seat-coloured ring changed no pixel beyond the threshold (signals.spec 0%)
- [M4-P4] Everything in Wyrm is generated, nothing bundled: slab maps computed in TypeScript (768 px tile per 288 mm, cached), embers and shimmer are shaders, the card back is a canvas drawing — reason: the design says ornament is geometric and generated; nothing to license or fetch
- [M4-P4] The slab uses its own MeshStandardMaterial (emissive map plus a few injected shader lines for the heat wave) instead of SceneContext.materials — reason: the material spec has no emissive map
- [M4-P4] Quality: high has everything; medium drops shadows and the shimmer; low drops post-processing and the embers; the slab's texture filtering follows the level — reason: the design drops shadows and post-processing first; passes the existing quality-ladder check
- [M4-P4] The shimmer is a post pass (order 500) over an ellipse around the projected stack lane (radius: lane half-diagonal + 20 mm), in the chain only while visible, 400 ms fade — reason: card faces are drawn after post-processing, so it never touches them, and an empty stack costs nothing
- [M4-P4] With reduced motion: no shimmer, embers frozen at a fixed moment, seam heat wave stopped — reason: the design makes all motion a cut
- [M4-P4] Neutral tone mapping and vignette darkness ≤ 1 — reason: three's vignette with darkness above 1 produced grey corners under neutral tone mapping
- [M4-P4] Seat colours have 3:1 or more contrast against the slab colour #17100e, are at least 0.15 apart from each other and from the ember orange, with dark text colour and a glow tint mixed 20% toward the ember; patterns and glyphs as in Paper — reason: design tokens and SEAT_CONTRAST
- [M4-P4] Lines are 2.5 px ribbons with Paper's seat dashes, chevron arrowheads (dot for attachments, none for marks), glow 1 (marks 0.5), flicker animation and embers ornament; the glow comes from the bloom (threshold 0.22) — reason: the scene does not read these fields yet; reported rather than added, since themes do not edit scene/
- [M4-P4] No sound set — reason: sound is optional and the design names none for Wyrm
- [M4-P4] New debug part __spellplane.wyrm.instances(), typed by extending the debug-hook types from themes/wyrm/debug.ts — reason: tests assert on the hook, not pixels; no contract file changed
- [M4-P4] Removed the placeholder expectation for Wyrm in tests/unit/themes/m4-contracts.test.ts, and limited the no-post-passes expectation in tests/e2e/render/hot-swap.spec.ts to Paper — reason: both were written against placeholder themes
- [M4-P4] The ten table-drawn gate states are tested on the render harness; speaking, away and pending confirmation wait for M4-P6's theme switch and signals spec; the ego-camera variant skips until M4-P1 — reason: on m4 the app is fixed to Paper and only the bird's-eye camera exists
- [M4-P4] Wyrm pending confirmation cannot be measured from themes/wyrm. The scene's stack outline never reads cardDressing.pending, and at the signal spec's zoom the ring widening is sub-pixel (about 22/255). Reported as a scene hook; the hollow unit check is removed. Reason: scene/ is outside M4-P4.
- [M4-P4] The slab tile stays 768 px and is built synchronously (about 55 to 80 ms once per page). The cache keeps only the byte maps. Anisotropy is set in place, clamped to the GPU maximum, and that clamped value is reported. Reason: a worker would mean an async build behind a synchronous Theme.build, plus a second bundle.
- [M4-P4] hot-swap.spec asserts Wyrm's pass list (shimmer optional) instead of skipping the check. Reason: the reviewer asked for an explicit pass list; the edit is outside the package's files and is listed for the integrator.
- [M4-P4] Merged m4 into m4-p4 rather than rebasing. Reason: never rewrite history; the only conflict was the append-only DECISIONS file.
- [M4-P4] Merged m4 (M4-P5 Stonetable) into m4-p4. Took M4-P5's hot-swap.spec.ts unchanged and kept only Dusk as a stub in m4-contracts.test.ts — reason: fewest edits outside M4-P4's files.
- [M4-P4] Pending confirmation stays red on Chromium until scene/table-scene.ts entryHighlight reads cardDressing.pending. The hook is requested (patch tested locally, not committed) — reason: PLAN M4 Ownership forbids themes editing scene/; Stonetable fails the same way on m4.
- [M4-P4] Animation clocks wrap at exact repeat periods: seam wave 200π s, shimmer 20π s, embers life × 16 × 64 s, with ember speeds quantised to 1/16 and rebirth places repeating every 64 lives — reason: float32 shader time would step after hours, and a seamless wrap needs whole cycles of every term.
- [M4-P4] The shimmer region cuts its rings at the near plane (UV clamped to -0.5..1.5) and is null only when both rings are wholly behind the camera or off screen — reason: an ego dolly into the lane must not switch the shimmer off.
- [M4-P3] Dusk's pending colour (`cardDressing.pending`) is the lamp's warm near-white #fffaf0, at least 0.36 from every seat colour and 0.2 from hover, selection and ghost (unit-tested) — reason: review round 2; since the M4-P5 integration the scene outlines a waiting stack entry in this colour instead of the controller's seat colour (the 0.8 → 2 mm width change alone was sub-pixel on the conformance table and failed tests/e2e/signals.spec.ts "Dusk › pending confirmation" in Chromium), and the earlier amber #ffb35c sat 0.07 from seat 4's amber, so a seat-4 entry would again have changed only in width
- [M4-P3] Dusk's room tone builds no nodes at gain 0 (a no-op handle), and each cue's master gain and the room tone's output disconnect from the destination when their last source ends — reason: review round 2 hardening; nothing runs silently until stop()
- [M4-P3] Known gap reported, not fixed (outside this package): nothing in the app plays a theme's SoundSet yet (no player reads its cues, `ambience` or `onByDefault` against Settings.sound), so Dusk's sounds are verified only by unit tests and the Dusk harness, and its muted ambience cannot be heard in the app — reason: review round 2; the sound player belongs in the gate report for the integrator
- [M4-P3] One warm spot lamp (#ffd2a0, 38° cone, penumbra 1, decay 0, 1700 mm up) plus a faint cool ambient fill; contact shadows only, no shadow maps; ACES tone mapping. Reason: the design's 'single warm lamp, soft contact shadows, slow light falloff'; real shadows are Stonetable's character
- [M4-P3] Bloom threshold 0.34 (linear luminance) sits between the table's colours (felt, rail, hairlines, labels, sleeves) and every seat colour; lines are 3 px. Reason: three's bloom samples at half resolution, so 2 px strokes never passed a threshold that kept the labels crisp
- [M4-P3] Vignette darkness is capped at 1. Reason: a darkness above 1 pushes colours below zero before ACES, which showed as a grey haze and red/cyan fringes
- [M4-P3] Matte sleeves (standard material, roughness 0.62, no clearcoat). Reason: a clear-coated sleeve reflected the overhead lamp into a highlight that the bloom turned into a white blob
- [M4-P3] Surface token is the felt as rendered half-way out (#1d3520; the browser spec checks it). Seat colours are pastels (at least 5.7:1 against it, closest pair 0.21 apart); border 3.3:1, muted 4.6:1. Reason: the contrast rules must hold against what the viewer actually sees
- [M4-P3] Dusk has its own geometric card back (night blue, brass frame, eight-pointed star). Reason: each theme dresses the neutral back; no official back allowed
- [M4-P3] Sound is synthesised with Web Audio and on by default; each cue peaks at most 25% of the viewer's gain, the room tone at most 5%, every envelope starts and ends silent, and every cue stops within 1.6 s. Reason: the design makes sound off by default only in Paper and names Dusk's 'muted ambience'
- [M4-P3] Quality ladder: high (contact shadows, post-processing, MSAA 4), medium (no shadows), low (no post-processing, pixel ratio 1); setQuality is a no-op because nothing on Dusk's table depends on the level. Reason: the design says shadows and post-processing are dropped first
- [M4-P3] The felt is exactly the table size; a flat 46 mm rail lies outside it. Reason: the rail must never cover a zone or hide cards in the ego view
- [M4-P3] Dusk's browser specs bundle their own page from source. Reason: until M4-P6 lands the app always shows Paper, and the render harness has neither confirmation rounds nor a HUD
- [M4-P3] m4-contracts.test.ts now only checks that a theme still stubbed names its package. Reason: Dusk replaced its stub; the relaxed form also lets Wyrm and Stonetable land without touching it
- [M4-P3] M4-P2's hot-swap spec now expects the post passes each theme's own settings ask for. Reason: it assumed every non-test theme was a stub with no post-processing
- [M4-P3] Dusk's ASSETS.md rows: one per generating module (textures.ts texture, sounds.ts sound), procedural, MIT (this repository), exported names in Notes; table.ts not listed — it only holds materials over those textures, which is not an allowed asset kind
- [M4-P3] Lamp cone 45° and intensity 4.2 (was 38° and 4.6); every corner of every format's table lies at least 3° inside the cone — reason: review round 1; the intensity was lowered so the felt half-way out still matches the surface token
- [M4-P3] Stopping the room tone holds the level and the breath depth (cancelAndHoldAtTime, with a cancel-and-set fallback) and glides both to 0 — reason: cancelScheduledValues during the fade-in, and zeroing the depth, both clicked
- [M4-P3] The Dusk e2e harness uses hudCssVars like the app and keeps only Dusk-specific evidence; speaking, away and pending confirmation are measured only by M4-P6's signals.spec.ts — reason: the copies used an easier setup that hid the Chromium failure
- [M4-P3] m4-contracts fails a non-Paper theme without a stub marker that shares Paper's build or lighting — reason: the relaxed stub check accepted a stub that had lost its marker
- [M4-P3] Known gap reported, scene/ not edited: the pending-confirmation signal is a sub-pixel seat-colour outline growth that ignores cardDressing.pending, so it cannot pass on a dark felt at DPR 1; fix belongs in scene/table-scene.ts entryHighlight
- [M4-P3] History: m4 is merged with a merge commit (fc4fb66, conflict resolution and ASSETS rows only), and the review fixes are a separate commit (fba5bd3); nothing was rewritten or force-pushed
- [M4-P3] Dusk's cardDressing.pending colour is the warm near-white #fffaf0 (DUSK_PENDING). It is at least 0.36 from every seat colour and at least 0.2 from hover, selection and ghost, and this is unit-tested. Reason: the integrated scene (e7885f9) outlines a waiting stack entry in this colour, and the old amber #ffb35c sat 0.07 from seat 4's amber.
- [M4-P3] Dusk's room tone builds no nodes at gain 0, and cue masters and the room-tone output disconnect from the destination when their last source ends. Reason: review round 2 hardening.
- [M4-P3] Known gap reported: no app sound player plays a theme's SoundSet yet. Reason: Dusk's on-by-default sounds and muted ambience cannot be heard in the app; this belongs in the integrator's gate report.
- [M4-P3] Merging m4 kept m4's hot-swap.spec.ts and dropped the P3 decision about editing it. Reason: M4-P5 had already made the spec read bloom from each theme, and the spec is M4-P2's.
- [M4-P3] Integration: m4-contracts.test.ts expects no stub at all now that Dusk, Wyrm and Stonetable have landed (the merge conflicted on which single theme was still a stub). Reason: union of both sides' edits.
- [M4-G] The scripted game per theme is the M1 gate's game with both players picking the theme in the lobby's picker; Ann alone switches mid-game through the HUD picker (to the next theme after turn 4, back after turn 5) while Ben stays in the game's theme, and presses P before Ben's turn 4 and again after it. Reason: themes are per viewer, so one table in two themes is the mid-game case worth gating; turn 4 needs only keys from Ann, so the ego view cannot disturb drag targets.
- [M4-G] The screenshot matrix runs on a two-player networked table (pass timer off in the lobby) set up through the UI: the fitted table, then a wheel zoom to cards about 64 px tall for the neutral table and the twelve signals; Ben's page supplies the attacker for blocking, the face-down card, the speaker (talk key held) and the away seat; only foil uses the debug hook (no UI path puts a foil on the play table). Reason: at the fitted view a battlefield card is about 30 px tall, too small to judge a signal.
- [M4-G] The gate's performance check gives Dusk and Wyrm Stonetable's budget of 60 draw calls. Reason: PLAN sets numbers only for Paper (20) and Stonetable (60); Dusk and Wyrm are lit themes on the same pipeline.
- [M4-P1] Both rigs reduce to one CameraPose that faces the viewer's forward axis (no yaw; pitch 90° is bird's-eye) and the table scene maps screen and table through it (scene/camera/pose.ts); the bird's-eye rig moved to scene/camera/birdseye.ts, scene/camera.ts re-exports it for the draft and builder surfaces — reason: one continuous family of poses makes the blend and the anchor solve exact.
- [M4-P1] The blend interpolates pitch and field of view linearly and the visible half-height at the target log-linearly, eased in-out cubic over theme.motion.cameraBlendMs (400 ms), and solves the camera's position parallel to the table so the anchor's table point stays exactly under the anchor; when a rig's limits keep the destination from holding it (a cursor at the bottom corners beyond the ego lean limit; a cursor beyond the table going to bird's-eye, whose centre stays on the table) the point moves on a straight line between where the two end poses show it — reason: no jump when the blend ends.
- [M4-P1] A switch to ego is solved from the seat (dolly 0): lean and pitch, then lean and dolly at a pitch limit; a switch to bird's-eye returns to the zoom the viewer left it at — reason: pressing P twice comes back to the same view.
- [M4-P1] Ego gestures: a sideways pan drag leans, a drag toward or away from the viewer dollies (pulling the table toward you moves forward), with the bird's-eye pan inertia; wheel and pinch dolly toward the cursor and correct lean and pitch so its point stays put; right-drag on empty table pitches 0.2° per px, dragging down looks further down (a right-drag from a card still draws a line, a right-click still opens the menu) — reason: DESIGN names lean (pan), dolly (zoom) and pitch without mappings.
- [M4-P1] Ego fit (0): back at the seat, centred, pitched so the viewer's near and far table edges sit symmetrically about the view centre (clamped); go to a seat, avatar taps and follow-the-active-player aim the view centre at the territory (lean, pitch from the seat) and dolly toward it until it is framed; zoom to a card does the same for one card at 80% of the viewport height, within the dolly limit — reason: "every gesture works the same in both" rigs.
- [M4-P1] "Never below the table edge" is a camera height of at least minHeight (120 mm) and "never behind the seat" is dolly ≥ 0, with M4-C's lean ±400 and dolly ≤ 700 unchanged; the ego seat stands in front of the viewer's own territory (offset along their right axis) — reason: three players and Two-Headed Giant share an edge.
- [M4-P1] setRig without an anchor (the HUD's View button, a theme's default rig) keeps the view centre; the P key passes the pointer (new optional CommandHost.pointer in ui/commands.ts, the HUD's last pointer position); an anchor outside the canvas falls back to the centre — reason: the contract says absent means the viewport centre; the design wants the cursor for P.
- [M4-P1] Pan, zoom, pitch and fling are ignored during the 400 ms switch; fit, seat keys and zoom-to during it change its destination — reason: the blend owns the camera and the anchor.
- [M4-P1] A theme's default rig applies when the scene starts (unless TableSceneOptions.rig is given) and on a theme switch until the viewer has switched rigs; a theme's ego overrides and Dusk's birdseyeTiltDeg (pitch 90° − tilt, at most 30°) are applied with it; fit and seat framing keep the straight-down maths for a tilted bird's-eye (inside the 5% margin for small tilts), zoom at the cursor and pan are solved exactly — reason: DESIGN "Defaults: preferred camera rig"; Dusk is "slightly tilted".
- [M4-P1] Follow the active player reacts to every turn change (another seat, or a new turn number) and frames a team's territory in Two-Headed Giant; follow the stack brings in the newest new item when its rest slot's centre is not at least 40 px inside the viewport, centring it at the same zoom; while a card, marker, pan, pinch, pitch, marquee or line gesture is held the move waits and runs when it ends — reason: "nothing ever moves the camera while the player is dragging", and the move should not be lost.
- [M4-P1] The HUD reads Settings.followStack/followActive once when the table scene is created and hands them to setFollow when one is on (the scene starts with both off); no HUD control changes them yet (settings consolidation is M5-P5) — reason: package ownership.
- [M4-P1] The HUD toolbar gains a "View" button that switches rigs like P — reason: DESIGN "One key (P) or a HUD button".
- [M4-P1] In the ego rig the texture tier and table-text legibility use the scale at the view centre; two-finger vertical drag (touch pitch) is left to M5-P1 — reason: per-card perspective level of detail is performance work; touch is M5-P1's package.
- [M4-P1] The scene harness gains the P key (anchored at the mouse) and a `turnPass` case in its reducer stub — reason: the follow-the-active-player e2e test needs a turn change.
- [M4-P1] The ego lean limit is widened per table and seat to reach the table's far side edge from the seat: max(EgoRigParams.leanMm, half the table's extent along the viewer's right axis + |seat lateral|) (`egoLimitsFor`; 700 on 1v1, up to 1650 on Two-Headed Giant); DEFAULT_EGO_RIG.leanMm (400) stays the floor and a theme may still set more — reason: with ±400 a switch from bird's-eye could not keep the cursor's table point under the cursor on three-player, four-player and Two-Headed Giant tables (PLAN M4-P1: less than 2 px); supersedes "lean ±400 unchanged" in the line above.
- [M4-P1] A switch to bird's-eye keeps the zoom the viewer left unless its centre would then leave the table; then it zooms in just enough to keep the cursor's table point under the cursor — reason: from a seat beside the centre line the ego view shows table points near the screen edge that a fitted bird's-eye cannot put there.
- [M4-P1] Cursors over the surround (not the table) are outside the 2 px guarantee: no rig pose within the limits need show a point beyond the table's edge at the cursor; there the point moves on a straight line between where the two rigs show it — reason: the bird's-eye centre stays on the table and the ego rig never goes behind the seat.
- [M4-P1] Fit (0) and zoom to a card (Z) in the ego rig are best effort within its limits: from the seat the near corners of the table lie outside a 45° view, and the dolly and minimum height keep a card below 80% of the viewport height — reason: DESIGN "never behind the seat" and the spec's seat geometry; bird's-eye is the rig for the whole table.
- [M4-P1] Follow moves wait while any pointer is pressed anywhere on the page (a press on the table below the drag threshold, a hand-fan drag still in the HUD) — reason: DESIGN "nothing ever moves the camera while the player is dragging", without a contract change for a HUD drag signal.
- [M4-P1] The HUD hands the stored follow toggles to the scene on mount and again whenever they changed when the window regains focus or becomes visible, both values each time — reason: SettingsStore has no change events (a `subscribe` would be a contract change for M5's settings panel).
- [M4-P1] A switch to ego solves lean, dolly and pitch together: from the seat forward in 25 mm dolly steps up to the dolly limit, at each step lean and pitch, then lean and dolly at the pitch reached, stopping at the first that holds the cursor's table point (within 0.05 px); otherwise the closest found — reason: a bird's-eye view panned or zoomed away from the cursor can need the ego rig to dolly forward (PLAN M4-P1: less than 2 px); supersedes "solved from the seat (dolly 0)" above.
- [M4-P1] Switching from a deep bird's-eye zoom can leave the cursor's table point unreachable inside the ego limits (for example a card zoom (Z) at (-600, 500) on 1v1 with the cursor at the screen's right edge, or at (0, -500) with the cursor in the top-left corner: no lean, dolly and pitch within the limits gets the point closer than about 430 and 400 px); the switch then lands on the closest ego pose and the point moves on a straight line between where the two rigs show it, like a cursor over the surround — reason: widening the limits further would put the camera below the table edge or past the viewer's own cards; outside the 2 px guarantee.
- [M4-P1] A fit, seat key, avatar tap, zoom to a card or follow move that arrives during the 400 ms switch restarts the blend from the camera's current pose toward the new destination over the move's duration, without the cursor anchor (with reduced motion it cuts and ends the blend) — reason: DESIGN "Motion uses critically damped springs" and the switch is "an animated camera move"; replacing the destination in place made the camera jump; supersedes "change its destination" above.
- [M4-P1] Each card's texture tier comes from its own on-screen height: the card's height times the scale at its own depth along the view (TableRig.pxPerMmAt), equal to the view centre's scale straight down; table-text legibility still uses the view centre's scale — reason: DESIGN Texture tiers by on-screen height; in ego near cards show much larger than far ones; supersedes the texture-tier half of the ego level-of-detail deferral above.
- [M4-P1] A switch to bird's-eye from an ego view leaned beyond the table's side edge keeps the cursor's table point under the cursor by leaving the bird's-eye centre beyond the table edge (at the zoom the viewer left) when no zoom can keep it on the table; from such a view a pan, zoom, inertia or resize may keep the centre where it is or bring it back toward the table but never push it further out (BirdseyeRig.clampView grows the table box to include the current centre), so nothing jumps; fit, seat keys and zoom to a card clamp to the table — reason: PLAN M4-P1 "less than 2 px" over the table (the ego lean must reach the far side edge for the switch the other way); supersedes the "zoom in just enough" fallback when that cannot hold the point.
- [M4-P1] A card's texture tier uses its projected on-screen height: the screen distance between the midpoints of its two short edges at the height it is drawn (TableRig.cardScreenHeight); straight down this is the card's height times the scale; in ego it includes foreshortening, and a tapped card's long axis (across the view) is not foreshortened — reason: DESIGN Texture tiers "on-screen height"; texture memory against the 400 MB cap; supersedes the per-card depth scale above.
- [M4-P1] Picking a card and its pile badge test the point where the pointer ray crosses the card's drawn top (its pile height, plus the drag lift while dragged, plus the card thickness), not the table plane — reason: in ego a 20 mm pile's top shows about 16 mm from its footprint; "every gesture works the same in both" rigs.
- [M4-P1] The camera's near clip plane is half the nearest depth at which anything up to 48 mm high (four drag lifts) can show: the larger of the table's nearest corner and where the view's bottom-edge rays reach that height (TableRig.clipPlanes); dollied over the table it stays about 40 mm or more instead of 1 mm — reason: depth precision for thin card boxes and decals in lit themes.
- [M4-P1] A pointer move with no button down ends a press the page never saw released (let go outside the window, a native context menu), so follow moves stop waiting — reason: DESIGN "nothing ever moves the camera while the player is dragging", without a stale press holding the camera forever.
- [M4-P1] Asking for the rig already shown (setRig to the current rig) is not the viewer's choice: a later theme switch still applies the new theme's preferred rig — reason: DESIGN Defaults, preferred camera rig.
- [M4-G fix] M4-P1 (camera rigs) merged into `m4` at its round-3 head `fada822`, with no separate review round, so the gate can take its ego half. The merge keeps M4-P2's render loop, theme rebuild, card boxes, contact shadows and held lift. A card is picked at its drawn top: pile height plus `HeldLift.value` plus `CardLayer.faceLift`, the same height the card layer draws it at. The idle predicate waits for both follow moves and lift springs. The M4-C stubs test is dropped, since both stubbed packages are now implemented — reason: docs/gates/M4-parked-m4-p1.md, "What a retry should do first"; the unreviewed round-3 changes remain for the owner's playtest (deep-zoom exemption, a bird's-eye centre beyond the table edge after a switch).
- [M4-G fix] The play table marks a foil printing that is shown face up. It gets the draft's drifting sheen as a 1.2 mm rim outside the face, in the outline slot, so selection and hover replace it. It also gets a badge: a small outlined diamond (half-diagonal 3.2 mm) over the card's bottom-left corner, on the collector line, drawn by the card shader like a counter badge. The face is never tinted. While foils are drawn and motion is not reduced, the sheen redraws at 20 fps. The foil signal's probe takes the outline signals' 4 mm margin, because its rim lies outside the face (themes/signals.ts) — reason: DESIGN "Foils are marked with a subtle sheen and a badge"; "card faces are never recoloured"; a battlefield has no gutter for the draft's badge beside the card.
- [M4-G fix] FOIL_SHEEN and foilSheenColour move from scene/draft.ts to scene/foil.ts, and draft.ts re-exports them — reason: the table scene needs them, and importing them should not pull in the draft surface.
- [M4-G fix] Decklists mark a foil printing with a trailing `*F*`, the Moxfield and Archidekt export marker. `*E*` (etched) also counts as foil. `DeckEntry.foil?: true` is an additive contract field (data/types.ts), and resolving a deck carries it to `CardRef.foil`. Only a marker at the end of the line counts. The M4 gate's matrix now puts its foil card on the table through the UI: one `*F*` line in Ann's list, dragged from her hand — reason: GAP finding (no UI path to a foil in a constructed game); precedent for an additive gate-fix contract change: [M3-G].
- [M4-G fix] The theme's sound set now plays. ui/sound.ts `ThemeSoundPlayer` follows `Settings.sound`: 'theme' plays sets with `onByDefault`, 'on' plays any set, 'off' plays nothing. The theme picker has a Sound select. Cues come from new action-log lines, at most one of each kind per batch: move, moveTemp, settle, toZone, draw and tidy play the slide; tap the tap; shuffleDone the shuffle; turnPass the turn chime; another seat's ask the prompt tick. The AudioContext is made on the viewer's first press or key, and only after the stored setting has been read. A theme switch stops the old set's ambience. The HUD root carries `data-sound` ('off', 'on' or 'playing') for tests. No sound is bundled; the sets are procedural (M4-P3, M4-P5) — reason: M4-P3's reported gap; DESIGN "What a theme package contains: Sound".
- [M4-G fix] The HUD toolbar wraps to a second row instead of running under the turn strip (max-width max(220px, 50vw − 336px)) — reason: M4-P1's View button pushed the theme button (◐) under the turn strip at 1440 px, which broke every HUD theme switch in the gate.
- [M4-G fix] The gate's perspective step starts from the theme's default rig: bird's-eye in Paper and Dusk, ego in Wyrm and Stonetable. P switches to the other rig for Ben's turn 4 and back after it. The performance check measures both rigs per theme — reason: DESIGN theme table, Default camera; PLAN M4-G "both camera rigs".
- [M4-G fix] CI now runs `pnpm check` in one job and the browser suite in a second job. That job is a matrix of 2 browsers × 4 shards, each with a 45-minute limit. The report and traces are uploaded even when a job fails or is cancelled. Under CI the test timeout is 180 s and the expect timeout 15 s; locally they stay 60 s and 5 s. No spec asserts on timing — reason: on GitHub's runners Chromium renders WebGL with SwiftShader, so a lit-theme frame costs many times what it does on a GPU, and one job ran past its 30-minute limit before it could print any error.
- [M4-G fix] The table's hover outline is at least 1.5 CSS px on screen, up to the 2 mm outline slot (scene/cards.ts legibleOutlineMm). Each theme's own width still applies when it is wider — reason: minor gate finding, hover hard to see at a play zoom of about 64 px per card. The width a theme sets in mm is under a pixel there.
- [M4-G fix] The table scene's face arrays grow only while their GPU bytes, mip-maps included, fit the 400 MB texture cap less the theme's own textures and a 16 MB headroom (scene/textures.ts grownLayers; FaceTextures `gpuBudget`). Each array grows by doubling, or by whatever smaller step still fits. Once full, the normal and large tiers evict their least recently drawn faces, and a card that does not fit shows a smaller tier. Thumbnails are pinned and still always grow. The draft and builder surfaces keep the plain cap — reason: with the ego rig merged, near cards ask for larger tiers, and the doubling arrays reached 510–630 MB at 300 cards (M4 gate performance check, PLAN M4 Performance "texture memory under 400 MB"). The parked-branch notes had flagged this risk.
- [M4-G fix] `SPELLPLANE_SWIFTSHADER=1` makes local Chromium runs render with SwiftShader, as CI's runners do (playwright.config.ts) — reason: CI's lit-theme failures could not be reproduced on a GPU.
- [M4-G fix] Under CI, render/performance.spec marks its lit-theme cases slow, which triples their budget — reason: on CI's SwiftShader, 300 lit cards with shadows and bloom ran past 180 s (run 43450220586, chromium shard 2). The spec asserts nothing on time.
- [M4-G fix] Root cause of "history diverged at seq N" after a reload or a sequencer close/reopen: on `pagehide` net/broadcast.ts reported every peer gone to its own page, whose engine was still running. The page's engine took the room for lost. As sequencer it proposed `away` for the other seat; as follower it claimed and recorded `sequencerClaim` and `away`. The session saved those records, which no other page had, so the reopened page (or the room, adopting the longer copy) found a fork. Now a page that unloads says `bye` but reports no peer gone to itself; only an explicit `leave` (the engine closes first) reports them — reason: M4 gate finding; reproduced in tests/sim/net/reload.test.ts, transport check in tests/unit/net/broadcast.test.ts.
- [M4-G fix] TrysteroTransport likewise ignores the peer leaves Trystero reports after `beforeunload` (Trystero leaves its rooms then and closes the connections ~100 ms later, while a reloading page is still alive) — reason: the same fork in production; tests/unit/net/trystero.test.ts on a stand-in room. A page restored from the back/forward cache after that stays cut off, as before.
- [M4-G fix] A fork repair whose abandoned part holds only `away` and `sequencerClaim` records shows no "history diverged" warning (nothing a player did is lost; the adopted history's sequencer derives those records afresh). A fork that abandons any other action, or one too old to read, still warns — reason: the message alarmed players where nothing was lost (a page cut off from the room that only noted the others gone).
- [M4-G fix] Not changed: a page that is reloaded (not reconnected) after it sequenced its own actions alone does not re-propose them when the room's history replaces that part, since its own cids are kept in memory only and the session's cids carry no identity. The warning shows in that case. For the integrator; a fix needs own-action tracking across reloads (session or the persisted save).
- [M4-P1 review] Round 3's fix (`fada822`) closes the ego-to-bird's-eye finding: a property over ego views reached by the viewer's own pan, zoom and pitch, every format and seat, five viewport sizes, with and without the HUD insets and Dusk's tilt, holds every cursor over the table to under 2 px (`tests/unit/camera/switch.test.ts`). The reverse direction did not hold. From the seat, within the 25°–60° pitch, the ego view cannot show the viewer's own near cards at the top of the screen, the far side at the bottom, or a point near one side edge at the opposite screen edge. So a switch from any panned or zoomed bird's-eye view moved the cursor's point for 10–25% of cursors over the table: at 1.5× zoom 10–16%, worst 300–600 px; for example 1v1, 1440×900, 3× zoom on the far side with the cursor at (700, 800) moved 153 px. Even the fitted view with the HUD insets on three players failed. The deep-zoom exemption above described only part of this. Fix: when the limits cannot hold the point, the switch to ego reaches beyond them (`egoSwitchNav`). It first widens the pitch to 5°–85° (`EGO_PITCH_HARD`), then also the lean and the dolly to twice their limits. The camera never goes below `minHeight` and never behind the seat. Only a cursor over the table reaches beyond the limits; a cursor over the surround stays outside the guarantee and lands within them. Later pans, zooms, pitches, inertia and theme changes are clamped to the limits grown to the current navigation (`TableRig.egoReach`, `egoLimitsWith`), so they keep the view there or bring it back toward the limits, never further out, and never jump. Fit, seat keys, avatar taps, zoom to a card and follow ease back within the limits — reason: PLAN M4-P1 "less than 2 px" and DESIGN "the point under the cursor stays under the cursor"; this mirrors round 3's bird's-eye centre beyond the table edge; supersedes the deep-zoom exemption. The owner should judge such landings at the playtest: they can be flatter or steeper than the pitch range allows, for example 11.7° in the 1v1 case above.
- [M4-P1 review] A wheel or pinch zoom from a bird's-eye centre that a switch left beyond the table edge keeps the cursor's table point while the cursor is over the table. Zooming out may then move the centre further out; zooming in or panning brings it back — reason: re-clamping pivoted the zoom on the frozen centre, and one wheel tick moved the cursor's point by up to about 100 px (a table edge's own clamp moves it by under 20); supersedes "never push it further out" for zoom in the [M4-P1] line on the bird's-eye centre beyond the table edge.
- [M4-G fix] The gate matrix presses P with the pointer at the centre of Ann's row (from c1's left edge to the foil's slot right of c2), not at the resting point left of c1. Each theme's ego view gets its own resting point left of c1 as that view shows it — reason: the switch holds the point under the pointer, and the ego view shows the row larger than the zoomed bird's-eye does, so from a point left of the row the row spread right under the log panel. A real player who presses P with the pointer beside their cards gets the same view; that is the design's rule ("the point under the cursor stays under the cursor"), and the player points at what they want to keep. Ann's pile column stays out of every ego shot: at the spec's seat (260 mm behind the edge, 620 mm up, 45° vertical field of view, pitched 35°), the near right corner of the viewer's own territory lies outside the view at any lean of 0 (DECISIONS [M4-P1] "Fit (0) and zoom to a card (Z) in the ego rig are best effort"). The owner should judge this at the Stonetable and Wyrm playtest, since those themes open in ego.
- [M4-G fix] The bloom's glow copy of lines and ping rings is drawn at least 4 device px wide (scene/post.ts GLOW_MIN_DEVICE_PX); the crisp stroke over the table keeps the theme's width — reason: the bloom finds bright areas in a half-resolution copy, one bilinear sample per 2 × 2 pixels, so a 3 px antialiased stroke could fall between two sample rows and stay under the threshold. Whether a line glowed depended on its sub-pixel position: Dusk's lines lost their bloom under the 10° bird's-eye tilt with MSAA (look.spec "soft bloom on lines", +0.6 instead of about +35).
- [M4-G fix] Dusk's hover outline is a quiet warm grey (#b9b4aa, 0.7 mm) and its selection outline a saturated gold (#ffbf40) at the full 2 mm outline slot (was pale cream #f0dcb4 against warm gold #ffd28a at 1.4 mm) — reason: minor gate finding, the two were hard to tell apart at play zoom. At about 64 px per card the legible hover minimum (1.5 px) is as wide as the selection outline, so hue and saturation have to carry the difference.
- [M4-G fix] Ambient motion is paced by what the last frame cost (scene/pacing.ts). Ambient motion is what moves with nothing changing: a lit theme's light and particles, Wyrm's shimmer, the pending pulse, ping fades and the foil sheen. While a frame costs at most 50 ms it draws every animation frame, as before. Once a frame costs more, the next ambient-only frame waits until 4 frame costs have passed since the last drawn frame. Changes (state, view, camera, drags, card motion, texture uploads) still draw at once, and theme animation advances by the time since the last drawn frame (at most 100 ms) — reason: on a software renderer (CI's SwiftShader: no GPU) a lit frame at 1440 × 900 takes 0.2 to 1.5 s of CPU. Drawing frames back to back left the page no time between them: on CI, five trivial page.evaluate calls took 13 to 25 s, so a lit page without reduced motion ran every spec into its 180 s timeout. A player without GPU acceleration had the same sluggish page. The quality levels are unchanged and specs still exercise each one.
- [M4-G fix] A card drag redraws when the pointer moved or the held card's springs still move, not on every animation frame while the button is held — reason: a pointer held still over a card at rest changes nothing on screen, and on a software renderer each of those frames cost a second of the page's time (the drag specs ran close to CI's 180 s).
- [M4-G fix] The face upload budget per frame (scene/faces.ts, 12 MB) grows with the last frame's cost: one budget at 60 fps, up to 8 when a frame takes eight 60 fps frames or longer (scene/pacing.ts uploadScale) — reason: the budget keeps a GPU frame smooth. On a renderer that takes a second a frame anyway, one budget a frame brought a zoom's faces in at a trickle, each batch with a mip-map pass over the whole array: CI's Stonetable 300-card check never settled within 15 s.
- [M4-G fix] An attribution flash is placed over its card at once when it starts, not at the next animation frame — reason: on a slow renderer the next animation frame can come after the 2 s flash is over, so the flash was never shown. The signals spec reads the flash name's colours as the node appears, for the same reason.
- [M4-G fix] The drag specs (render/quality-lift "a held card lifts …", Stonetable "a held card lifts 12 mm …") run at a 960 × 600 viewport; they still run at the high quality level. They read lift, tilt and shadows from the debug hook, not from pixels — reason: a drag draws a frame per pointer move, and on CI's SwiftShader a 1440 × 900 high-level frame took 1.5–3 s, leaving these specs at 2.3–2.7 min of a 3 min budget.
- [M4-G fix] The tick whose spring step brings a card to rest draws that frame (the rest pose, a held card's tilt back to flat) — reason: before, only a step that left the card still moving asked for a frame. A drag used to force a frame on every tick, which hid this; with drags drawing on demand, a held card's last tilt stayed on screen until something else redrew.
- [M4-G] pw-guard counts only on-screen prompt windows of at least 50x50 px — UserNotificationCenter left a 4x4 px empty window on screen for over an hour, which blocked every Playwright run with exit 3 although no dialog was showing.
- [M4-G fix] One stored quality choice (`Settings.quality`) serves every theme; each theme resolves it to its own level with `qualityLevel` (Paper's one level, Dusk's three), so what the choice drops is per theme — reason: DESIGN "a per-theme quality setting" read as the theme's levels; storing a choice per theme would need a new `Settings` key (store/settings.ts accepts only keys of DEFAULT_SETTINGS in store/types.ts, a contract file). The scene keeps the choice across theme hot-swaps (scene/theme-runtime.ts).
- [M4-G fix] The quality select (High / Medium / Low) and the "Follow the stack" / "Follow the active player" checkboxes live in the HUD's theme popover (ui/settings/Theme.svelte), not in the lobby's picker; ui/settings/view.ts `ViewSettings` reads them from the settings store on mount, focus and visibility, writes a change at once and hands the scene only what differs from what it has; a change on the page wins over a read that started before it — reason: these settings act on the table scene, which only the HUD has; it replaces the HUD's follow-only sync.
- [M4-G] Gate green after the main session merged three fix branches (render pacing and Dusk bloom, M4-P1 review, net reload fork) and the settings gap fix; M4-P1 counts as done after its independent review — see docs/gates/M4.md.

## M5 Polish

- [M5 owner] The owner answered the open gate questions on 13 Nov 2026, and M5 builds those answers. They are recorded in docs/PLAN.md, Milestone 5, "Owner decisions for M5":
  - iPad Safari as the tablet;
  - opponents top-left and top-right in three-player games from every seat;
  - spectators listen;
  - Ctrl as the talk key;
  - ego reframed to show your own piles;
  - updates only between games;
  - installable on iPad.
- [M5 main] Where the owner did not answer, the main session took its own recommendations. The audit runs automatically at game end. Missing audit records read as "could not be checked", not as a mismatch. The talk mic opens only after Ctrl has been held alone for about 200 ms, so shortcut chords never send audio. The radial menu holds tap, flip, inspect, +1/+1 counter, to hand, to graveyard, exile, and to stack. — reason: the owner asked to implement M5 in one go after these were explained.
- [M5-C] Audit bundles travel as one public action `auditPublish {bundle}` (family 'meta', not undoable, not open to spectators) that each seated browser proposes once per finished game, not as a separate wire message — reason: a sequenced action reaches every peer in the same log order, is saved with the log and arrives on catch-up, so every peer computes the same verdicts with the pure `verifyAudit` (core/audit.ts), and a peer that reloads still has them. The reducer treats it as a public record until M5-P2 adds its structural checks.
- [M5-C] A published audit record (`PublishedAuditRecord`) carries opaque instance ids, nonces and orders only: `ReissueRecord.retired` (identities) is dropped and draft `PackRecord`s are not published — reason: CLAUDE.md, hidden information leaves its owner's browser only through the design's reveal paths, and the audit needs orders, not identities. M5-P2 adds the scenario to the shared leak test and checks that a later game does not reuse the published ids.
- [M5-C] Audit verdicts are `verified` (with shuffle and draw counts), `mismatch` (a list of `AuditMismatch` with the seq and kind: commit, nonces, order, orderHash, draw, reissue, unrecorded, malformed) or `unverifiable` with reason `recordsMissing` and a count of the records that did agree; a seat with some records missing and none disagreeing is unverifiable, any disagreement makes it a mismatch — reason: PLAN M5 owner decisions reserve "mismatch" for records that exist and disagree.
- [M5-C] A game's span is `AuditGame {game, fromSeq, toSeq}`: from the `stageSet` into 'match' to the action that left it; how an evening that ends mid-match is signalled is M5-P2's — reason: the owner names "Next game" and "the evening ends" as triggers, and only the first has an action today.
- [M5-C] Touch recognition is a pure recogniser fed with pointer samples and their own timestamps (scene/input/touch.ts): tap ≤ 300 ms within 10 px, double-tap within 300 ms and 30 px (reported instead of the second tap), long-press 500 ms, two-finger pitch when both fingers move within 30° of vertical and their distance changes by less than 24 px, else pan/zoom; the mode is chosen once per gesture — reason: DESIGN gives the gestures but no thresholds; these are common platform values and M5-P1 may tune them.
- [M5-C] The radial menu's stack slot runs `cast`, or `ability` where cast does not apply (a permanent on the battlefield); the thumb bar is Attack, Settle, Resolve, Ask and a hold-to-talk button; touch targets are at least 44 CSS px (ui/touch/actions.ts) — reason: owner decision "put on the stack (cast or ability)"; Apple's minimum target size for iPad Safari.
- [M5-C] Keyboard focus moves with the arrow keys (cards in a zone left/right, zones up/down, PageUp/PageDown first/last) handled by ui/a11y, outside the remappable keymap; the focused card counts as hovered for every keymap action and is drawn through `ViewState.focused` — reason: arrow keys follow the WAI-ARIA composite-widget convention and are unused by the keymap; Tab is "next phase"; adding keymap ActionIds would force bindings and scenarios into the M0 keymap tests for keys that act on no card.
- [M5-C] The Ctrl talk key is the bare-modifier chord `{key: 'Control'}` (`CTRL_KEY_CHORD`, `isModifierChord` in games/types.ts) with the 200 ms hold-alone rule as a pure gate (voice/types.ts `HoldAloneGate`, `TALK_HOLD_ALONE_MS`); stored `KeymapOverride`s keep their shape, so an earlier override of talk (e.g. V) stays valid and a letter talk key opens at once as before. MTG_KEYMAP's default stays V until M5-P7 switches it — reason: owner decision (Ctrl, either key; chords stay shortcuts); keeping V in the contracts keeps M0–M4 tests green until M5-P7 changes the default with its tests.
- [M5-C] Spectators get a listen-only voice session through `VoiceHost.listenOnly` (no microphone, key presses ignored, indicator 'listening'); players add their track to spectator connections enabled for open talk only — reason: owner decision "Spectators listen"; team talk already enables the track only on the teammate's connection.
- [M5-C] New settings `cardBack: 'spellplane' | 'official'` (default 'spellplane') and `hintsSeen: HintId[]` (default []), with hint ids welcome, tableKeys, touchGestures, talkKey (M5-P5) and installHomeScreen, persistStorage (M5-P3); `TableRecord.protocol?` records the protocol version a room was played with, absent read as 1 — reason: PLAN M5-P3/M5-P5 and the owner decisions. No stored shape changes incompatibly: missing settings read as defaults and missing `protocol` as 1, so no migration and no database version bump is needed.
- [M5-C] The official back is `https://backs.scryfall.io/<size>/<id[0]>/<id[1]>/<id>.jpg` with Scryfall's standard back id 0aeebaf5-8c7d-4636-9e82-8c27447861f7 (data/back.ts), loaded at runtime only when chosen and handed to the scene through the optional `TableScene.setCardBack` — reason: DESIGN "Card data and images"; the fixtures trim `card_back_id`, so M5-P5 confirms the id against a live card object at the playtest.
- [M5-C] Backups are one JSON file `{format: 'spellplane-backup', version: 1, exportedAt, decks, tables, identity?, settings?}` (store/types.ts BackupFile); a saved table carries its table record, full log and the owner's own secret store, and the identity's key pair as JWK is included whenever tables are — reason: owner decision (export decks and saved tables because Safari may delete storage); without the key pair a restored table cannot reclaim its seat. The file is the owner's own copy and the app never sends it.
- [M5-C] The image cache's LRU order is the Cache API's insertion order: a hit re-inserts its entry and eviction deletes from the front while the cache holds more than `IMAGE_CACHE_LIMITS.maxEntries` (2000) or the origin uses more than half its quota (store/quota.ts) — reason: PLAN M5-P3 asks for LRU eviction; this needs no new IndexedDB store or schema change.
- [M5-C] An update applies only where `updateMayApply` allows: pages without a table and a table in the 'lobby' stage (ui/update.ts); the page posts `spellplane:skip-waiting` to the waiting worker and reloads; the HUD notice reads "New version available: it applies after this game" (util/pwa.ts, with the web app manifest constants) — reason: owner decision "applied at the lobby, never mid-game"; util/ holds the constants because sw.ts, vite.config.ts and the HUD all need them.
- [M5-C] PROTOCOL_VERSION stays 1 in the contracts; M5-P6 bumps it to 2 together with its version check and the tests that hard-code `v: 1`, because `auditPublish` is an action a v1 reducer refuses. `NetEngine.versionMismatch?()` (optional until M5-P6) returns `{mine, theirs, peerId, nickname}`, and `versionAdvice` says 'reloadAfterGame' to the older page and 'waitForTable' to the newer one (net/version.ts) — reason: owner decision (a clear message naming both versions and what to do); changing the version in the contracts would break every M0–M4 test that writes `v: 1` before the check exists.
- [M5-C] The stack collapses to its count badge while a stack card would be drawn shorter than 72 CSS px (`STACK_COLLAPSE_CARD_PX`), and the debug hook gains `stackBadge()`; the default ego view must show the viewer's own pile column and battlefield row at 1440×900 and 1194×834 (`EGO_FRAMING_VIEWPORTS`); M5-P7 may change the values (not the shape) of `DEFAULT_EGO_RIG` and `CAMERA.ego` within DESIGN's limits — reason: owner decisions (far-zoom stack, ego framing); 72 px is about where a title bar's text falls under 7 px.
- [M5-C] No layout contract change for "opponents top-left and top-right from every seat": `TableLayout` already carries every seat's territory and angle, so M5-P7 can either seat three players rotationally (e.g. 0°/120°/240°) or map territories per viewer inside scene/layout* and games/mtg/layout.ts; if it changes three-player geometry it must keep saved three-player tables readable — reason: either approach fits the existing types, and choosing between them is M5-P7's design work.
- [M5-C] The soak harness (tests/sim/soak/harness.ts) measures "bounded memory" on what each engine retains (debug message records, pending, buffered and history counts), not on the process heap — reason: PLAN asks for bounded memory; V8's heap under Vitest is too noisy to bound reliably.
- [M5-P5] One settings panel (ui/settings/Settings.svelte) with the sections Display and sound (the existing theme picker with sound, quality and follow), Voice (the existing voice panel at a table; the stored talk mode and talk button elsewhere), Dictation (the existing speech settings), Keys, Card back, Storage and About; it opens from a ⚙ toolbar button on the table and at `#/settings`, and the ◐ theme popover stays as the shortcut — reason: PLAN M5-P5 asks to consolidate and reuse the existing pieces.
- [M5-P5] Key remapping (ui/settings/keys.ts `KeymapSettings`, shared by the HUD, the panel and the cheat sheet) refuses a chord another action of the same context holds, names that action and offers a swap; it also refuses the untap-offer key and the focus keys (arrows, PageUp, PageDown); the same chord in the other context is allowed; a remap back to an action's only default key drops its override — reason: DESIGN "no chord twice within one context", prompt keys already share keys with table keys (Enter, H), and DECISIONS [M5-C] keeps the arrows for keyboard focus.
- [M5-P5] A bare modifier (Ctrl, Shift or Alt alone) is accepted only for 'talk', captured when the modifier goes down and up with no other key between, so Ctrl+T still captures Ctrl+T; team talk stays a chord — reason: owner decision (Ctrl talk key, remappable); bound to any other action a bare modifier would fire on the first key of every shortcut.
- [M5-P5] The official back is requested only once chosen, at the 'normal' size with crossOrigin 'anonymous', once per page, and drawn whole: scaled to fit with its aspect kept, any margin filled dark; a failed load keeps the neutral back, shows the failed-image state with Try again and keeps the choice, so the next page tries again — reason: DESIGN Card back (opt-in, loaded from backs.scryfall.io) and Scryfall's image rules (never cropped, tinted or distorted).
- [M5-P5] First-run hints show one at a time in the bottom-right corner, take no focus and let pointer events through except on "Got it"; the order is welcome, then tableKeys (or touchGestures on a touch screen), then talkKey at a table with voice; the start page shows only the welcome, a spectator only the welcome; texts name keys from the player's keymap; nothing shows before `hintsSeen` is read, and a dismissal merges with the stored ids — reason: PLAN M5-P5 "short, dismissible, never blocking"; M5-P3's hints share the setting.
- [M5-P5] Empty and error states come from one catalogue (ui/empty/states.ts): no deck = none of the player's cards on the table during the match (inline above the hand, dismissible, with Import a deck); storage full = a save warning that names the quota or an unhandled QuotaExceededError on the table page, and 98 % of the quota in the storage section (90 % warns); failed image = the card's name in its frame (CardFace) or the failed back; no cards found and search unreachable in the token search; no peers and relays unreachable reuse the lobby's existing texts (ui/lobby/connectivity.ts), with no extra banner on the table — reason: PLAN M5-P5 names these states; the lobby already diagnoses connectivity.
- [M5-P5] Two-Headed Giant teams by chair: a room created as Two-Headed Giant already gets its teams at seq 0 (session/net.ts freshNetTable, M3-P2), so M5-P5 adds tests (the fresh table, players sitting down, four sessions on the loopback network) and no code — reason: the M3 gate's note predates M3-P2's fix.
- [M5-P5] New routes `#/settings` and `#/about` (linked from the start page) and the module test page `?harness=settings`; the storage section reads store/quota.ts `storageStatus` (M5-P3) and says "does not report" while it is unavailable — reason: settings and About must be reachable away from a table; the harness makes every state reachable for the acceptance tests.
- [M5-P5] One settings panel (display and sound, voice, dictation, keys, card back, storage, About), opened from a new ⚙ toolbar button on the table and at #/settings; the ◐ theme popover stays as the shortcut — reason: the plan asks to consolidate and reuse the existing pieces
- [M5-P5] Remapping refuses a key another action holds in the same context, names that action and offers a swap; it also refuses the untap-offer key and the arrow and page keys; the same key in the other context is allowed; remapping back to an action's only default removes its override — reason: DESIGN "no chord twice within one context"; prompt keys already share keys with table keys (Enter, H)
- [M5-P5] Ctrl, Shift or Alt alone is accepted only for 'talk', counted when the key goes down and up with nothing in between; team talk stays a chord — reason: owner decision (Ctrl talk key, remappable); on any other action it would fire on the first key of every shortcut
- [M5-P5] The official back is loaded only once chosen, once per page, at the 'normal' size with crossOrigin anonymous, and drawn whole with its proportions kept (any margin filled dark); a failed load keeps the neutral back, shows the failed-image state with Try again, and keeps the choice — reason: DESIGN Card back and Scryfall's image rules
- [M5-P5] Hints: one at a time in the bottom-right corner, no focus, clicks only on "Got it"; order welcome → tableKeys or touchGestures → talkKey; the start page and spectators get only the welcome; nothing shows before hintsSeen is read; a dismissal merges with the stored ids — reason: "short, dismissible, never blocking"; M5-P3's hints share the setting
- [M5-P5] Empty and error states come from one catalogue (ui/empty/states.ts). Storage full = a save warning that names the quota, a QuotaExceededError on the page, or 98 % of the quota used (90 % shows a warning). No deck = none of the player's cards on the table during the match. No peers and relays unreachable reuse the lobby's existing texts — reason: the plan names these states, and the lobby already diagnoses connectivity
- [M5-P5] Two-Headed Giant teams by chair already form at creation (freshNetTable, M3-P2); this package adds tests only — reason: the M3 gate note was written before M3-P2's fix
- [M5-P5] New routes #/settings and #/about, start-page links, and the ?harness=settings test page (one line added to src/main.ts) — reason: settings and About must be reachable away from a table, and every state must be reachable in a harness
- [M5-P5] Shared files touched only on this package's own lines: src/ui/Hud.svelte (settings button and panel, keymap through KeymapSettings, card back, hints, empty states), src/scene/table-scene.ts (setCardBack), src/ui/App.svelte and routes.ts (new routes, start-page hint), src/ui/CardFace.svelte (failed image), src/ui/TokenSearch.svelte (search states), src/ui/fakes.ts (RecordingScene.setCardBack) — reason: card back, remapping UI and empty states are this package's per the M5 ownership note
- [M5-P3] The service worker runs in production builds always, in test builds only with `?sw=on` (the offline and update specs), and never in dev builds — reason: Playwright's request routing does not see requests a worker answers, so every other browser test (Scryfall routes, harness pages) must run without one; the offline acceptance test still runs against the test build.
- [M5-P3] vite-plugin-pwa (a dependency since B0) only builds `src/sw.ts` and injects the build's file list (`self.__WB_MANIFEST`) and the web app manifest from `util/pwa.ts`; the worker has no Workbox runtime and no new dependency was added — reason: the task asks for no heavyweight plugin unless needed; the precache, versioning and update hand-off are about 100 lines of our own.
- [M5-P3] One app-shell cache per build version (`spellplane-shell-<FNV hash of the file list>`), shared by every `?profile=`, filled on install past the HTTP cache; navigations into the app get that version's cached index.html (cache-first), activation deletes older versions' shells — reason: the files are the same for every profile, and a waiting version must not overwrite files (lazy theme chunks) the running version still loads.
- [M5-P3] An update applies by itself as soon as it waits where `updateMayApply` allows (the start page, the diagnostics page, a table in its lobby); mid-game the notice "New version available: it applies after this game" shows (top centre, never taking clicks). A table page whose table is not open yet (stage unknown while joining) does not apply. A page whose update another tab applied keeps running and reloads when it reaches the lobby. The page also asks for a new version hourly — reason: owner decision "applied at the lobby, never mid-game"; reloading at the lobby resumes the table from its save.
- [M5-P3] The storage section (estimate, cached image count, persistence, backup export and import) lives on the start page `#/lobby` as `ui/storage/StoragePanel.svelte`, which M5-P5's consolidated settings can embed — reason: the start page is the one page without a table that every player sees; the HUD's settings are M5-P5's.
- [M5-P3] The app asks for persistent storage at every start (browsers that prompt, like Firefox, ask then; Chrome and Safari decide by themselves); while it is not granted, the start page shows the persistStorage prompt once ("Keep them" asks again on a click, "Not now" dismisses), and the storage section keeps a "Keep data on this device" button — reason: DESIGN "requests persistent storage but never depends on it", PLAN "storage-persistence prompt".
- [M5-P3] The Home Screen hint shows on iPhone and iPad (including iPadOS Safari that reports a Mac with touch), not when running from the Home Screen, on the start page and as a corner card in a table's lobby — never over the table itself — reason: owner decision (a one-time hint explaining Safari's 7-day wipe) without covering play under touch.
- [M5-P3] Image cache LRU: a hit is deleted and put again (moves to the end of the Cache API's order); after each store the cache is trimmed to `imageEntryLimit`: at most 2000 entries, and when the origin uses more than half its quota, the overshoot divided by 100 kB per entry fewer — reason: PLAN asks for LRU eviction within quota; per-entry sizes are not exposed by the Cache API, and 100 kB is a normal-tier image.
- [M5-P3] Scryfall and MTGJSON requests have a deadline covering the body (Scryfall 20 s, MTGJSON set files 60 s, MTGJSON's set list 20 s); a failed connection or timeout throws `UnreachableError` (data/offline.ts) whose message says whether the device is offline and that cards or sets already loaded still work; while `navigator.onLine` is false no request is made or queued; network failures are not retried — reason: PLAN "clear message, cached data still usable, no endless spinners"; the 429 back-off stays as before.
- [M5-P3] The backup UI exports one file with all decks, all saved tables and the settings; restoring takes over the file's identity by default only when this browser has no networked table of its own, verifies that the key pair signs and matches its peer id, never restores a networked table under another identity, and treats the untouched solo table every visit creates as no conflict — reason: owner decision (export and import decks and saved tables); a seat belongs to its key pair.
- [M5-P3] The backup's leak scenario is a unit simulation (tests/unit/store/export-roundtrip.test.ts): B's exported file scanned with the shared scanner against A's hidden cards, then restored into a wiped browser that reclaims the chair with the same hand — reason: the file holds exactly B's own IndexedDB records; the scenario proves it carries nothing of A's.
- [M5-P3] App icons are drawn by `scripts/make-icons.mjs` (Node, zlib, no image library): the neutral card-back motif of the favicon, listed in docs/ASSETS.md with the script as source — reason: own artwork without Wizards marks, reproducible.
- [M5-P3] Unit tests for the worker, update hand-off, hints and the unreachable data clients live in `tests/unit/storage/` beside `tests/unit/store/quota*` and `export*` — reason: they test `src/sw.ts`, `ui/storage/*` and `data/offline.ts`, which the quota and export names do not cover.
- [M5-P3] The service worker runs in production builds always, in test builds only with ?sw=on (plus ?swv=<v> for a second version in the update spec), never in dev — reason: Playwright's request routing does not see requests a worker answers, so other browser tests must run without one
- [M5-P3] vite-plugin-pwa (already a dependency since B0) only builds src/sw.ts and injects the file list and manifest; no Workbox runtime, no new dependency — reason: no heavyweight plugin wanted
- [M5-P3] One app-shell cache per build version (spellplane-shell-<FNV hash of the file list>), shared by every ?profile=; navigations get that version's cached index.html; activation deletes older shells — reason: a waiting version must not overwrite files the running one still loads
- [M5-P3] An update applies by itself where updateMayApply allows (start page, diagnostics, a table in its lobby); a table page whose stage is not known yet does not apply; mid-game a top-centre notice that takes no clicks; a page whose update another tab applied reloads when it reaches the lobby; hourly update check — reason: owner decision 'applied at the lobby, never mid-game'
- [M5-P3] The storage section (estimate, image count, persistence, backup) is ui/storage/StoragePanel.svelte on the start page #/lobby; M5-P5's consolidated settings can embed it — reason: the HUD settings belong to M5-P5
- [M5-P3] Persistent storage is requested at every start; the persistStorage prompt shows once on the start page while it is not granted — reason: DESIGN requests it but never depends on it; PLAN asks for a prompt
- [M5-P3] The Home Screen hint shows on iPhone and iPad (including iPadOS that reports a Mac with touch), never when launched from the Home Screen, on the start page and as a corner card in a table's lobby, never over the table — reason: owner decision without covering touch play
- [M5-P3] Image cache LRU: a hit is deleted and put again; after each store the cache is trimmed to imageEntryLimit (2000, minus the overshoot above half the quota at 100 kB per entry) — reason: the Cache API exposes no per-entry size
- [M5-P3] Data request deadlines cover the response body (Scryfall 20 s, MTGJSON set file 60 s, set list 20 s); UnreachableError messages say offline, timeout or no connection and that cached data still works; nothing is requested while offline; no retries on network failure — reason: PLAN 'clear message, cached data still usable, no endless spinners'
- [M5-P3] Backup restore takes over the file's identity by default only when this browser has no networked table of its own, verifies key pair and peer id, never restores a networked table under another identity, and treats the untouched solo table as no conflict — reason: a seat belongs to its key pair
- [M5-P3] The backup's leak-test scenario is the unit simulation tests/unit/store/export-roundtrip.test.ts (shared scanner over B's file against A's hidden cards) — reason: the file holds exactly B's own IndexedDB records
- [M5-P3] Icons are drawn by scripts/make-icons.mjs and listed in docs/ASSETS.md with that script as source — reason: own artwork, reproducible, satisfies the asset manifest check
- [M5-P3] Unit tests for the service worker, update hand-off, hints and unreachable data clients live in tests/unit/storage/ — reason: they cover src/sw.ts, ui/storage/* and data/offline.ts, which the quota*/export* names do not
- [M5-P3] Implemented in the contract's store/backup.ts (not store/export.ts as the task text said); the task's public/manifest.webmanifest is generated by the build from util/pwa.ts — reason: follows the committed M5-C contracts and PLAN ownership
- [M5-P3] Small edits in shared files: App.svelte (update notice outside rooms), lobby/StartRoute.svelte (hints and storage panel), lobby/TableRoom.svelte and RoomRoute.svelte (notice and lobby hint, update prop), main.ts (worker, fake update, storage debug part, persistence request), index.html (apple-touch-icon and meta tags) — reason: PLAN ownership note for M5-P3
- [M5-P1] A tap replaces the selection as a click does (a card alone; empty table clears it); touch multi-select is long-press then drag on empty table, a marquee that adds to the selection like Shift+drag — reason: touch has no Shift, and a toggling tap would make the next double-tap tap the whole selection.
- [M5-P1] Double-tap does what double-click does (taps a card, draws from the own library, opens a pile; on empty space beside a card zooms to it); the dblclick and contextmenu a browser synthesises within 800 ms of a touch are ignored — reason: DESIGN gives double-tap both "taps the card" and "zoom to a card or pile"; the target decides, as for the mouse.
- [M5-P1] The radial menu opens while the long-pressing finger is down and stays open after it lifts; only a fresh press on the menu runs a slot (so the click Safari sends at the lift picks nothing); a slot that does not apply is shown disabled; its centre (⋯) opens the card's full context menu; a press outside or Esc closes it. A long-press on empty table opens the table menu when the finger lifts, unless the finger drags on (a marquee) — reason: eight slots cannot hold every action and the touch-only gate needs untap all, tidy, dice, tokens, transform, notes and library actions.
- [M5-P1] Once a one-finger drag holds a card, a stack marker, a line or a marquee (`TouchRecognizer.capture`), further fingers are ignored until it ends; a second finger before the drag starts, during a long-press or during a pan starts a two-finger camera gesture instead — reason: "nothing ever moves the camera while the player is dragging" and "two-finger gestures always drive the camera" meet here; what the finger already holds wins.
- [M5-P1] The two-finger mode is chosen once both fingers moved half the slop (or one moved three slops): pitch when both move within 30° of vertical the same way and their distance changes by under 24 px, else pan and zoom; pitch turns 0.2° per px like right-drag (fingers up look less steeply down); in bird's-eye a pitch-shaped drag pans — reason: pointer events report each finger on its own, so the first sample alone would read every vertical drag as a pinch; DESIGN: pitch is ego only.
- [M5-P1] The thumb bar and the scrolling hand fan show on touch devices (`any-pointer: coarse` or touch points, which iPadOS Safari reports while presenting as a Mac) and on any device once a finger touched the page; spectators get no thumb bar. The bar sits 8 px above the hand; the prompts above the hand and the table fit's bottom inset move up by 64 px while it shows — reason: DESIGN "tablets with touch are fully supported"; no setting needed; the fitted table stays clear of the bar.
- [M5-P1] Thumb bar buttons run their keymap actions through Commands (Attack and Settle act on the selection, Resolve on the top item, Ask opens the Ask field as K does, with an Ask button to send it without a keyboard; Talk talks while held, wherever the finger then lifts) — reason: DESIGN, every action has the same effect on every route.
- [M5-P1] Hand fan on touch: a finger lifts the card it touches and it stays lifted (for reading) until a press elsewhere; a first move sideways scrolls the fan and moves the lift to the card under the finger; a first move upward plays the card once it leaves the fan; a long-press opens the radial menu. Every card keeps at least 44 px uncovered: when the 80 % fan would overlap more, the fan spans the screen width less 8 px and scrolls — reason: DESIGN "scrolls horizontally and lifts cards on touch for reading"; Apple's 44 pt minimum target.
- [M5-P1] iPad Safari tuning: the page has `touch-action: manipulation` (no double-tap zoom) and `overscroll-behavior: none` (no pull-to-refresh) in index.html; the table HUD has `user-select: none` and `-webkit-touch-callout: none` (no callout or magnifier; text fields keep selection) and cancels Safari's `gesturestart` family while it is mounted (no page pinch at the table); the HUD layer and the hand fan sit inside `env(safe-area-inset-*)`, and the radial menu keeps clear of them; context-menu entries are 44 px tall on coarse pointers — reason: owner decisions; outside the table the page keeps pinch zoom for accessibility.
- [M5-P1] Touch e2e specs run in WebKit under `devices['iPad Pro 11 landscape']`: single taps through `page.touchscreen.tap`, held, moving and multi-finger gestures as touch pointer events dispatched in the page to the element under the finger at its press (implicit capture, as in Safari) — reason: Playwright's touchscreen only taps and multi-touch input needs CDP (Chromium only).
- [M5-P1] Presence of a finger (scene/presence.ts, an M1-P5 file, edited here because touch input is this package's): a touch press shows a drag to the others only while the scene reports cards it drags (none for a line, a marquee, a pan or a two-finger gesture), with the cards hanging off where the finger is when the scene picks them up; a second finger ends a press the scene is not dragging, and moves of other fingers are not followed; the tracker takes the pointer id and type as optional arguments, so mouse and pen are unchanged — reason: DESIGN "Other players' cursors" shows a dragged card so intent is visible; a ghost card sliding along a touch line would show an intent that never lands.
