- [M5-P1] A second finger that turns a touch into a camera gesture closes the radial menu the long-press opened and drops the table menu a long-press on empty table would open, even when the fingers then lift without moving (a two-finger tap) — reason: DESIGN "two-finger gestures always drive the camera"; a menu left over a card the camera moved points at nothing.
- [M5-P1] Safe-area insets: the scrolling hand fan is laid out between the left and right insets, its drag-out line sits above the bottom inset, the fitted table's insets grow by the safe-area inset of each edge (`fitInsets`' new `safe` argument), and the context menu is clamped inside them (ui/touch/fan.ts `clampMenu`) — reason: owner decisions, safe-area insets respected.
- [M5-P1] Out-of-ownership edits for the integrator: `TouchRecognizer.capture?()` (optional, additive) on the M5-C shape in scene/input/touch.ts; ui/ContextMenu.svelte (44 px entries on coarse pointers, safe-area clamp); ui/hand.ts `fitInsets` (optional `extraBottom` and `safe` arguments); scene/presence.ts (optional pointer argument). Touch taps reach the scene through `onTouchGesture`'s 'tap' case (it calls `clickAt`), not the mouse press path: a tap behaviour another package adds to the press path (M5-P7's far-zoom stack badge expanding on tap) must go through `clickAt` or that case too — reason: integration notes from review.
- [M5-P1] A tap replaces the selection as a click does; multi-select by touch is long-press then drag on empty table (a marquee that adds to the selection) — touch has no Shift, and a toggling tap would make the next double-tap tap the whole selection
- [M5-P1] Double-tap does what double-click does (taps a card, draws from the library, opens a pile; beside a card it zooms to it); browser-synthesised dblclick/contextmenu within 800 ms of a touch are ignored — the target decides, as for the mouse
- [M5-P1] The radial menu opens while the finger is down and stays open after it lifts; only a fresh press runs a slot; a slot that does not apply is disabled; its centre (⋯) opens the full context menu; long-press on empty table opens the table menu on release — eight slots cannot hold every action the touch-only gate needs
- [M5-P1] Once a one-finger drag holds something, further fingers are ignored (capture); a second finger before that, or during a pan or long-press, starts a two-finger camera gesture — reconciles 'nothing moves the camera while dragging' with 'two-finger gestures always drive the camera'
- [M5-P1] The two-finger mode is chosen once both fingers have moved (pointer events report each finger separately); pitch uses 0.2°/px like right-drag; in bird's-eye a pitch-shaped drag pans — DESIGN: pitch is ego only
- [M5-P1] The thumb bar and the scrolling fan show on touch devices (any-pointer: coarse or maxTouchPoints > 0) or once a finger touches the page; not for spectators; prompts and the table fit move up 64 px while the bar shows — no setting needed
- [M5-P1] Ask on the thumb bar opens the Ask field as K does, plus an Ask button to send it without a keyboard; Talk acts while the button is held — one action per route, as Commands defines it
- [M5-P1] On touch, every hand card keeps 44 px uncovered; when the 80% fan would overlap more, the fan spans the screen width less 8 px and scrolls; a lifted card stays lifted until a press elsewhere — Apple's 44 pt target, DESIGN 'scrolls horizontally and lifts cards'
- [M5-P1] iPad Safari: touch-action manipulation and overscroll-behavior none on the page; user-select none and -webkit-touch-callout none on the table HUD (text fields excepted); gesturestart cancelled while the table shows; HUD layer and fan inside env(safe-area-inset-*) — owner decision; pages outside the table keep pinch zoom
- [M5-P1] Touch e2e runs in WebKit under devices['iPad Pro 11 landscape'] with page.touchscreen.tap for single taps and in-page touch pointer events for held and multi-finger gestures — Playwright has no multi-touch API without CDP
- [M5-P1] A finger's presence: a touch press shows a drag to the others only while the scene reports cards it drags (none for a line, a marquee, a pan or a two-finger gesture), and a second finger ends any press the scene is not dragging — reason: DESIGN 'Other players' cursors' shows a dragged card so intent is visible; a ghost sliding along a touch line shows an intent that never lands. presence.ts belongs to M1-P5 but was edited here, additively (optional pointer argument).
- [M5-P1] A second finger that turns a touch into a camera gesture closes the radial menu and drops a pending table menu, even for a two-finger tap without movement — reason: DESIGN, two-finger gestures always drive the camera.
- [M5-P1] Safe-area insets are respected by the scrolling fan (side insets), the fan's drag-out line (bottom inset), the fitted table (each edge's inset is added through fitInsets' new optional safe argument) and the context menu (clampMenu) — reason: PLAN M5 owner decisions.
- [M5-P1] For the integrator: the files outside ownership touched are scene/input/touch.ts (optional capture), ui/ContextMenu.svelte, ui/hand.ts and scene/presence.ts; touch taps go through onTouchGesture → clickAt, not the mouse press path — reason: so the merges with other M5 packages (M5-P7's stack badge) are clean.
- [M5-P7] Three players sit round the table at 0°, 120° and 240° (seat indices still clockwise: seat 1 top-left, seat 2 top-right as seat 0 sees them), each territory 1100 × 440 with its centre 600 from the table centre, on a 2000 × 1800 table; this replaces DESIGN's spec row "Three players | 2000 × 1300 | 0°, 180°, 180° | seat 0 full width, seats 1 and 2 share the far edge" — reason: owner decision "opponents top-left and top-right from every seat, every viewer sees the same shape"; a viewer's frame is a rigid turn of the canonical one (state hashes stay viewer-independent), so the same shape from every seat needs a threefold turn; 1100 matches the four-player territories, 600 keeps a gap between neighbours and a free centre for the 200 × 320 lane, 2000 × 1800 holds every territory and keeps Dusk's lamp cone over the corners. Saved three-player tables load unchanged: battlefield cards keep their stored poses (all on the new table), piles move to the new anchors.
- [M5-P7] The camera rigs see the table as an oriented rectangle (scene/camera/table.ts); every right-angled format passes the layout's table unchanged, the three-player table hands each viewer the same 2000 × 1800 rectangle turned to their seat (scene/layout.ts cameraTable), and the fitted frame is taken in the viewer's frame (FrameBox.rot) — reason: with a canonical rectangle seats 1 and 2 would get a different fit, a different ego seat (their projected "table edge" lies 500 mm further back) and other reach; the M4-P1 cursor-point guarantees hold for points on that camera table (the property tests run on every seat). The themes still draw their table surface as the canonical rectangle, so seats 1 and 2 see its edges at a slant; a round or hexagonal surface would need a theme contract change (left for the owner playtest).
- [M5-P7] The ego seat is pulled back to 900 mm behind the edge and raised to 860 mm (CAMERA.ego; DEFAULT_EGO_RIG.maxDollyMm 700 → 1200 so the closest dolly still reaches about 170 mm above the table), keeping the spec's 45° field of view, 35° pitch and 25°..60° limits — reason: owner decision "ego framing shows your own piles" at 1440 × 900 and 1194 × 834; the widest own row (1v1, 1400 mm) needs the near corners about 1200 mm from the eye at 45°, and with the near edge just above the hand fan (22% of the height) and the far own edge below the 74 px toolbar this is the closest seat that holds for every format and seat (tests/unit/camera/framing.test.ts). DESIGN's "260 behind, 620 above" is superseded by these values.
- [M5-P7] The talk key is the bare Control key (either), read by ui/voice/input.ts under the hold-alone rule, not by the KeyEngine (a Control keydown reports ctrlKey, so it matches no chord there): after 200 ms alone it talks; another key (Shift and Alt included), a pointer press or a wheel turn first makes it a shortcut until released; Ctrl pressed while a pointer is already down (a Ctrl-drag that skips snapping) or together with Alt or Meta is a shortcut at once; once talking, a later key does not close the mic; team talk is Shift held before Ctrl; a remapped letter key goes through the KeyEngine and opens at once, a remapped bare modifier (Shift, Alt) gets the same rule; labels read "Ctrl" and "Shift+Ctrl" — reason: owner decision (chords stay shortcuts; Ctrl+click; the drag modifier of DESIGN's drop resolution must not open the mic).
- [M5-P7] Spectators get the listen-only controller (VoiceHost.listenOnly) from openSpectatorSession: it never calls getUserMedia or addStream, ignores keys, shows "Listening" with no join or mute button and a panel of per-player volume only; on players' pages a spectator's connection (PeerInfo.role 'spectator') is enabled for audio 'all' only and never for team talk, even when its key pair names a teammate's chair — reason: owner decision "Spectators listen … team-only talk never reaches them"; a disabled track sends silence, as for any player who is not the teammate.
- [M5-P7] STACK_COLLAPSE_CARD_PX is tuned from 72 to 36 CSS px: the lane collapses when its bottom slot's card would be drawn shorter than that (the fitted three- and four-player views, about 25 px), while the fitted 1v1 and Two-Headed Giant views (40–44 px) keep the lane the M1–M4 gates draw lines from; the badge is a DOM element over the canvas at the lane's centre showing the count; hover (mouse, pen), focus or a click/tap pins it open; the expanded list shows each item's number, kind, controller colour, note and whole card face at 168 px (a face the viewer may not see shows a plain back); a pointer press elsewhere or Escape closes it; the collapsed lane's cards are neither drawn nor picked — reason: owner decision "far-zoom stack" (M3 gate finding) without breaking the stack interactions the earlier gates rely on.
- [M5-P7] At the three-player table what is drawn, where drops land and what is fitted are the same for every viewer: the theme is built as for a viewer at seat 0's angle and its objects turned about the table centre by the viewer's camera-table turn (ThemePlacement.tableRot, scene/theme-runtime.ts; the light rig and key light stay at the real seat, which is where the turned theme expects them), the table outline among the markings is turned likewise, and free drops are clamped to the intersection of the three turned 2000 × 1800 tables (scene/layout.ts dropTable, scene/snap.ts DropTable.turns), a twelve-sided area that holds every territory, pile and command area — reason: review round 1 found that with a canonical surface and clamp, seats 1 and 2's fitted view hid two corners of the drawn table (under the toolbar and the hand fan) where seat 0 could still drop cards; this supersedes the earlier line's "the themes still draw their table surface as the canonical rectangle". Cards of a three-player table saved before M5 that lie outside the shared area (the old far corners) are drawn where they are and can be panned to; moving one clamps it in.
- [M5-P7] Beyond its listed ownership, M5-P7 edits the M4-P1 camera code for the turned three-player camera table (scene/camera/birdseye.ts FrameBox.rot, clampView, onTable, setTable; scene/camera/rig.ts depthRange, zoomAt, the switch landing; new scene/camera/table.ts), scene/theme-runtime.ts (tableRot), scene/snap.ts (DropTable), scene/lines.ts (DebugSeatView.zones), scene/harness.ts (&rig=ego, stackHover()), ui/lobby/Lobby.svelte (the three-player lobby drawing) and ui/Hud.svelte (the talk key wiring) — reason: the owner decision "every viewer sees the same shape" needs the camera, theme and drop clamp to share the turned table; listed so the merge with M5-P1's two-finger camera gestures (same rig code) is checked.
- [M5-P7] The modifier talk key reads both bindings: talk (a bare modifier) and team talk (Shift with a bare modifier, default Shift+Ctrl); Shift held before the team binding's modifier is team talk whatever talk is bound to, Shift held before the talk modifier when team talk is bound elsewhere makes that press a chord (a shortcut); the HUD and the unit tests share one wiring (attachModifierTalkActions → the HUD's talk actions → runTalkAction), and attachTalkInput no longer has its own modifier path; a pointer whose pointerup never reached the window is dropped on its next move with no button down; the hold-alone state shows as the talk indicator's data-hold — reason: review round 1 (a remapped team talk binding had no effect; a stale pointer turned every later Ctrl hold into a shortcut; the unit tests covered a path the app did not run; the muted gate step could pass before the hold counted).
- [M5-P7] The far-zoom badge also expands while keyboard focus is inside it, Escape closes it wherever the focus is (WebKit does not focus a tapped button), a transparent strip bridges the 10 px gap to the list so a mouse can reach the items, and hovering an item of the list makes it the scene's hovered stack item (source and targets highlighted; the HUD's N, X and I reach it) — reason: review round 1; dragging items out of the list, reordering there and starting target lines from a collapsed lane are left for the owner's gate review (zoom in, or use R and Shift+R).
- [M5-P7] Three players sit at 0°/120°/240°, territories 1100×440 with centres 600 mm out, on a 2000×1800 table (2100 square broke Dusk's lamp-cone test). This replaces DESIGN's spec row. A viewer's view is a rigid turn of the shared frame, so the same shape from every seat needs threefold symmetry, and state hashes stay independent of the viewer. Saved three-player tables still load: battlefield cards keep their stored poses, which all lie on the new table.
- [M5-P7] The camera rigs treat the table as a rectangle that may be turned (scene/camera/table.ts). Formats with right-angle seats pass the layout's table unchanged; at the three-player table every viewer's camera gets the same 2000×1800 rectangle turned to their seat, and the fit frame is measured in the viewer's own frame. Otherwise seats 1 and 2 would get a different fit and an ego seat 500 mm further back. The M4-P1 cursor-point property tests now cover every seat. The themes still draw the table surface as the unturned rectangle, so seats 1 and 2 see its edges at a slant. A round or hexagonal surface would need a theme contract change; left for the owner playtest.
- [M5-P7] The ego seat is 900 mm behind the edge and 860 mm up; field of view 45°, pitch 35° and the 25–60° limits are kept; maximum dolly 700 → 1200 mm. This is the closest seat that keeps every format's own territory, pile column and command area inside the viewport, between the 74 px toolbar and the hand fan, at 1440×900 and 1194×834.
- [M5-P7] The talk key is the bare Ctrl key, handled by a hold-alone attachment in ui/voice/input.ts rather than the KeyEngine (a Ctrl keydown never matches a chord there). After 200 ms held alone it talks. Any other key, Shift included, a pointer press or a wheel turn first makes it a shortcut. Ctrl pressed while a pointer is already down, or with Alt or Meta, is a shortcut at once. Once talking, a later key does not close the mic. Team talk is Shift held before Ctrl. A key remapped to a letter goes through the KeyEngine as before.
- [M5-P7] Spectators get the listen-only controller from openSpectatorSession: no microphone, no outgoing stream, keys ignored, 'Listening' with no join or mute button. On players' pages a spectator's connection is enabled for audio to everyone only, never team talk, even when its key pair names a teammate's seat.
- [M5-P7] STACK_COLLAPSE_CARD_PX tuned from 72 to 36 px. The fitted three- and four-player views (about 25 px stack cards) collapse to the badge; the fitted 1v1 and Two-Headed Giant views (40–44 px), where the M1–M4 gates draw lines from stack items, keep the lane. The badge is a DOM element showing the count; hover or focus expands it, a click or tap pins it open, and a press elsewhere or Escape closes it. The expanded list shows each item's number, kind, colour, note and a whole 168 px card face (a face the viewer may not see shows a plain back). Cards of a collapsed lane are neither drawn nor picked.
- [M5-P7] Test seams beyond my own files: `&rig=ego` in the scene harness, `&watcher=1` in the voice harness, a `zones` field on DebugSeatView (tableView debug part) with zone corners on screen, and the lobby map draws turned territories. Existing e2e, gate and unit tests now use Ctrl instead of V and the new seat values where the owner decisions change them (camera blend, ego, rigs, layout, contracts, captions, signals, M3 and M4 gates).
- [M5-P7] Three players: the theme is turned with each viewer's camera table (ThemePlacement.tableRot) and so is the table outline; free drops are clamped to the intersection of the three turned 2000x1800 tables (dropTable / DropTable.turns). Reason: what is drawn, where drops land and what is fitted must be the same for every viewer, and no seat may have a blind corner. This supersedes the earlier 'themes still draw the canonical rectangle'.
- [M5-P7] Files edited outside the listed ownership: scene/camera/birdseye.ts, rig.ts, table.ts (new), scene/theme-runtime.ts, scene/snap.ts, scene/lines.ts, scene/harness.ts, ui/lobby/Lobby.svelte, ui/Hud.svelte. Reason: the turned three-player table and the talk key wiring; check the merge with M5-P1's camera gestures.
- [M5-P7] The modifier talk key reads both the talk and the teamTalk bindings, and the HUD and the unit tests share one wiring (attachModifierTalkActions with runTalkAction); stale pointers are dropped on a move with no button down; the talk indicator shows the hold state as data-hold. Reason: review round 1.
- [M5-P7] The far-zoom badge expands on keyboard focus, closes with Escape from anywhere, bridges the gap to its list, and a hovered list item becomes the scene's hovered stack item. Dragging out of the list and target lines from a collapsed lane are left for the gate review. Reason: review round 1.
- [M5-P6] PROTOCOL_VERSION is 2; the Trystero app id stays 'spellplane-v1' so pages of different versions still meet and show the notice instead of never finding each other. The transport's `Message.v` is a number (was the literal 1) — reason: a fixed literal would make every version change a type change in net/transport.ts, and pages must read a foreign `v` to name it.
- [M5-P6] The version notice names the other player, both versions and the advice: the older page "reload it after the game (a newer Spellplane is published)", the newer page "wait for the table to update — <name> reloads Spellplane after the game"; a version this page cannot read counts as older. It goes in the log once per connection and in a banner over every stage of a networked table (ui/version/VersionNotice.svelte, mounted by ui/lobby/TableRoom.svelte, dismissible per mismatch); the engine forgets a peer's mismatch when its connection goes — reason: owner decision (both versions and what to do, never silent); a stale banner for a page that has gone or reloaded into this version would mislead.
- [M5-P6] A browser that opened a link (not the creator) and finds only pages of another version in the room after its wait is refused with the notice ("Could not join this table: Version mismatch: …") instead of sitting alone at a table nobody can join; the creator, and a returning player with a chair, sit down and show the banner — reason: owner decision "never a silent failure"; a table started alone in an occupied room is the silent failure.
- [M5-P6] The table record stores `protocol: PROTOCOL_VERSION` on every write (session/persist.ts); reopening a room whose record names a newer version adds a log warning naming both versions and saying to reload; an older or missing version continues silently (protocol 2 only adds an action, so a version-1 log replays) — reason: owner decision "a room records its protocol version"; the record is the only place a page can learn that a newer page played the room.
- [M5-P6] Soak: four engines, 5,000 sequenced actions past the common table under delay ≤250 ms, 3% drop, reordering and a non-sequencer peer killed and revived every 600 actions; "bounded memory" is every structure Engine.retained reports staying under ENGINE_RETAIN_LIMITS (none depends on the number of actions; the debug message ring is capped at 200,000 and empty in production), queues empty once quiet, every structure but the remembered ids whose caps lie above the soak (cids, own cids, nacks: at most one more per sequenced action) and the debug ring no larger at the end than half-way (small slack for a peer away at the probe and the filling hash windows), and the state's bytes per table object steady (random play leaves tokens, so the state itself grows with the table) — reason: PLAN acceptance; decision M5-C (retained counts, not the heap).
- [M5-P6] Solo actions survive a reload after a disconnect: an engine started from a save counts every stored action whose `by` is the seat its identity holds (or a seatTake for it) as its own, except the sequencer's records of its own view (away, sequencerClaim, commitClose, draftTimeout, a respond timeout), so an abandoned part is proposed again as without a reload. Ownership comes from the log and the seat, not a new stored field — reason: only the browser holding a seat's key pair can propose for it (the sequencer checks `by`), so the seat in the stored log already says whose action it was; no contract change.
- [M5-P6] The pass-draft driver keeps every handed-on copy of a pack while the draft runs (at the end of the draft only the last visit's copy, as before) and moves a copy back to the held packs when the confirmed state no longer holds its pass (the pack is back with this drafter or before it); the pick is not resolved twice, and the pass the engine proposes again hands on the same cards. A copy of a pack the confirmed state does not know (its `packOpen` was abandoned too) is kept until the draft ends; when the room refuses the pick proposed again (another pack is ahead in this drafter's queue there), the driver proposes it again once the pack is at the front; and a remembered unopened pack is offered again after a fork rather than a new one generated (each remembered pack once per driver), so the pack the engine proposes again stays openable. A pick of an abandoned part that the room's history never takes again is not undone (the engine proposes it again) — reason: M2 gate follow-up; the stall came from dropping the copy, and own actions of an abandoned history are proposed again by design.
- [M5-P6] Undo of an action another player already built on is refused with "Cannot undo: <name> has acted on <card|the turn|a line|the stack> since", and the refused step leaves the stack, so the next Ctrl+Z goes on with the player's older actions (hardening round 1: a step that stayed made every later Ctrl+Z refuse until a barrier). A stretch this page received only as a catch-up from a snapshot or a rewound history counts as one change of everything it changed, at its first seq, by the first other seat among its reported actions, or "another player" when a snapshot skipped them unseen; a rewound stretch of own actions only counts for nobody. Built on means: another seat's action sequenced after it changed a card record, line (or a card a line is anchored on), stack item or the turn marker that the undone action changed (core/undo.ts changedObjects, builtOn); life, player counters and zone order are deltas that compose and never block; own later actions are undone first anyway. A gesture built on in part is refused whole — reason: DESIGN "personal undo … no table-wide rewind"; undoing it would silently take the other player's change back too.
- [M5-P6] Undo after a reload rebuilds the stack from the saved log since the stored snapshot (at most 200 actions back; older steps are inside the snapshot and past undoing): own undoable actions with their inverse planned on the confirmed state before each, minus steps already undone, cleared at an own Ready, shuffleCommit, mulligan or recovery, and on the solo table at a whole-zone private reorder (its shuffle; in a networked log that shape is a scry or reorder of a whole small zone, which the live session keeps its stack for), with the mulligan's draws (one group) and the recovery's draws (however many groups, until the agreed hand count; the stack is cleared again then, as the live session does) left off and steps naming ids a re-issue retired dropped; a draw goes back on top of the library, another card returns to the hand's end (the hand's earlier order is not in the log). Net and solo sessions — reason: DESIGN Ctrl+Z reverts the player's own most recent actions; a reload is invisible to the player otherwise.
- [M5-P6] TextureLru keeps each tier's evictable textures in draw order (a draw moves one to the end) and thumbnails apart, so finding the least recently drawn texture looks at one entry per tier instead of the whole cache; the face budget headroom (16 MB) moves to scene/textures.ts (FACE_BUDGET_HEADROOM) and atlas.ts exports layerBytesOf for the budget checks; a model of FaceTextures under an ego-rig sweep (300 cards, a near row at the large tier) checks the cap and that the near row fits — reason: PLAN M5-P6 performance pass on texture eviction; no timing assertions (CI).
- [M5-P6] The version banner's Hide holds for the same version of the same peer; a mismatch first known from a `bye` (no peer yet) stays hidden once the peer's hello names it (ui/version/dismiss.ts); the banner reads the engine by duck typing because TableSession has no version API — reason: hardening round 1 (a hidden banner came back when the hello followed the bye); a typed `versionMismatch()` on the session interface needs a contracts change.
- [M5-P6] `Message.v` in net/transport.ts (an M0-C contract file) is a number, changed outside a contracts package as part of the PROTOCOL_VERSION bump PLAN gives M5-P6 — reason: hardening round 1 asks the integrator to acknowledge it in the gate report.
- [M5-P6] Undo after a reload: a mulligan's draws are owed across every own action sequenced while its shuffle settles (a nonce for another seat's shuffle, a response, chat, even a tap), and the first draw after the mulligan clears the stack, because the live networked session clears its stack after those draws (session/net.ts mulligan) and so drops whatever was made in the wait too; if the mulligan never drew (its shuffle failed, an empty library), the player's next draw is taken as its draw and clears the stack — one undo step less than live, never one more. A solo shuffle of a one-card or empty zone is a barrier too — reason: hardening round 2 (Ctrl+Z after a reload took back the mulligan hand when an own shuffleNonce fell between the mulligan and its draws); DESIGN: a reload must not change what Ctrl+Z reverts.
- [M5-P6] A networked undo step the dry run refuses ("Cannot undo: <reason>", e.g. an own stack item no longer on top because another player pushed one) leaves the stack like a built-on step, so later Ctrl+Z goes on with older own actions — reason: hardening round 2; a step that stayed made every later Ctrl+Z refuse until a barrier.
- [M5-P6] Steps a hand re-issue retires are found by whole id values anywhere in the inverse (core/undo.ts namesAny, used by undoStackFromLog and NetTable's re-issue), not by substring, so retiring `c1` keeps a step naming `c12` — reason: hardening round 2.
- [M5-P6] The table record stores the highest protocol version the room was played with (max of the loaded record's and this page's; session/persist.ts), so an older page saving a room a newer page wrote keeps the version notice coming at every reload — reason: PLAN M5 owner decisions, Version mismatch: never a silent failure.
- [M5-P6] Kept as is for the owner playtest and the gate report: another seat's line anchored on a card counts as a change of that card, so it blocks undo of the player's earlier actions on that card even where the inverse would compose (conservative: never silently takes their line's meaning back); built-on history keeps the last 2000 other-seat changes (KEEP_OTHERS), older own steps fall back on the restore patch's compare-and-set; after a reload an undone card goes back to the end of the private hand (the hand's earlier order is not in the log and is not persisted per step); handed-on pack copies stay in the own secret store for the whole draft — reason: hardening round 2 minor findings judged design interpretation, none moves hidden information or splits peers.
- [M5-P6] Solo actions survive a reload also when the solo stretch crosses a periodic snapshot: the networked save holds its every-200 snapshot while no other peer is connected (session/persist.ts `holdSnapshot`, `aloneInRoom`), so the stored tail after the snapshot covers the whole stretch played alone and the reloaded engine finds every own action of it; the held snapshot is made at the first write with a peer connected again, or once the stored tail reaches 600 actions (SNAPSHOT_HOLD_LIMIT, as far back as a replica can rewind). An engine started from a stored tail treats its restored snapshot as unverified until the sequencer's history agrees after it: a fork at the first seq after it loads the sequencer's snapshot at once ("history diverged at or before seq N") instead of rewinding onto a state that may itself be forked and resyncing later on a hash mismatch reported as a reducer bug. Known limit: an original sequencer that sequences its own actions in the seconds before it notices it is cut off (AWAY_AFTER_MS) still counts as connected then; if a periodic snapshot falls in that window and the page is reloaded, the solo actions inside the snapshot are lost (those after it are proposed again) — reason: review M5-P6 round 3 (blocking: stretch inside the stored snapshot lost `alone-1`); ResumePoint carries no pre-snapshot actions and is a contract (net/types.ts), so the save keeps them after the snapshot instead.
- [M5-P6] A stored action counts as this identity's own after a reload only while the identity held its seat, followed through the stored tail from the snapshot's seating (its own seatTake, a seatLeave of its chair); a previous holder's actions of a chair it took over are not proposed again by it (net/engine.ts ownOfTail) — reason: review M5-P6 round 3.
- [M5-P6] The solo session shows the room version notice too when its save names a newer protocol (createSoloSession, as openNetSession does); `NetEngine.versionMismatch()` names the peer that most recently came to differ, and a retried hello or bye of an earlier one no longer moves it to the front, so with two peers on other versions the banner does not switch between them; the banner reads the engine through a NetSession type guard (`'engine' in session`), no cast — reason: review M5-P6 round 3 minor findings; the contract says "the latest mismatch".
- [M5-P6] After a reload, undoing an own action that took a card from inside or the bottom of the own library puts it back on top (core/undo.ts `opposite` places a card returned to a hidden zone at 'top'; the private library order before each step is neither in the log nor stored per step), where before a reload it goes back to its old index — reason: review M5-P6 round 3 (minor, not a leak and no cross-peer effect); storing libraryBefore per undo step with the secrets would change the SecretStore contract.
- [M5-P2] The audit's game span stays M5-C's (`stageSet` into 'match' to the `stageSet` out of it); a game ends on any stage change out of the match ("Next game" to the builder, "New draft" or the end of the evening to the lobby). An evening whose players simply close their pages has not ended its game (DESIGN: a game can be continued days later), so nothing is published then — reason: publishing nonces while the game may still resume would show the remaining library orders.
- [M5-P2] Every seated browser proposes `auditPublish` for every finished game in which its secret store holds an identity of a card its seat committed to, as soon as the game's end is sequenced (also later, after a reload or a reconnect), at most three refusals per game; the reducer refuses a bundle while its game is being played, for another seat, from an empty chair, for a span that has not ended, and any field beyond those of the published shapes (so no `CardRef`, salt or retired identity can ride along) — reason: owner decision (automatic at game end; never mid-game), CLAUDE.md hidden-information rule.
- [M5-P2] Verdicts: the first sequenced bundle of a seat per game counts. Records are matched to the public log by shuffle id and, for reorders, by zone and order hash (else the next unused one of that zone). A stacked shuffle is one 'order' finding; draws are then checked against the order the public hash committed to, not the seed's, so one cheat is not reported once per draw. A private reorder of `count` cards may move at most `count` cards (the length minus the longest run that kept its relative order), so "put on top" after a search and a scry to the bottom pass, a stack does not — reason: DESIGN gives the records, not how strictly a reorder is bounded; `reorderOne` publishes count 1 for a card from anywhere in the library.
- [M5-P2] The audit follows a library's order from a shuffle or reorder through draws, cards leaving, and single cards its owner put on top or at the bottom (`toZone`, the undo of a draw); after a mulligan, another seat's move into the library or any other unplaced arrival, and before the first shuffle of the match, nobody committed to the order, so draws are not checked until the next shuffle or reorder (no mismatch, no "could not be checked"; the verdict's draw count shows what was checked). A shuffle made before the match started (not possible from the HUD) is outside the span and its draws are unchecked — reason: a mismatch must mean records that exist and disagree (owner decision).
- [M5-P2] A seat without a bundle is "waiting for records" in the panel until every player published, 15 s (`AUDIT_WAIT_MS`, this browser's clock) passed since the game ended, or the next game started; then it reads "could not be checked: records missing", and a bundle that arrives later (the player came back with the save) replaces that with its verdict and a new log line. A bundle that says `incomplete` (records lost with the storage) is checked as far as it goes: missing records are unverifiable, disagreeing ones still a mismatch — reason: owner decision (missing records neutral, never a mismatch).
- [M5-P2] Verdict lines are system lines of each browser's own timeline ("Audit of game 1: Ben — verified: 1 shuffle and 7 draws checked"; a mismatch is a warning naming the first finding), placed after the seat's `auditPublish` line; `auditPublish` itself reads "publishes the audit records of game N (k records)". A reload rebuilds the ledger by replaying the saved log from the room's seq-0 table and keeps it only when the replay ends on the confirmed state (a log with a gap audits later games only) — reason: verdicts are derived from the shared log, so they need not be stored.
- [M5-P2] The audit panel lives in the stage view (ui/flow/Stage.svelte), so it shows in the builder or lobby that follows a game; it opens by itself when a game ends, not again after a reload, closes to an "Audit" button, and lists earlier games of the evening. Spectators and the solo table have no audit (no seat, nothing to publish) — reason: PLAN asks for a per-player verdict on the players' pages; the gate verifier reads verdicts through the debug hook's `audit` part (registered by the networked session).
- [M5-P2] Pack seeds are not published: draft pack rounds (purpose 'pack') belong to the draft, not the game, and checking them needs the packs' card identities, which the M5-C decision keeps out of bundles — reason: "pack seeds where relevant": none is relevant to a game's shuffles and draws.
- [M5-P2] (audit round 1) A browser proposes its bundle only once every other player of the game who still holds a chair has acted in its history after the latest time the sequencer marked them away or answered a round for them (`away`, a `respond` 'timeout'); until then its own row reads "waiting for records" (any later action, such as the other player's own bundle, releases it). The session's transport (session/audit.ts `gatedTransport`) also replaces the bundle of an own `auditPublish` proposal by a stub every reducer refuses when the confirmed history does not accept it or the ledger holds no finished game with exactly its span, and the rollback of such a stub is no refusal and no log line — reason: a history sequenced alone may be abandoned for one in which the game is still being played (DESIGN "Resume and failure handling": later arrivals reconcile to the copy with the highest seq), and net/engine.ts requeueLost proposes the abandoned own actions again unchecked; the bundle would show the live library order (audit round 1 fork scenario, now in tests/sim/audit/leak.test.ts). A re-check inside requeueLost would be the cleaner place; net/engine.ts is M1-P1's.
- [M5-P2] (audit round 1) The stage view reaches the audit through `sessionAudit(session)` (session/audit.ts, a WeakMap the networked session fills) instead of a `TableSession.audit` member; the panel is mounted with one line in ui/flow/Stage.svelte (M2-P7's), the only view shown in every stage after a game — reason: contract files are M5-C's (a contracts package may add `readonly audit?: AuditControls` to `TableSession` and drop the lookup).
- [M5-P2] (audit round 1) The ledger counts a bundle only for the game whose span it names exactly; after a rewind to another history or a catch-up, the session replays its timeline from the room's seq-0 table or the latest saved snapshot (also on reload) and keeps the replay when it runs without a gap to the confirmed state, so a game in progress survives an adopted history; otherwise games that may have been replaced are dropped as before — reason: audit round 1 (an honest player's bundle was lost after a fork).
- [M5-P2] (audit round 1) A "could not be checked" verdict of an incomplete bundle names the public shuffles and reorders without a record (by action) and how many draws were checked — reason: a selective gap (one stacked shuffle's record left out) shows to the table; missing records stay neutral (owner decision).
- [M5-P2] (audit round 2) A browser proposes its bundle once every other player of the game who still holds a chair acknowledged the game's end in its history: that player's heartbeat names the sequencer this browser follows, at a seq at or after the end and not ahead of this browser (the sequencer's own: capped at this browser's seq), received while this browser is in step and, as a follower, has applied an action from that sequencer since it last changed; or that player's own bundle of the game is sequenced. Acknowledgements are dropped on a rewind or a snapshot catch-up. This replaces round 1's "acted after the last away or timeout mark", which deadlocked when two players let "Next game?" pass on the pass timer and let a bundle out while a player cut off after his "ok" still played the game in a history that won — reason: audit round 2; the session reads heartbeats through its transport wrapper (session/audit.ts `gatedTransport` observe) because `PeerInfo` (net/types.ts, a contract) does not say which sequencer a peer follows.
- [M5-P2] (audit round 2) A player who left before the game's end (never acknowledged it) still holds the others' bundles until they are back or their chair is free; the own row says so ("Published once Ben (away) has the game's end"). A player who leaves after their browser reported the end no longer holds anything — reason: a history in which the game goes on can win only through a player who has not seen the end; releasing on away alone reopens the round-2 leak (gate: design tension for the owner, a bounded hold would trade the audit for the secrecy of that case).
- [M5-P2] (audit round 2) The own seat never gets a "records missing" verdict while this browser can still publish its bundle (played the game, not published, fewer than three refusals); a 'verified' verdict with no shuffle and no draw checked reads "verified: nothing to check (…)"; the panel's sentence says every player published only when every row is verified or a mismatch — reason: audit round 2 minor findings.
- [M5-P2] (audit round 3) The own bundle waits for an acknowledgement of the game's end from every seat that holds a chair now (name not empty), not only the game's players: a peer who sat down mid-match without a deck sequences like any other when cut off and may hold a history in which the game goes on; the verdicts still cover only the game's players, and the row's note names the chair's current holder — reason: audit round 3 (late-sitter fork scenario, now in tests/sim/audit/leak.test.ts).
- [M5-P2] (audit round 3) The log line of a seat without a bundle, written when this browser's wait ran out, reads "could not be checked: records missing (none published so far; records published later are still checked)"; a bundle published later gets its own line — reason: the wait runs on each browser's clock, so peers may differ in this line (display only, never in a verdict).
- [M5-P2] (audit round 3) No explicit "End the evening" signal: a game is audited when it ends by "Next game" or "New draft" (a stageSet out of the match); a table closed mid-match is a game that can be continued days later (DESIGN "Resume and failure handling"), so publishing then would expose live library orders — reason: gate question for the owner (an "End the evening" proposal or a hint in the evening bar, both outside M5-P2's files).
- [M5-P4] Focus order details: a battlefield reads as the viewer sees it (rows far to near within half a card's height, each left to right), piles and the stack start with the top card, the own hand follows the fan order (`moveFocus` gained an optional `opts.handOrder`); a library and an empty zone take focus as a zone without a card; moving to another zone lands on its first card; focus on a card that leaves passes to the card that took its place — reason: DESIGN says "cycling focus through cards in a zone" but not the order; reading order matches what the viewer sees, and libraries hide their cards.
- [M5-P4] The keyboard focus takes precedence over a resting pointer for the keymap; a pointer that moves more than 4 px onto another card clears it, and Escape (with nothing else to close) leaves it — reason: the last input wins, so neither a mouse at rest nor a key press steals the other's target.
- [M5-P4] The accessible mirror is one focusable element (tabindex 0; Tab itself is the "next phase" key, so it is reached with the arrow keys or a screen reader's own focus navigation) with role "application" (aria-roledescription "card table") described by the focused card and listing the focused zone's cards; arrow keys give it DOM focus; sighted keyboard users see a chip above the hand naming the focus; the scene draws the ring as a double SVG stroke (theme focus colour on a halo of the theme background) 2.5 mm outside the card's projected corners — reason: the table is a canvas; a screen reader in browse mode passes keys through only to an application or widget; an overlay ring works in every theme without a new shader outline and stays apart from hover and selection outlines.
- [M5-P4] The log's live region is one page-wide pair of visually hidden regions (polite, assertive) fed with new log lines only (not the log list, which is no longer aria-live: filters and tabs re-rendered lines would be re-read); polite lines within 400 ms are one announcement, more than three become "N new log lines. Latest: …"; the viewer's own chat is not echoed; focus changes are spoken at once; a confirmation round to answer and the untap offer are assertive — reason: PLAN M5-P4 "polite, not flooding"; DECISIONS [M5-C] reserves assertive for prompts addressed to this player.
- [M5-P4] Seat glyphs (ui/a11y/SeatGlyph.svelte, the same shapes as avatar and lobby) stand beside every seat-coloured name or mark in the HUD: log lines, turn strip, confirm prompt, attribution flashes, team hands, draft seats; a unit test requires a glyph in every HUD component that colours by seat — reason: DESIGN "nothing depends on hue alone".
- [M5-P4] Paper: six seat colours darkened slightly (each still ≥ 4.5:1 on white) so seat-coloured names on the grey around the table (#f1f1ee) reach 4.5:1, and the ghost outline's opacity raised from 0.25 to 0.5 (1.7:1 → 3.5:1, WCAG 1.4.11) — reason: "Paper meets WCAG AA contrast throughout".
- [M5-P4] Reduced motion: a page-wide `prefers-reduced-motion` rule (ui/a11y/motion.ts, installed by App and Hud) cuts every CSS animation and transition to one 1 ms run; components whose signal is an endless animation must give a still alternative (unit-checked); the HUD follows the setting live instead of reading it once — reason: PLAN M5-P4 "every animation cuts or fades"; 1 ms rather than 0 keeps animationend/transitionend firing.
- [M5-P4] axe runs with its default rule set (WCAG 2.0–2.2 A/AA rules and best practices axe enables by default; `target-size`, off by default in axe, is not run) via @axe-core/playwright 4.14.0 (dev dependency) on the start page, the lobby (host and guest), the table HUD (with and without the cheat sheet) and the builder list, in Chromium and WebKit; serious and critical findings fail — reason: PLAN M5-P4 acceptance; touch target sizes are M5-P1's (44 px on touch). The builder list rows' accessible name now contains their visible text (WCAG 2.5.3).
- [M5-P4] With a keyboard focus the keymap acts on the focused card only; a focus on a zone without a focusable card (a library, an empty zone) gives the keymap no card, so library keys fall back to the own library and a resting pointer never acts behind the focus — reason: a screen-reader user told the focus is on the library must not tap or move a card they cannot perceive (review round 1).
- [M5-P4] The table focus ring is dashed (7/4 px, the theme focus colour on a solid halo of the theme background) and stands off the card's projected outline by max(2.5 mm, 7 px), offset edge by edge so the gap is even in the ego view's trapezoid; hand-fan and pile-viewer cards with keyboard focus get a dashed `--sp-focus` outline 4 px outside the card, beside the hover lift — reason: Paper, Wyrm and Stonetable draw selection in the focus colour, so the ring must differ in form, and in the far view (about 1 px/mm) a 2.5 mm gap vanished under the halo; ViewState.focused "distinct from hover and selection".
- [M5-P4] Focus changes are spoken through the live region only: the mirror's aria-describedby is the fixed key hint, not the focused card — reason: a changed description of the focused element is read too, which spoke every move twice (PLAN "polite, not flooding").
- [M5-P4] The focus order (focusZones, focusCards, FocusOptions) lives in the leaf module ui/a11y/focus.ts, re-exported by ui/a11y/index.ts; index.ts no longer re-exports ./announcer (imported by its own path) — reason: removes the runtime import cycles index ↔ describe and index ↔ announcer. For the integrator: the M5-C shared shape `moveFocus` gained an optional sixth parameter `opts: FocusOptions` (additive), and M5-P4 touched files outside its listed ownership: ui/builder/list/ListView.svelte (visually hidden action prefix for WCAG 2.5.3, needed by the builder-list axe test), ui/App.svelte (installs the reduced-motion CSS), ui/draft/Draft.svelte (seat glyph), ui/HandFan.svelte and ui/PileViewer.svelte (focus outline).
- [M5-P4] The pile viewer's looked-at cards of the own library (Search, Look at top N) take keyboard focus in the viewer's order (`FocusOptions.looked`), named from the looked identities in this browser only; opening the viewer on a focused library moves focus onto its first shown card, closing it leaves the library a zone without a card — reason: DESIGN "every card action is reachable from the keyboard" includes tutoring and scrying (review round 2).
- [M5-P4] The context-menu key and Shift+F10 open the menu of the keyboard-focused card (the table menu on a zone without one; without a focus, the hovered card's) beside the card; the menu follows the WAI-ARIA menu pattern (first entry focused when opened from the keyboard, Up/Down wrap, Home/End, Enter/Space run, Escape and Tab close, focus returns to the mirror); ContextMenu.svelte (outside ui/a11y) changed for it — reason: keyless menu entries (Add trigger, named counters) must be reachable from the keyboard (review round 2).
- [M5-P4] With a keyboard focus the keys' selection counts only when it holds the focused card, and no stack marker under a resting pointer counts (`CommandHost.view().keyFocus`); the scene gets no hover while a keyboard focus is set; a focus that passes to another card because its card left is spoken; a log line that changed in place is spoken again only when it is the newest — reason: review round 2 (one target, nothing acted on unperceived, polite not flooding).
- [M5-P4] Review round 3: the stack chip no longer dims Counter and a not-ready Resolve with opacity (#111 at 55 % was 4.17:1, its key label 2.5:1); a ready Resolve is marked by a heavier foreground border and weight instead; while a keyboard focus is set the scene draws no hover outline on its own pointer-hovered card either (scene debug `cards()[].hovered`); a keyboard-opened menu anchors to the focus ring, hand card or pile card before any other element naming the card (log lines come first in the DOM); arrows and Page keys do not move the card focus while the cheat sheet is open or when pressed in the log lines; a focus passed on because its card left is spoken after the log lines waiting in the batch (`AnnounceQueue.focusAfterLog`, after the update settles); the table ring test bounds the ring's width by the card's plus 48 px rather than 1.5 times it (a card about 34 px wide in the overview gets a ring about 51 px wide from the 7 px stand-off and halo) — reason: "Paper meets WCAG AA contrast throughout" and axe on the table HUD with a stack item; one target on screen; the passed-on focus follows the line saying what happened to the old card.
- [M5-G] M5-P2, M5-P4 and M5-P6 are merged into m5 as parked (their last round-3 commits unreviewed); their open gaps are accepted for the owner playtest rather than reworked: the audit holds a bundle while any seated chair holder has not acknowledged the game end (no bounded hold), a table closed mid-match is not audited, and own solo actions already inside a stored snapshot can still be lost when a stretch outlives the 600-action snapshot hold — reason: the gate needs the audit, axe, soak and version check, `pnpm check` and their suites are green on the merge, and each gap is a judgement call already recorded for the owner.
- [M5-G] Lines from and to a collapsed stack: a right-drag from an item of the far-zoom badge's expanded list starts a line from that stack item, and a line (from anywhere) ends at the list item under the pointer; the list expands while a line's end is over the badge and a collapsed lane's items anchor their lines at the lane centre (the badge); the M3 and M4 gate specs reach stack items through the list when the lane is collapsed — reason: M3/M4 gate regression (no target lines from spells on the stack in the fitted 4-seat and default ego views); DESIGN "lines from and to stack items" must work without zooming in.
- [M5-G] The badge touches its button text only when the count changes and closes on any outside press (pointer, touch or mouse), clearing a hover a tap left — reason: Linux WebKit CI (stack-badge.spec :60, :134); replacing the text node under a pressed pointer every frame lost the click, and WebKit reports a tap's compatibility hover without a leave.
- [M5-G] The default ego view honours the HUD's fit insets: when the viewer's territory, pile column or command area would project under the toolbar, avatars column, log or hand fan, the seat is pulled back along its default view ray and shifted sideways (smallest pull that fits, found by bisection; pitch 35° and field of view 45° kept) — reason: owner decision "ego framing shows your own piles", which the gate checks against the HUD (the package test only checked the viewport bounds). This extends M5-P7's seat (900/860) per viewport.
- [M5-G] Offline notice: while `navigator.onLine` is false every page shows a pointer-transparent notice ("Offline: tables, decks and saved card images still work here; rooms, new card data and new images wait for the connection."), top centre below the turn strip; the update notice moved below it (104 px) so it no longer covers the phases — reason: gate M5-G screenshot "offline banner"; PLAN M5-P3 graceful behaviour offline.
- [M5-G] A test-only `__spellplane.audit.tamper()` (dev and test builds only, through the debug hook) makes the next own bundle report its first shuffle's order with the top two cards swapped; the gate reads verdicts by their `status` field (the contract's name) — reason: the gate's tampered-game step; the spec read a non-existent `verdict` field.
- [M5-G] First-run hints: the welcome ("import a deck to try it alone") is not shown at a networked table (one with live voice); on a touch screen the talk hint names the thumb bar's Talk button; table hints sit top centre below the turn strip; on the start page the welcome waits while the Home Screen or storage hint shows, and the storage prompt waits for the Home Screen hint — reason: M5 gate findings (hints over the hand fan, three at once, desktop wording on the iPad).
- [M5-G] Touch wording: on a touch device the draft prompt names taps, the builder help names fingers, and the toolbar and turn strip hide key labels; the Draw/Mill-how-many field has a Draw button — reason: M5 gate findings; DESIGN "every action has three routes" (no return key on a tablet without a keyboard).
- [M5-G] A finger lifting from the table clears its presence cursor on the other pages (a mouse cursor still rests) — reason: M5 gate finding (Ben's arrow stayed where his finger last lifted).
- [M5-G] Below 900 px width (an upright tablet) the log starts hidden and turning the device across that width hides or reopens it; on touch screens below 1000 px the log ends above the thumb bar; the in-match evening bar stays within the 190 px avatars column — reason: M5 gate findings (iPad portrait HUD overlaps, evening bar over the hand fan).
- [M5-G] The lobby's waiting and connectivity messages carry the shared empty-state titles ("No other players connected", "Relays unreachable") from ui/empty/states.ts — reason: M5 gate finding (the catalogue entries were used only in the settings harness).
- [M5-G] The audit panel docks at the bottom right while the table is in its lobby (its settings column fills the top right), and a hidden log is a small bar rather than a full-height box; on screens below 1000 px the turn strip packs tighter — reason: M5 gate (the panel covered the packs field on the iPad; the hidden log still covered the table in portrait).
- [M5-G] A press outside the far-zoom badge also clears the keyboard focus a tap gave its button (Linux WebKit focuses a tapped button; a tap on the canvas moves no focus) — reason: CI on Linux WebKit (stack-badge.spec, a tap elsewhere did not collapse the badge).
- [M5-review] A finger that lifts after a drag repeats the drag end once more on its own 1 s timer (scene/presence.ts DRAG_END_ECHO_MS); a mouse keeps repeating it with the resting-cursor resend — reason: a lifted finger clears its cursor and resend timer, so a lost drag end (unreliable channel) left a ghost card for 30 s.
- [M5-review] HUD inset and viewport changes reframe the ego seat only while the ego view is still the default framing (TableRig.egoAtDefault; it stays the default); a navigated ego view keeps its pose and the change applies once it is out of sight (bird's-eye) or on a new table, seat or theme. Insets and the reframe are held back during a rig switch and apply when it ends — reason: opening the log jumped a navigated ego camera ~75 mm, and a change mid-switch broke the cursor-point guarantee (up to 295 px); same rule as the bird's-eye rig, which re-fits only a fitted view.
- [M5-review] A finger on an item of the far-zoom badge's expanded list feeds the scene's touch gestures (StackBadgeOptions.onTouch): long-press then drag draws a line from that stack item as from a card, a plain drag scrolls the list (its items take no browser panning, touch-action none, so Safari does not cancel the finger), a tap or long-press alone does nothing on the table under the badge. A line that ends with the mouse resting on the badge leaves the list open as a hover (trackLine with `end`) — reason: on iPad no line could start from a badge item (right press only), and the list collapsed under the pointer after a line.
